\section{Purpose and~reading guide}
\index{documentation}
\index{manual!scope}

\product{} is~a~self-hosted photo gallery. The~photographs remain on~storage you
control, while the~application adds the~catalog, navigation, access rules,
thumbnails, maps, search, downloads, and~administrative tools around them.
A~single installation can~be~used for~a~public portfolio, a~family archive,
client delivery, event photographs, travel collections, or~a~large personal
library.

The~manual is~organized around work that an~owner actually performs. The~early
sections cover installation and~the~first gallery; the~middle of~the~manual
deals with publishing, media, privacy, presentation, and~maintenance.
Architecture and~developer material is~kept near the~end so~it~does
not~interrupt ordinary administration.

For~a~new installation, begin with Section~\ref{sec:first-hour}. Collection
planning is~covered in~Section~\ref{sec:planning}; routine gallery and~photo
editing is~in~Sections~\ref{sec:create-edit-gallery}
and~\ref{sec:photo-workflow}. Section~\ref{sec:audiences} is~useful before
publishing because it~compares several common audience types. The~contents
and~index are~intended for~jumping directly to~a~setting or~workflow when
the~site is~already in~use.





\subsection{How to~use this manual}
\index{manual!how to use}

Admin names such as~\ui{Theme} and~\ui{System Health} are~shown in~bold.
Monospaced text denotes filenames, setting names, values, or~commands.
Procedures are~written as~ordered lists where sequence matters; checklists
and~reference material use bullets or~tables. Technical implementation notes
are~included only where they explain a~visible behavior, a~recovery decision,
or~a~maintenance requirement.

\subsection{Further project references}

Developer and~hosting references remain separate from this owner manual.
The~References section lists the~repository documents used for~architecture,
database, source ownership, testing, and~contribution rules
\cite{readme,architecture,database,codemap,testing,agents}.\footnote{Those files
are~the~better source when changing code, inspecting schema details,
or~preparing a~release.}

The~\href{https://phpgallery.site/}{project website} offers
the~published download and~documentation in~English, Czech, German,
and~Swedish. Its~light/dark switch changes the~presentation page;
the~enlargeable application screenshots retain their captured language,
appearance, and~version. Screenshot source links lead to~the~public gallery
or~the~corresponding documentation. These controls do~not~change your
installed gallery's theme.

\subsection{Terminology}

\begin{description}
  \item[Gallery] A~named collection of~photographs. A~gallery can~contain
  photographs and~smaller galleries.
  \item[Photograph or~image] One item in~a~gallery, together with its title,
  description, tags, visibility, and~available camera information.
  \item[Thumbnail] A~smaller copy created for~quick display in~gallery grids,
  search results, covers, and~previews.
  \item[Visitor] A~person viewing the~public side of~the~site.
  \item[Administrator] A~trusted person who signs in~to~create and~manage
  content and~settings.
  \item[Selected gallery] The~gallery currently open on~the~screen.
  \item[Gallery branch] A~gallery together with every smaller gallery contained
  beneath it.
\end{description}

\section{Product overview}
\label{sec:overview}
\index{product overview}
\index{shared hosting}

\product{} turns a~folder collection into a~browsable photographic website.
Visitors see covers, titles, descriptions, photo grids, full-screen viewing,
maps, tags, search, and~downloads according to~the~choices made by~the~owner.
Administrators receive private tools for~adding photographs, arranging
collections, controlling privacy, improving descriptions, and~keeping
the~installation healthy.

Ordinary PHP/MySQL web hosting is~the~primary deployment target. The~owner keeps
the~source photographs in~recognizable folders, while the~database supplies
the~catalog and~application state needed for~browsing.\footnote{The~project
overview provides the~short installation and~feature summary \cite{readme}.}

\subsection{Core capabilities}

\begin{itemize}
  \item Create galleries inside other galleries, for~example \emph{2026 / Italy
  / Rome} or~\emph{Clients / Northwind / Final selection}.
  \item Give every gallery a~title, description, date range, cover, public
  address, privacy choice, and~individual appearance.
  \item Upload photographs through the~browser or~discover files copied into
  gallery folders.
  \item Add titles, stories, tags, visibility, ordering, dates, and~location
  information to~photographs.
  \item Let visitors search, browse tags, view photographs full screen, follow
  maps, vote, and~download permitted collections.
  \item Protect family, client, or~sensitive collections with access controls
  and~share methods.
  \item Find likely duplicate photographs and~review them with direct links
  to~both locations.
  \item Keep the~installation current through guided updates, health checks,
  backups, and~maintenance tools.
  \item Automate scoped uploads, exchange galleries with compatible
  installations, and~prepare selected-photo or~gallery downloads.
  \item Generate optional AI metadata and~translation drafts, while keeping
  every generated value reviewable before publication.
  \item Publish crawler-friendly robots and~image-sitemap output for~the~public
  content that visitors can~actually access.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Filesystem and~database partnership}
\index{filesystem-first design}
\index{database}

PHP Gallery stores persistent data in~both the~filesystem and~the~database.
Gallery directories hold the~source photographs in~a~recognizable tree.
The~database stores the~information that does not~belong in~the~file itself:
titles, descriptions, tags, ordering, access settings, votes, dates,
generated-media records, and~other application state.

Neither side is~a~complete backup on~its own. Restoring only the~database leaves
catalog entries without their source files; restoring only the~gallery tree
loses the~administrative and~presentation state associated with those files.
Backup and~recovery should therefore treat the~database, gallery directories,
and~local configuration as~one set. Section~\ref{sec:user-data} returns to~this
in~more detail.

\section{Requirements and~environment}
\label{sec:requirements}
\index{requirements}
\index{PHP}
\index{MySQL}
\index{MariaDB}

Use this section when choosing a~hosting plan or~checking an~existing account.
The~requirements table is~also suitable for~sending directly to~a~hosting
provider.

\subsection{Minimum runtime}

\begin{longtable}{>{\bfseries}p{0.27\linewidth}p{0.65\linewidth}}
\toprule
Component & Requirement\\
\midrule
\endhead
PHP & Version 8.1 or~newer.\\
Database & MySQL 8.4 LTS, MariaDB 10.11 LTS, or~MariaDB 11.4 LTS.\\
PDO & PDO MySQL extension for~application queries and~migrations.\\
Image processing & GD for~common thumbnail generation. Additional local image
tooling can~support specialized formats.\\
Archives & ZipArchive for~package, download, and~transfer workflows that produce
or~consume ZIP data.\\
Filesystem & Writable application data, gallery, cache, and~generated-media
locations.\\
Web server & Apache-style rewriting is~recommended for~clean URLs; query-string
routing supports compatible environments without rewrites.\\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}

The~maintained support matrix names MySQL~8.4 LTS, MariaDB~10.11 LTS,
and~MariaDB~11.4 LTS as~representatives directly exercised by~required CI.
The~canonical support document lists MySQL~8.0 and~MariaDB~10.4--10.10 as~legacy
best-effort series without a~required project database workflow or~project
regression and~security assurance; they~are~outside the~maintained qualification
set. MySQL~5.7 or~older and~MariaDB~10.3 or~older are~unsupported. Other
database-server series remain unqualified until~required CI tests them directly;
a~higher version alone does~not~establish compatibility. See
\filepath{docs/DATABASE_SUPPORT.md} for~the~full policy.

Outbound HTTPS access supports application updates and~remote release metadata.
Local operation and~manually deployed releases can~function with a~more
restricted outbound policy.\footnote{Network policy should follow
the~installation owner’s update and~privacy requirements. Update code validates
packages and~preserves recovery data according to~the~local updater
implementation.}

\subsection{Permissions}
\index{permissions!filesystem}

The~web process needs read access to~runtime code and~write access to~designated
mutable locations. Keep runtime code read-only where hosting permits it, except
during a~controlled updater operation. Gallery source files deserve backup
coverage equal to~the~database because the~complete application state spans both
domains.

\important{Create backups of~the~database, gallery files, and~installation
configuration together. A~database-only backup preserves metadata while omitting
the~authoritative media tree.}

\section{Installation and~initial setup}
\label{sec:installation}
\index{installation}
\index{setup}

Internal application, tooling, documentation, cache, data, and~log directories
carry their own Apache authorization guards, independent of~URL rewriting.
The~host must honor those directives or~provide equivalent server rules; PHP's
development server does~not~read them. Direct HTTP requests to~CLI commands
receive an~empty 404 before application startup. Continue to~use the~public
front controller for~browser routes and~the~shell for~maintenance commands. See
\filepath{docs/HTTP_ENTRYPOINTS.md}.

Build requested production folders or~ZIPs with
\filepath{scripts/package_production.php}. The~PHP 8.1+ builder uses
\filepath{app/production-files.json}, requires current integrity data,
and~verifies exact membership and~complete SHA-256 content before publication.
Existing outputs, unsafe paths and~filesystem links are~refused. Tests, private
media, installed configuration and~runtime state cannot enter the~package.
WinApp sources retain their separate companion inventory. Historical interactive
and~FTP deployment scripts have been removed. See
\filepath{docs/PRODUCTION_FILES.md}.

Companion font/license resources use the~optional
\codeword{companion_resource_files} inventory field. Installed readers
can~ignore
this addition while the~current package policy validates its~exact allowed
paths.
The~original companion field stays readable by~older companion-aware updaters;
neither field grants CMS replacement ownership. Direct updates use a~complete
newer package and~all pending migrations, without requiring each intermediate
release. This does~not~qualify arbitrary historical hosting or~database states.
Cancel a~failed pre-activation job before selecting a~corrected newer package;
retrying the~same incompatible build cannot repair its~inventory.

\subsection{Browser installation}

\subsubsection{Installation sequence}

The~browser installer gathers database settings, initializes the~schema through
migrations, creates the~first administrator, prepares writable locations,
and~writes local configuration. A~fresh request redirects to~installation when
required configuration is~absent.\footnote{Installation state and~generated
configuration should receive the~same protection as~credentials. Source archives
should continue excluding the~local \filepath{config.php}.}

Migrations finish before the~first administrator, \filepath{config.php},
and~the~installation lock are~created. Repairs use the~verified installer
connection and~the~shared gallery-writer lock; before configuration exists,
gallery storage is~the~installation's \filepath{galleries/} directory.
If~a~migration fails, correct its cause and~submit the~administrator step again.
Completed migrations remain recorded and~the~failed callback is~retried.
Preserve the~migration ledger and~leave completed installations' configuration
and~locks in~place.

Typical steps are:

\begin{enumerate}
  \item Create an~empty MySQL or~MariaDB database using the~hosting control
  panel.
  \item Upload the~application package to~the~intended web directory.
  \item Open the~site and~follow the~installer.
  \item Enter database connection values and~the~initial administrator
  credentials.
  \item Confirm writable directories and~extension checks.
  \item Complete installation and~enter the~Admin area.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Command-line setup}

\subsubsection{Local commands}

The~repository provides plain PHP scripts for~migration and~administrator
creation:

\begin{lstlisting}[language=bash]
php scripts/migrate.php
php scripts/create_admin.php <username> <password>
\end{lstlisting}

Command-line setup follows the~same persistent schema model as~browser setup.
Review \filepath{config.example.php}, create the~local configuration, provision
the~database, run migrations, and~create the~first administrator.

\subsection{First administrative review}

After installation, review:

\begin{itemize}
  \item Site name, language, theme, page width, and~public rendering
  preferences.
  \item Gallery root permissions and~discovery results.
  \item Thumbnail formats, dimensions, quality, and~maintenance state.
  \item Access, password-reset, email, telemetry, update-channel, and~backup
  preferences.
  \item Rewrite compatibility and~canonical public URLs.
  \item System Health diagnostics and~pending migrations.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Clean URLs and~rewrite compatibility}
\index{URL rewriting}
\index{query-string routing}
\index{clean URLs}

PHP Gallery supports clean public paths when the~hosting environment applies
the~project's rewrite rules. Clean URLs are~a~presentation and~routing
preference, not~a~requirement for~the~gallery data model. Query-string routes
remain the~compatibility path and~continue to~work when rewriting is~disabled
or~cannot be~verified reliably.

The~URL rewriting setting controls which form PHP Gallery emits in~generated
links. When rewriting is~off, helpers use query-style addresses for~galleries,
photographs, thumbnails, and~other application routes instead of~assuming that
the~web server will translate a~clean path. Slugs, gallery folders, database
rows, and~source photographs do~not~need to~be~renamed when this setting
changes.

If~clean paths fail on~a~new host, first disable URL rewriting in~Admin
and~confirm that the~query-string form, such as~\codeword{?page=gallery}, works.
On~Apache or~LiteSpeed, then verify that the~project's \filepath{.htaccess}
is~honored and~that rewrite support is~available. On~other web servers, keep
the~compatibility mode unless equivalent routing rules have~been configured.

\section{Your first hour with PHP Gallery}
\label{sec:first-hour}
\index{first hour}
\index{getting started}
\index{gallery owner}
\index{Admin dashboard}

For~the~first session, keep the~scope small: create one gallery, add a~few
photographs, and~open the~result from a~browser that is~not~signed in~as~Admin.
Theme work and~deeper organization can~wait until that basic path is~known
to~work.

\subsection{Open the~Admin area}
\index{Admin login}

Choose \ui{Admin login} from the~public header and~enter the~account created
during installation. The~dashboard is~the~starting point for~everyday ownership.
It~summarizes galleries, photos, system state, and~available maintenance
actions.\footnote{The~exact dashboard contents depend on~enabled features,
completed migrations, and~development settings. A~healthy installation can~still
display informational notices that invite an~administrator to~complete optional
work.}

After authentication, a~return link can~open a~local page of~this installation.
External URLs and~return paths containing backslashes are~refused;
the~application instead uses the~safe local fallback of~the~login workflow.

Spend a~few minutes identifying these areas:

\begin{itemize}
  \item \ui{Galleries}, where collections are~created, discovered, edited,
  and~arranged;
  \item \ui{Theme}, where the~public site's appearance and~presentation defaults
  are~selected;
  \item \ui{Tags}, where reusable subjects and~categories are~maintained;
  \item \ui{System Health}, where migrations, thumbnails, storage,
  and~consistency are~reviewed;
  \item \ui{Account}, where credentials, recovery email, and~mail delivery
  are~managed.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Temporary administrator support access}
\index{Admin!temporary support access}

A~permanent administrator can~open \ui{Account}, choose \ui{Temporary admin
access}, and~generate a~single-use support link. The~link is~shown only
at~creation and~expires exactly 24~hours after issuance, regardless of~when
the~recipient first accepts it. Share it~privately with a~trusted support
person. The~recipient should use a~separate browser profile and~explicitly
accept the~invitation; no~new permanent administrator account is~created.

The~Admin header displays a~temporary-access notice and~UTC expiry.
The~recipient can~inspect diagnostics and~work on~permitted gallery/media tasks,
but cannot~change administrator credentials, global security settings,
persistent tokens or~updater configuration. The~owner can~revoke the~grant
at~any time; the~next authorization check refuses every associated session.
Expiration and~revocation require no~scheduled cleanup.

\securitynote{The~unused invitation link is~a~bearer credential. Never publish
it~in~logs, tickets or~screenshots. Existing permanent Admin sessions remain
unaffected by~revocation. Apply pending database migrations before issuing
support invitations.}

\subsection{Create a~first gallery}
\index{gallery!creating the first gallery}

Open gallery administration and~create a~gallery with a~short title such
as~\emph{Summer Walk}. Review the~proposed public path and~folder name, then add
one sentence describing what the~collection contains.

For~this first check, make the~gallery public and~leave advanced branding
or~inherited overrides at~their defaults. Add three to~ten photographs. That
is~enough to~verify ordering, thumbnail generation, titles, and~the~lightbox
without turning the~exercise into a~large import.

\subsection{Preview as~a~visitor}
\index{anonymous preview}
\index{visitor preview}

Use the~public-gallery link or~visitor-preview control. For~a~realistic check,
open the~public address in~a~private browser window where the~Admin session
is~absent. Confirm that the~title, description, photographs, and~navigation
appear as~intended. Open a~photograph, move forward and~backward in~the~viewer,
and~return to~the~gallery.

Administrator views can~contain edit controls and~additional assets. Anonymous
preview gives the~clearest picture of~the~experience received by~family,
clients, or~the~public.\footnote{Protected galleries and~age-restricted content
deserve a~separate anonymous test because an~active administrator session
already has~broader access.}

\subsection{Complete the~first-hour checklist}

\begin{enumerate}
  \item Set the~public site name and~language.
  \item Create one gallery with a~title and~description.
  \item Upload a~small group of~photographs.
  \item Add a~meaningful title to~at~least one photograph.
  \item Reorder two photographs and~confirm the~new order publicly.
  \item Open the~gallery as~an~anonymous visitor.
  \item Check the~gallery on~a~phone-sized screen.
  \item Confirm that System Health reports no~urgent migration or~permission
  problem.
  \item Create a~coordinated backup after the~initial setup is~complete.
\end{enumerate}

\section{Planning a~gallery collection}
\label{sec:planning}
\index{planning galleries}
\index{collection design}
\index{gallery hierarchy!planning}

A~thoughtful structure helps visitors understand a~collection and~helps
the~owner maintain it~over time. Start with the~way people will look
for~photographs. Folder names and~database details can~follow that human
organization.

\subsection{Choose a~structure people recognize}

Common structures include:

\begin{description}
  \item[By~year and~event] A~top-level year contains weddings, trips,
  celebrations, and~projects. This suits family and~historical archives.
  \item[By~place] Countries contain regions and~cities. This suits travel,
  aviation, landscape, and~documentary photography.
  \item[By~client or~project] Each client contains assignments and~delivery
  galleries. This suits professional work with separate access rules.
  \item[By~subject] Wildlife, architecture, portraits, aircraft, and~similar
  subjects form enduring categories. Tags can~provide a~second way to~browse
  across them.
  \item[By~workflow] Incoming, selected, delivered, and~archive galleries
  represent stages. Visibility settings keep working collections private while
  finished selections become public.
\end{description}

Use galleries for~strong navigational boundaries and~tags for~relationships that
cross those boundaries. A~photograph from Prague can~live in~\emph{Travel /
Czech Republic / Prague} and~carry tags such as~\emph{architecture},
\emph{night}, and~\emph{tram}.\footnote{A~photograph has~one gallery location
in~the~normal content model, while reusable tags provide several semantic
connections. Copy and~move tools support deliberate changes to~physical
organization.}

\subsection{Decide how deep the~hierarchy should be}
\index{gallery hierarchy!depth}

Nested galleries support deep collections, yet~most visitors browse more
comfortably when each level has~a~clear purpose. Two to~four levels serve many
sites well. A~deeper archive remains practical when breadcrumbs, meaningful
titles, and~consistent dates guide the~reader.

Create a~child gallery when it~deserves its own title, description, cover,
access policy, date range, or~public address. Keep photographs together when
a~new level would add only one extra click.

\subsection{Prepare titles and~descriptions}
\index{gallery title}
\index{gallery description}

A~gallery title should make sense outside the~Admin interface. A~description
can~answer three questions:

\begin{itemize}
  \item What does this collection show?
  \item Where and~when was~it~created?
  \item What context makes the~photographs meaningful?
\end{itemize}

Descriptions can~be~brief for~casual albums and~extensive for~documentary work.
Use the~first sentence as~the~essential summary because visitors often scan
before reading the~full text.

\subsection{Plan privacy before uploading}
\index{privacy planning}
\index{gallery visibility}

Choose whether a~collection is~public, unpublished for~administrative
preparation, or~protected for~a~known audience. Parent-gallery policy
can~influence descendants, so~place collections with similar audiences together
where practical.

Examples include:

\begin{itemize}
  \item a~public portfolio with carefully selected photographs;
  \item a~password-protected family branch;
  \item an~unpublished client proofing gallery during preparation;
  \item a~public event gallery with selected individual photographs marked
  private;
  \item an~age-gated branch for~content that needs visitor confirmation.
\end{itemize}

\section{Creating and~editing galleries}
\label{sec:create-edit-gallery}
\index{gallery!creation}
\index{gallery!editing}
\index{gallery editor}

\subsection{Create a~gallery from Admin}

Use the~\ui{+} control on~a~public gallery or~\ui{Create gallery here} in~its
editor to~start a~child gallery. The~right-side Admin panel asks only
for~the~new title; it~already knows the~parent gallery that supplied
the~control. The~new gallery starts \ui{Unpublished}. After creation, the~same
panel opens its complete editor for~description, date, language, tags, access,
display, media, and~photographs. The~public page does~not~navigate away during
this enhanced workflow.

To~start from~the~standalone Admin create page, enter a~title and~optionally
provide SimBrief details, description, source language, and~tags before
submitting. Its closed \ui{More options} section contains the~folder name,
initial visibility, date range, parent, voting, filename display,
and~contained-picture badge. A~short summary below it~shows the~current
visibility and~parent. The~dedicated create-and-upload form remains available
when photographs should be~uploaded during creation. On~a~validation error,
submitted non-secret values and~parent context stay available for~correction.

As~you type at~least two characters in~the~title field, Admin may~show
the~remaining text of~a~matching existing gallery as~a~faint inline suggestion.
Recent titles under the~selected parent are~preferred; matches elsewhere
are~considered only after the~bounded sibling search is~exhausted. Older titles
can~be~omitted, so~no~suggestion does~not~prove that a~title is~unique. The~form
requests at~most eight candidates instead of~loading the~complete title catalog.

With the~caret at~the~end and~no~text selected, press unmodified \codeword{Tab}
or~\ui{Right Arrow}, or~select the~suggested text, to~accept it. Modified
shortcuts and~input-method composition keep their ordinary behavior. The~first
\codeword{Escape} dismisses completion; a~later press can~reach the~surrounding
panel. Translated help and~a~polite announcement make suggestions discoverable
without changing the~field name.

Unicode matching accepts only safe whole-character boundaries. Some non-ASCII
suggestions are~omitted when server extensions or~browser segmentation support
are~unavailable. A~slow, failed, or~outdated lookup never prevents ordinary
typing. Acceptance changes only the~title field: it~does~not~submit the~form,
rename an~existing gallery, or~alter its access rules. The~same behavior applies
after a~side-panel form is~replaced. See \filepath{docs/TITLE_COMPLETION.md}
for~matching limits and~technical details.

After the~name-only creation, the~gallery is~already a~real collection
in~the~gallery tree, even before additional editor fields are~saved. Its
physical folder can~receive photographs through the~browser, filesystem
transfer, discovery, or~another supported upload workflow. Fill and~save
the~editor fields before publishing. The~database stores descriptive
and~presentation settings; the~normal access policy determines what visitors
can~see.

Each generated create or~classic-upload form also carries a~private operation
identity. If~a~connection fails after the~server may~have finished the~work,
retry the~same form instead of~opening a~new one: the~same administrator,
operation, and~content receive the~original completed result. A~freshly opened
form represents a~new intentional operation and~can~still create a~deliberate
duplicate. This identity is~not~a~password or~a~replacement for~sign-in and~CSRF
protection.

\subsubsection{Creation sequence}

\begin{enumerate}
  \item Open the~intended parent and~choose its \ui{+} control, or~start a~root
  gallery from~Admin.
  \item Enter a~visitor-friendly title; optionally accept a~title suggestion.
  \item Create the~Unpublished gallery. The~full editor opens in~the~same panel
  when JavaScript is~available.
  \item Add the~description, date or~date range, source language, tags,
  and~optional SimBrief data in~\ui{Identity}.
  \item Review \ui{Access}, \ui{Display}, \ui{Media management}, and~the~gallery
  folder/parent values in~Advanced where relevant.
  \item Save the~gallery, add photographs, and~choose a~cover after suitable
  photographs exist.
  \item Publish when ready, then preview the~result anonymously.
\end{enumerate}

\subsubsection{Remembered defaults and~SimBrief drafts}
\index{gallery creation!remembered defaults}
\index{SimBrief!pre-create draft}

The~editor can~remember the~current SimBrief identifier and~source language
for~the~signed-in administrator. Select the~nearby \ui{Remember for~future
galleries} choice only for~a~value you~want to~reuse. Later galleries show that
value already selected with a~small note saying it~came from~your saved
defaults. The~value remains editable; leaving Remember unchecked preserves
the~previous saved choice. Defaults belong to~one administrator and~do
not~publish credentials or~copy access rules. The~normal migration action must
apply \codeword{202609250001\_gallery\_creation\_preferences.php} before
Remember can~save a~new default; gallery creation still works without that
optional table.

The~visible SimBrief field accepts a~Pilot ID or~pilot name. Digits alone
are~treated as~a~Pilot ID, while other text is~treated as~a~name. On~a~new
or~existing gallery, \ui{Generate description draft} can~fetch the~latest OFP
and~fill an~editable Markdown description in~the~selected content language. When
gallery localization is~enabled and~its translation storage is~ready, the~draft
also fills the~maintained-language description fields. Review and~edit
the~description fields before saving. The~import keeps a~private reference
for~30~minutes, limited to~the~same administrator and~session; changing
the~identifier invalidates that reference. For~an~existing gallery, the~OFP
and~available route coordinates are~attached only after you~save the~gallery.
If~an~optional attachment fails, the~gallery remains saved and~the~panel reports
a~warning. Re-import when the~draft expires or~the~session changes.

\subsubsection{Working in~the~compact editor}
\index{gallery editor!side panel}

The~ordinary work areas are~\ui{Identity}, \ui{API}, \ui{Access}, \ui{Display},
and~\ui{Media management}; image and~specialized tools follow as~available.
The~sticky top navigation keeps the~disk \ui{Save} button visible while
the~settings form or~photo list scrolls. Changing tabs does~not~save
automatically. Use the~button after editing, then reopen the~gallery if~you~want
to~verify the~stored values. Longer explanations sit behind keyboard-accessible
question-mark controls, and~less frequent choices sit under \ui{Advanced}
disclosures. A~direct Admin editor follows the~same fields, while a~browser
without JavaScript uses the~normal form submission.

\ui{Identity} keeps title, description, date, source language, SimBrief,
and~tags readily available. Its Advanced section includes slug, folder, parent,
sort order, and~AI gallery text when enabled. \ui{API} displays a~copyable
upload endpoint, the~key label/action, and~a~newly generated key only once;
existing keys appear only when there are~any. AI metadata regeneration,
migration, and~the~global API manager live under \ui{Advanced API tools}.
\ui{Access} groups visibility, password, share-link controls, and~the~NSFW
checkbox, with longer policy explanations under question marks. \ui{Display}
uses compact translucent groups for~the~grid, photo cards,
gallery cards, navigation and~maps, visitor interactions, and~thumbnail quality.
In~\ui{Photo cards}, choose Default, 16:9, 21:9, 4:3, 1:1, 4:5, or~9:16.
Default preserves the~existing 4:3 desktop and~square mobile previews; explicit
formats apply on~every screen, only to~photos inside this gallery. Originals,
lightbox images, and~gallery cover cards remain unchanged. Save gallery commits
the~format and~other staged controls. File-name, voting, and~Picture Game
switches
save independently with JavaScript; recursive thumbnail copying and~the~coupled
grid controls remain staged. A~failed or~unverified switch save keeps drafts
and~blocks shared-form replay until the~current editor is~reviewed. Help opens
beside its question mark; breadcrumb previews remain in~a~disclosure.
Flight-route text remains beside SimBrief in~\ui{Identity}.

In~\ui{Admin > Features}, \ui{Gallery PDF documents} and~\ui{Gallery music}
are~independent switches. Both default ON for~new installations and~upgrades
without a~saved choice; an~explicit OFF remains saved. OFF hides the~matching
tables/players and~prevents uploads, delivery and~PDF conversion, including
stale forms. Files, playlists and~preferences remain stored for~re-enabling.
With both OFF, core Presentation settings remain when lightbox is~enabled.
With lightbox also OFF, the~empty tab is~omitted; old tab URLs select Identity.
Existing converted page photographs keep their normal access rules. SimBrief
can~still refresh descriptions/routes with PDF OFF and~keeps an~old original.

In~\ui{Media management > Music}, the~catalog is~on~the~left and~this
gallery's playlist on~the~right; narrow editors stack them. Play appears
before each song title and~Add at~the~row end. Select several items with
checkboxes and~use the~Add icon beside search. Existing songs remain playable
but~their Add controls are~disabled, including songs supplied by~live albums.
Albums use a~files icon and~a~distinct chevron; only expanding them loads
their indented songs. Preview does~not~assign music. Batch additions run
in~order and~report partial progress if~an~item fails. Removing a~gallery
source restores available Add actions without deleting shared originals.
The~shared header holds the~player and~gallery-wide slideshow music switch.
In~\ui{Display}, \ui{Newest uploads first} is~off by~default. When enabled,
an~upload or~changed scan orders this gallery by~registration time, newest
first. Equal timestamps use the~image ID. The~same preference is~available in
\ui{Media management > Images}; both switches control one saved setting.
Without JavaScript, use the~adjacent save button. Images also offers a~one-time sort; manual order lasts until the~next changed scan.
Registration time is~local to~the~image row; re-import can assign a~new time.
In~\ui{Media management > Images}, a~large
existing thumbnail does~not~replace the~required 300px preview. Use
the~thumbnail tools to~create gallery thumbnails, then retry Images when
that size is~missing. Disable browser-side rebuilding to~generate on~the~server.
Loading editor previews never downloads originals as~a~fallback.

A~failed music action shows a~reference matching \ui{Admin > Logs},
category \ui{Media}. It~records the~gallery, selection, action, stage
and~whether the~write completed, without raw exceptions, SQL, credentials
or~absolute storage paths. If~Admin logging fails, the~safe record goes
to~PHP's error log. Missing originals require restoring or~uploading audio;
a~dirty source requires the~music catalog task in~Maintenance Center.
Move SQL, \filepath{data/music/} and~gallery sidecars together between
machines. After a~saved-but-unrefreshed result, reopen the~editor first.

\subsubsection{Attach and~convert a~PDF document}
Expanded conversion controls keep the document column bounded. If the previous
target is missing or changed, confirm a new private result; earlier files and
galleries are preserved.

Translucent cards keep PDF file count, total size and~total pages visible,
along with the~music track count and~combined duration. Unknown metadata
remains a~dash; totals never silently omit an~unreadable original.
Cards use~the~gallery's available width: wide windows place them beside
each other, narrower windows stack them and~move them below the~description.

Smart Galleries use the~same hero cards, \ui{Presentation} button and~share
panel. PDFs and~OFPs come from accessible physical sources contributing matching
photos to~the~complete collection; hidden documents stay absent even for~an
administrator on~the~public Smart page. PDF links retain source access checks.
The~shared link preserves language and~a~selected source filter, omits page
numbers, and~starts at~matching photo one. Explicit launch defaults to~autoplay
and~music ON with medium speeds without changing ordinary Smart slideshow music.
The~same black introduction, activation confirmation and~exclusive mode apply.

Below the~cards, \ui{Presentation} starts at~the~first photo with configured
speeds and~soundtrack for~that launch. The~saved slideshow music preference
stays
unchanged. The~share icon opens an~overlay that creates a~compact gallery link
at~the~application root, e.g. \codeword{/Galerie/presentation=1a2b3c4d},
without an~album path, pagination or~preview state.
The~eight lowercase hexadecimal characters identify a~stored snapshot of~the
playback options, language and~any Smart source filter. The~same gallery and
settings reuse the~same code; another gallery receives its~own code.
Links have no~automatic expiry and~never grant gallery access.
Without clean URLs, the~short code uses the~\codeword{presentation} query
parameter.
Run the~normal database migration after upgrading; unavailable storage shows
an~error and~disables copying. Older long links remain supported.
The~database stores versioned JSON and~a~full SHA-256 fingerprint separately
from the~surrogate primary key, preserving supported snapshots when options
grow.
Generated gallery URL slugs reserve eight-character hexadecimal names and
presentation prefixes, adding \codeword{gallery-} when necessary; displayed
gallery titles remain unrestricted. Playback retains the~short root address.
The~share overlay uses compact translucent Theme colors, small switches,
thin speed sliders and~an~icon-only Copy action with visible keyboard focus.
Opening it~starts the~same presentation after ordinary access checks.
Browser autoplay restrictions may~require \ui{Play}; denied clipboard
access reveals a~selectable link for~manual copying.
Anonymous visitors can~use~the~same link after ordinary access checks.
If~native fullscreen or~sound needs activation, the~black opening waits for
\ui{Start presentation}. After confirmation, unsupported native fullscreen
retains the~browser viewport; \ui{Play} can~retry later audio failures.
The~start confirmation matches the~share panel with compact transparent
Start/Close actions, visible keyboard focus and~larger touch targets
on~narrow screens.
Use~\ui{Edit gallery > Media management > Presentation} and~its~transparent
\ui{Presentation settings} card to~choose public button visibility, music,
photo speed and~transition speed. The~Save icon uses the~ordinary guarded
gallery form. Preview and~Share use current selections, including unsaved ones.
New links store music and~both speeds; later gallery edits do~not~change
those snapshots. Visitors can~change playback controls temporarily.
\codeword{autoplay=1} also starts a~presentation. If~sound needs activation,
the~player hides with the~idle HUD; movement shows it~again.
Click \ui{Play} or~the~photo to~retry sound and~requested native fullscreen.
Stop or~Close cancels pending audio. Hiding public buttons does~not~revoke
links.
Defaults use versioned \codeword{galleries.presentation_json} after the~column
migration. Explicit writes require verified schema; older galleries retain
enabled, music and~medium defaults until configured.
Autoplay defaults ON for~every gallery until explicitly disabled in~the~card.
The~share overlay has~speed sliders and~switches for~autoplay, music,
fullscreen and~\ui{Without controls}; its~Copy icon copies the~current URL.
Share drafts do~not~save gallery settings. \codeword{autoplay=0} opens photo
one paused and~silent. Fullscreen and~exclusive choices use
\codeword{presentation_fullscreen=1|0}
and~\codeword{presentation_exclusive=1|0}.
Without controls defaults OFF; enabling it~hides controls, metadata
and~diagnostics while Esc exits. Browser sound restrictions still apply.
Each launch holds black for~at~least one second and~until photo one decodes,
then checks requested fullscreen and~music. If~the~browser needs activation,
black remains with~a~compact \ui{Start presentation} confirmation.
One click starts music and~requests fullscreen before the~selected reveal.
This confirmation also appears for~links without controls; it~then disappears.
Esc or~the~close icon cancels. Autoplay OFF stays paused and~silent.
This opening never repeats at~the~gallery loop. Closing cancels pending work.
Native fullscreen may~need a~click; the~fallback fills the~browser viewport.

PDF and~Music launchers show file counts beside the~description,
or~below it~on~narrow screens. Click a~launcher to~open a~small overlay.
Its~list has~a~maximum height of~12rem and~scrolls independently. PDF rows
retain size, page count and~small action icons; a~title or~eye opens
the~protected viewer. Administrators can~expand conversion, hide/show
and~confirm deletion. Hidden PDFs remain denied through direct links;
generated photos remain after deletion. Escape, the~close icon, launcher
or~an~outside click dismisses the~applet. Native details keeps a~bounded
inline list without JavaScript or~Popover support. Reduced motion disables
disclosure animation. In-place replacement retains the~open list and~scroll.

The~existing-gallery Upload photos chooser and~drop queue also accept PDFs.
By~default, an~upload attaches only the~original document; it~does not~create
a~subgallery or~PNG pages. To~convert during upload, open \ui{Advanced}
and~explicitly check \ui{Also convert PDF pages to~PNG}, then select a~private
child gallery or~append pages and~choose the~DPI. Opening Advanced alone does
not~enable conversion. Mixed selections upload photos first, then attach PDFs
and~perform only the~selected conversions. The~panel stays open with progress
and~cancellation controls. Retries retain the~original conversion choice;
for~an~enabled conversion, unchanged inputs automatically recreate a~missing
empty child only when no~page was acknowledged.

\index{PDF!gallery documents}

The~gallery editor combines photos and~media in~\ui{Media management}, with
\ui{Images}, \ui{Music}, \ui{PDF}, \ui{Presentation}, \ui{Artwork}
and~ZIP preferences. Its~single \ui{Upload files} picker accepts enabled
photo, PDF and~audio formats together and~routes each original automatically.
PDF replacement, deletion and~conversion remain in~\ui{PDF}; the~optional
OFP panel remains there too. Artwork and~presentation defaults retain their
gallery settings form. The~sticky top navigation includes a~disk
\ui{Save} button, available above long photo lists. Uploads and~photo
ordering still save independently. The~compact upload drawer hides support
and~instructions under a~plain question mark; Escape or~an~outside click
closes help. \ui{Advanced} contains iCloud and~processing options; PDF page
conversion remains an~unchecked opt-in. Former Images links remain supported.

An~administrator can~attach a~general PDF to~a~physical gallery even when it~has
no~SimBrief data. The~gallery keeps the~original file and~a~small index with its
stable document ID, filename, title, provenance, exact size and~SHA-256 digest.
The~protected same-origin viewer and~download link follows the~gallery's
visibility, password, share-link and~NSFW access rules. SimBrief OFPs keep their
existing manifest and~links.

Choose \ui{Convert PDF pages} to~render the~document in~the~browser. PDF.js
creates PNG pages sequentially and~the~existing upload process reports progress,
acknowledges batches, resumes interrupted work and~can~be cancelled. New
conversions use 150~DPI by~default; choose 96, 150, 200, 300, 600, 1200
or~2400~DPI. There is~no~page-count limit. A~page above the~conservative
16,384-pixel edge or~32-million-pixel advisory threshold shows a~warning but
does not block rendering or~silently downscale the~page. Its memory estimate
covers only the~canvas RGBA buffer at four bytes per pixel, not total browser
memory. Actual browser canvas allocation and~upload limits can still make
rendering or~upload fail.

Choose either a~reserved private child gallery or~append after the~source
gallery's last photograph. A~child stays private until you~change its visibility
explicitly; publication is blocked while its PDF conversion is incomplete
or~cancelled, so finish or~safely reconcile the conversion first. Appending
keeps the~source gallery's visibility and~existing photographs, then places
converted pages at~the~end. Conversion never replaces the~original PDF. Progress
markers live in~the~source gallery's hidden conversion sidecar for~both
destinations and~record the~target gallery ID. Photo ZIP exports and~gallery
migration do~not~transfer these general PDF originals. A~full filesystem backup
must include hidden sidecar folders. Existing JPG page galleries remain
separate. The~former server-side OFP conversion request now returns HTTP 409
and~directs you~to~reload; its rasterizer helper is~for~CLI and~test
compatibility only and~is~not~called by~browser actions. See
\codeword{docs/GALLERY\_PDF.md} for~the~storage and~conversion contract.

\subsubsection{Global Music Manager}

Each song has~an~independent Play/Pause action. The~compact player provides
Previous, Play/Pause, Stop, Next, Repeat and~Mute. Its~timeline shows
elapsed time and~the~original duration; click, drag or~use the~keyboard to~seek.
Seeking while paused keeps playback paused; Play resumes the~same cursor.
Stop releases the~source and~resets its~timeline. Listening never assigns files
or~changes gallery defaults. Repeat cycles All, One, Off and~back to~All.
All starts enabled and~follows the~whole saved album, including songs beyond
the~displayed page. One restarts the~current song; Off stops after the~last song.
Active modes have an~accent tint and~line; the~One icon adds~1 and~Off crosses
the~loop arrows out. Tooltips name the~current mode. Changing mode preserves
Pause and~the~cursor; Next/Previous still select another song. Each list retains
its~choice when switching players; reloading resets All. If~the~catalog changes
during a~continuation, reload
before continuing. Mute carries between player scopes on~the~same page, while
JavaScript is~required for~these controls. Native editing and~upload forms
remain available without JavaScript.

In~\ui{Albums \& playlists}, select or~create an~album, then use
\ui{Upload files} below the~existing catalog-song selector. Both ways of~adding
songs remain available. Choose up~to~twenty audio files, at~most 64~MiB each,
and~press \ui{Upload}; no~gallery is~required. Hosting limits also apply.
Identical original bytes reuse the~existing song even after renaming. A~song
can~belong to~several albums independently, and~removing its~last membership
retains its~original in~the~catalog. Reuploading an~existing member preserves
its~order. Progress and~individual results remain visible; a~failure stops
further files. Reload before retrying an~unverified response. Without
JavaScript, the~native form submits the~whole selection in~one request.
Uploading never starts playback.

Open \ui{Admin > Galleries > Music Manager} for~the~compact song catalog.
Reconcile existing playlists in~bounded batches; originals and~playlist order
remain intact. Search by~title, filename or~music tag as~you type; only the
latest search response updates the~results. The~table separates Title,
\ui{Files},
\ui{Locations}, \ui{Tags}, and~Actions. Click a~song title to~open its~existing
rename editor, add-to-album control, and~shortcuts to~tag, file, and~location
details; its~independent checkbox selects it for~merge. The~\ui{Songs} tab is~a
full-width catalog. \ui{Albums \& playlists} places the~tree and~creation form
left of~the~selected album and~its songs, stacking on~narrow screens. With no
selection, a~prompt invites you~to~choose or~create an~album. Legacy album links
open this tab. Creating an~album selects it automatically; deleting the~selected
album clears the~selection, and~a~stale album link returns to~the~prompt. Adding
from~the title popup appends a~song to~an~album; an~existing
membership stays in~its current order. Click a~song title in~the~album to~edit
its~title or~open its~tag editor, using the~same song metadata as~the~catalog.
Drag a~song by~its grip to~change album order; an~insertion line marks the~
destination. Up/down buttons remain available
for~keyboard use and~single-step moves. The~Files
disclosure lists physical variants. \ui{Locations} expands to~direct gallery
and~music-album placements: two variants in~one gallery count as~one location,
and~derived Smart inclusion adds nothing. Gallery links in~both disclosures
open the~public gallery using its~canonical URL, current language and~the~site's
configured rewrite style; a~missing gallery has~no~link. Each music tag appears
as~its own compact chip.
Header links sort Title, Files, Locations, or~Tags in~either direction across
the~complete result set before pagination, with stable ID tie-breaking. Tags
sort alphabetically by~the~song's first music tag.
The~narrow action column appears only when a~visible song has variants to~split.
The~manager shares the~site's typography and~accent colors, with compact
translucent surfaces and~softly rounded borders. Each song title sits beside
its~file count, shown with a~stacked icon and~number. Variant and~\ui{Locations}
disclosures follow the~global Admin side-panel duration; 0 makes them immediate.
Floating row disclosures close on~an~outside click, a~repeat click on~the~same
trigger, Escape, or~when another row disclosure opens. Interaction inside
an~open
disclosure leaves it~open, and~the~compact row layout is~preserved.

The~catalog-heading gear contains the~global filename-extension preference.
The~adjacent new-album gear hides parent and~description fields until needed.
Native disclosures keep collapsed song and~album rows compact; the~pending
reconciliation count appears only when nonzero. Import keeps its~text label for
an~empty catalog and~becomes icon-only after entries exist. Icon-only actions
retain accessible names and~visible keyboard focus.

Click a~song title to~open its~inline editor near the~pointer. Clicking the~same
title or~outside closes it; clicking another title switches editors. Text is~not
selected automatically. The~editor stays within the~viewport and~wraps long
titles. Enter saves the~title as~one line; textarea wrapping is~visual only and
never renames the~original file. The~global
\ui{Show file extensions in song titles} switch defaults OFF, including after
upgrade, and~affects filename-derived names only. Explicit titles keep their
text. Select songs to~preview an~explicit same-song relationship and~choose
surviving metadata. The~survivor chooser offers only selected songs, and~the
chosen survivor remains selected only while it~is~still in~the~selection.
Split a~variant to~undo a~wrong link. This never grants
access to~another source or~changes its download permission.

Create albums and~nested subalbums, then navigate the~compact tree with native
disclosure controls or~use breadcrumbs. Selecting an~album opens its~ancestor
path automatically. Its~context gear opens native details for~the~album title,
description, move and~delete controls; song membership and~ordering stay
visible. Add, remove or~reorder songs. The~tree supports eight levels and~2,000
albums. A~pending gallery count identifies snapshots awaiting
reconciliation; an~unreadable source prevents a~complete import. Removing
relationships do~not~delete centrally stored originals. Albums are~Admin
collections without a~new public
library. Music tags use the~shared taxonomy with a~separate media type;
image/gallery tags and~public image searches remain isolated. PDF tagging
is~reserved. Existing tag slugs remain globally unique. Back up~SQL,
\filepath{data/music/} and~hidden legacy indexes together; run normal forward
migrations and~bounded reconciliation after recovery. Resolve stale-edit
refusals by~reopening current
state. Disabling \ui{Gallery music} preserves this metadata and~all~audio.
Local Trash restore reconnects current tracks to~existing song identities
after gallery IDs change. Old snapshots or~removed identities create fresh
catalog entries; transferred archives never supply trusted logical IDs.

\subsubsection{Music storage}

Open~\ui{Edit gallery > Media management > Music} to~upload audio and~manage
this gallery's
playlist. New uploads store immutable originals in~the~protected central
\filepath{data/music/} repository; audio never enters image conversion.
Direct song assignments remain independent items. Album assignments are~live
playlists: later album membership and~order changes appear in~every assigned
gallery. The~gallery stores ordered references and~per-item download permission,
not~a~copy of~the~shared original. MP3, M4A, AAC,
Ogg/Opus, WAV, FLAC and~WebM are~accepted; browser codecs determine
playability. Playback and~download use the~destination gallery's current access
rules and~that item's download permission; source-gallery privacy does~not
authorize unrelated requests.

The~table shows each track's duration and~one Play/Stop icon before
its~title. The~same icon changes with playback; there is~no~Pause action
or~separate playback column. Long titles use an~ellipsis and~full tooltip;
only the~playing title scrolls horizontally. Reduced motion retains static
text. Closing the~list keeps music running and~the~existing miniplayer
visible. Compact
Previous/Play/Stop/Next icons are~available on~the~page and~in~the~photo viewer
whenever audio exists. One shared player prevents overlapping music between
rows, editor and~slideshow. The~whole playlist repeats and~navigation wraps.
Automatic slideshow music is~initially off; an~administrator can~enable
it~for~this gallery. The~switch saves its~preference automatically and~keeps
the~editor open while reporting save status. An~enabled slideshow replaces
other music and~starts at~the~first track. Stopping or~closing the~viewer
releases its music;
independently requested table playback can~continue. Photo and~speed changes
leave music running. Browser refusal permits an~explicit Play retry;
a~completely unplayable playlist stops after one pass.

Smart Galleries merge visible music from~all~accessible physical sources
contributing matching photos. Pagination and~temporary source filters do~not
narrow this union; no~Smart audio folder is~created. SHA-256 deduplicates
identical original bytes even after renaming. Equal names or~durations alone
never remove different audio. Different encodings remain distinct unless
an~administrator explicitly links them in~Music Manager. Hidden tracks
are~excluded, while visible download-locked
tracks remain playable. Existing and~new tracks default visible. Protected
source URLs recheck access and~visibility. In~the~Smart editor's presentation
overrides, enable its~independent slideshow music preference; it~starts off
and~does not~inherit source autoplay settings.

Each uploaded track initially forbids downloads. Small administrator icons
beside each track allow or~lock its download; the~header icon changes all
current tracks. Later uploads still start locked. Public download icons appear
only for~allowed tracks, and~the~endpoint rechecks permission and~gallery
access. Playback delivers audio bytes to~the~browser, so this setting cannot
prevent a~technically capable visitor from capturing those bytes.

Durations use a~silent browser metadata reader, one visible row at~a~time, with
a~ten-second deadline. Unknown durations show a~dash. Browsers without
visibility observation use the~same serialized reader for~connected rows. No
server decoding occurs. Upload retries preserve acknowledged photos, PDFs
and~audio; an~unknown audio response requires checking the~gallery before
submitting again.

The~existing gallery playlist index remains in~its~hidden
\filepath{.gallery-music/} folder; immutable audio bytes live centrally in
\filepath{data/music/}. The~optional \ui{Maintenance > Maintenance Center} task
migrates legacy originals in~bounded, resumable steps. It~verifies each central
copy before switching the~reference and~preserves track IDs, order, visibility,
download flags and~slideshow settings; uncertain local files are~not~deleted.
Back up SQL, \filepath{data/music/} and~hidden legacy
indexes together. Each central asset counts once in~storage totals regardless of
gallery links; unmigrated local originals count separately. Deleting a~gallery
removes its~references but~retains shared originals. Gallery ZIP exports
and~transfers
omit music. See \filepath{docs/GALLERY_MUSIC.md}.

Removing a~catalogued local track first preserves and~verifies its~original
centrally; it~can~then be~added again without uploading. Failed preservation
keeps the~old playlist and~file. After relocation, the~selected maintenance task
removes at~most 20 catalog records per~step without gallery use or~repository
assets. Dirty sources and~registered assets remain protected, including assets
whose files need restoration. Album/tag-only records do~not~retain an~unbacked
song. Cleanup deletes metadata only and~logs its~count. Analyze again before
approving this revised task. Audio already deleted by~an~older removal needs
an~original or~backup; maintenance cannot recreate it.

The~same Maintenance Center includes a~default-selected optional
reconciliation for~legacy public PDF/music card counts. It~resumes in~steps
of~at~most 25 galleries and~hashes at~most one legacy OFP candidate up~to
25~MiB per~step. Missing or~dirty results stay omitted. Analysis does~not
read originals; the~task does~not~change them. Manual filesystem edits require
reconciliation. If~a~legacy OFP lacks a~digest, the~task hashes it~under the
shared persistent source lock and atomically saves the~digest in~its manifest;
the~PDF and~audio bytes remain unchanged. Music reconciliation may~create the
private ilepath{.gallery-music/} lock directory. A~new gallery with no~source
metadata can~start with ready-zero counts.

If~the~durable initial-pass receipt is~missing, the~first successful public
HTML request for~the~home, a~gallery, or~a~Smart Gallery queues the~media-count
pass after the~response. It~processes at~most 25 galleries per background step
and~continues while work remains; a~later public request can~recover interrupted
or~busy work after its~bounded retry delay. This is~a~one-time pass,
not~periodic
scanning. The~separate Maintenance Center task remains available for~explicit
reconciliation after later manual filesystem edits.

\subsection{Edit identity and~context}
\index{gallery metadata}
\index{date range}

The~editor lets you improve a~gallery over time. Titles and~descriptions
can~change as~a~collection develops. Date fields can~represent a~single day,
a~trip, a~season, or~a~longer historical period. EXIF date suggestions
can~examine photographs and~descendants, then offer an~editable range
for~approval.

The~complete editor records the~exact gallery revision that supplied its fields.
If~another tab, administrator, upload, move, scan, or~maintenance workflow
changes the~gallery first, an~older save is~refused instead of~silently
overwriting the~newer state. The~conflict view preserves entered non-secret text
and~shows a~safe current comparison. Review and~copy the~intended changes into
the~latest editor; re-enter passwords and~reselect files because private values
are~never copied into conflict details.

Use date suggestions as~a~helpful starting point. Scanned photographs,
screenshots, edited exports, and~cameras with an~incorrect clock can~produce
dates that deserve human review.\footnote{Applying an~EXIF-derived suggestion
updates the~proposed gallery fields through the~existing editor workflow.
The~administrator remains responsible for~the~final dates shown to~visitors.}

\subsubsection{Review date suggestions across galleries}
\index{gallery dates!EXIF review}

The~dedicated \ui{Gallery dates} maintenance page applies the~same EXIF evidence
across many galleries. It~can~review the~whole installation or~one selected
gallery branch. Each row shows the~current manual date range, the~suggested
range, the~number of~EXIF photographs and~gallery nodes that contributed
evidence, and~editable \ui{From}/\ui{To} fields. Rows without an~existing manual
date are~selected for~application by~default; rows that already have a~manual
date are~left unchecked so~a~bulk review does~not~silently overwrite
owner-entered chronology.

Only checked rows are~saved. Unchecked suggestions are~ignored,
and~the~administrator can~edit either date before applying the~selection.
If~older imported photographs do~not~have scanned EXIF capture dates yet, run
the~normal gallery image scan/import first. The~same focused suggestion action
is~available inside an~individual gallery editor and~can~refresh that editor
in~place.

Admin date fields keep the~native browser date input as~the~submitted value
but~add a~compact calendar opener plus \ui{Today} and~\ui{Delete} helpers.
\ui{Today} writes the~browser's current local calendar date; \ui{Delete} clears
the~field. The~enhancement is~re-applied to~forms loaded into~the~Admin side
panel, while the~underlying native date field remains usable without the~extra
JavaScript controls.

\subsection{Gallery editor control reference}
\paragraph{Schedule publication}
The~enhanced picker uses your browser's named timezone, so~its current-time
suggestions refer to~your local clock. A~UTC preview below it~shows the~exact
stored instant. No~timezone preference is~saved. Without JavaScript,
the~field remains explicitly UTC and~requires UTC input.
Nonexistent spring clock-change times are~rejected. For~a~new edit
in~a~repeated autumn hour, check the~UTC preview: the~browser chooses
the~earlier occurrence. An~unchanged saved later occurrence is~preserved.

In~\ui{Access}, enter a~future date and~time in~the~native
\ui{Schedule publication} field, following its displayed timezone, and~save.
The~pending indicator shows the~exact UTC deadline. Edit it~to~reschedule;
leave it~empty to~cancel. Choosing \ui{Published} also clears the~schedule.
For~the~unenhanced UTC field, convert your intended local time to~UTC
before entering it.

An~unpublished gallery retains its normal direct URL before the~deadline;
a~private gallery retains its private access rules. At~or~after the~deadline,
the~next ordinary PHP page GET/HEAD, including an~anonymous crawler, changes
canonical visibility to~public. Passwords, NSFW, share tokens, expiry,
and~parent restrictions stay unchanged. Scheduled publication also restores
public listing, just like choosing \ui{Published} manually.
Incomplete PDF children remain protected and~children are~not automatically
published. No~traffic means no~execution; static/CDN hits cannot trigger it.
A~large backlog catches up~in~batches of~16 across visits. No~cron, daemon
or~browser timer is~needed. Run normal database migrations before using this
control. Schedules stay in~the~database; sidecars retain resulting visibility
without transferring pending deadlines to~another installation.
The~lightweight check reuses the~existing gallery model and~access service;
publication writers load only when an~indexed due row exists.

\index{gallery editor!controls}
\index{gallery!visibility}
\index{gallery!display overrides}

The~gallery editor is~the~authoritative place for~settings that belong to~one
physical gallery. The~ordinary tabs are~Identity, API, Access, Display,
and~Media, followed by~Images and~optional Organizer/Renamer tools where
available. Advanced disclosures retain infrequent settings without removing them
from~the~shared save form. The~following table summarizes the~current
gallery-owned controls in~Version~\version{}.

\begin{longtable}{%
    >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.21\textwidth}%
    >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.27\textwidth}%
    >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.43\textwidth}%
}
\toprule
\textbf{Area} & \textbf{Control} & \textbf{Current behavior}\\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Area} & \textbf{Control} & \textbf{Current behavior}\\
\midrule
\endhead
Identity & Title and~description & Public editorial text. Maintained
translations can~be~stored separately without changing the~source text.\\
Identity & Manual date and~date range & Optional event or~shooting date plus
supported start/end range. EXIF-derived suggestions remain editable before
save.\\
Identity & Slug & Controls the~public gallery URL identity. Keep established
slugs stable when possible because bookmarks and~external links can~depend
on~them.\\
Identity & Folder name & Read-only physical name derived from the~sanitized
gallery title. Saving a~title change renames the~folder and~its~descendant paths
through the~normal mutation service.\\
Identity & Parent and~sort order & Changes hierarchy and~sibling ordering.
Moving a~gallery physically relocates its folder tree where the~configured
gallery root permits it.\\
Identity & Tags & Reusable tags separated by~commas. Suggestions are~weighted
by~nearby galleries, photographs, and~folder context rather than~being a~flat
random list.\\
Identity & SimBrief identifier and~defaults & A~single visible field accepts
numeric Pilot ID or~pilot name. The~administrator may~remember that identifier
and~the~source language for~future galleries; pre-filled values are~marked.\\
API & Upload endpoint and~keys & Copy the~gallery-scoped endpoint, generate
a~key with a~label, and~copy the~new raw value immediately. Active keys appear
only when present; the~raw value is~not~shown again. AI regeneration
and~migration are~under Advanced.\\
Access & Visibility & \ui{Public} is~listed normally. \ui{Unpublished} is~hidden
from normal listings but~can~still open from its normal URL when access rules
allow it. \ui{Private} is~administrator-only except through supported
direct-token access.\\
Access & Password lock & Independent from visibility. A~public, unpublished,
or~private gallery can~have its own password policy where the~access schema
is~available. Leaving the~new-password field empty preserves the~existing
password.\\
Access & Share link & Can~be~generated, regenerated, revoked, and~optionally
given an~expiry. Stored token material is~hash-protected; an~older
or~non-decryptable token may~need regeneration before a~copyable link
can~be~shown again.\\
Access & NSFW / 18+ & Marks the~gallery branch for~anonymous age confirmation.
The~protection also applies to~subgalleries and~served media according
to~the~effective access policy.\\
Display & Picture Game & Visitor-interaction switch for~the~comparison game
in~this gallery branch. Enabling the~game also requires image voting
to~be~available.\\
Display & Image voting & Enables public image likes for~this gallery. Disabling
it~leaves stored scores intact but~removes new vote controls and~blocks new
submissions.\\
Display & Show file names & Off by~default. Raw uploaded filenames remain hidden
unless enabled; custom photo titles and~descriptions are~still shown.\\
Display & Photo card format & Default preserves 4:3 desktop and~square mobile
previews. Explicit 16:9, 21:9, 4:3, 1:1, 4:5, and~9:16 crop photo previews only
inside this gallery on~every screen. Originals and~lightbox remain unchanged.\\
Display & EXIF/GPS display & \ui{Use global default}, \ui{Force on},
or~\ui{Force off}. The~effective state controls public GPS pins, gallery EXIF
maps, and~GPS coordinate display.\\
Display & Breadcrumb style & \ui{Default / inherit from Theme} follows the~Theme
choice; a~gallery can~select any of~the~nine registered styles. An~invalid
gallery override inherits the~Theme choice, then the~built-in \codeword{chevron}
fallback.\\
Display & Gallery-card description format & Inherits Theme by~default
and~can~override the~card layout for~this gallery where supported.\\
Display & Contained-picture badge & Inherits Theme or~overrides
the~stacked-picture icon and~contained-image count shown on~gallery cards.\\
Display & Lightbox browsing mode & Inherit Theme, classic single image, picture
strip, or~3D carousel. Fullscreen and~slideshow behavior remain available across
the~modes.\\
Display & Custom grid & First visible Display control: optional columns and~rows
for~this gallery. Without an~explicit grid, the~nearest parent grid that permits
subgallery inheritance applies, then Theme.\\
Display & Responsive thumbnail bounds & Optional minimum/maximum thumbnail-size
guardrails. A~save can~copy gallery bounds recursively to~descendants; recursive
save is~off by~default.\\
Display & Flight route & Advanced manual route text supports local identifier
lookup and~explicit \codeword{NAME@latitude,longitude} points. SimBrief can~save
an~OFP, description draft, and~resolved route coordinates.\\
Media management & Title picture & Automatic or~a~selected photograph, including
eligible photographs from descendants when the~picker offers them.\\
Media management & Uploaded gallery thumbnail & Separate gallery-card media
asset stored independently from ordinary gallery photographs.\\
Media management & Background source & Inherit Theme, upload a~new background,
choose an~existing gallery image, or~generate a~collage from public galleries.\\
Media management & Branding assets & Optional gallery-specific
banner/logo/separator assets override Theme fallbacks only for~that gallery
where configured.\\
Media management & Scan/import & Re-scans the~physical gallery folder
and~synchronizes supported media into the~catalog.\\
Media management & Create all thumbnails & Rebuilds thumbnails for~this gallery.
Select \ui{Include subgalleries} to~cover its descendants; other gallery
branches remain outside the~job. Browser generation is~selected by~default when
available, with a~server-generation option.\\
Tools & Organizer, Renamer, API & Date organizer, physical Media Renamer, upload
API key management, AI regeneration, and~gallery migration appear only when
their schemas/features are~available.\\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}

\important{Gallery-specific controls are intentionally not flattened into
the~global Settings hub. Inheritance is~part of~the~data model, so~a~global
search result may~link to~the~gallery editor without turning that gallery-owned
value into a~site-wide setting.}

\subsection{Choose a~cover and~visual identity}
\index{gallery cover}
\index{gallery branding}

A~cover acts as~the~gallery's visual invitation. Choose an~image that remains
recognizable as~a~thumbnail and~represents the~collection fairly. Faces, strong
silhouettes, clear subjects, and~uncluttered compositions often work well
at~small sizes.

Gallery branding can~provide a~logo, banner, background, separator,
or~presentation override according to~available controls. Begin with inherited
theme styling, then add gallery-specific assets where a~collection has~a~genuine
identity, such as~a~wedding, exhibition, client, or~long-running project.

\subsection{Move and~reorder galleries}
\index{gallery!moving}
\index{gallery!reordering}

Moving a~gallery changes its place in~the~hierarchy and~can~affect inherited
access or~presentation. Review the~destination before confirming the~move.
Reordering changes the~sequence shown among siblings and~supports a~curated
reading order.

Moving photographs between galleries uses a~durable operation journal.
The~Organizer physically moves each original and~verifies its recorded identity
before changing catalog ownership. If~a~worker or~connection stops partway
through, System Health and~Runtime Diagnostics show a~bounded action state
without exposing private paths. A~failed move gives a~safe reason on~screen;
trusted Admin logs retain more detailed private diagnostics. Stop competing
storage work, preserve a~backup, and~follow
\filepath{docs/IMAGE_MOVE_RECOVERY.md}; recovery retains changed or~unverifiable
files instead of~overwriting them.

Ordering may~be~chronological, geographical, narrative, alphabetical,
or~manually featured. Within one parent gallery, keep the~choice consistent
enough that visitors can~anticipate the~next item.

A~signed-in administrator can~also reorder the~direct subgallery cards
on~the~public gallery page. The~compact public reorder toolbar is~limited
to~the~cards visible on~the~current pagination page. The~save request includes
that page's offset and~visible count, and~the~server refuses the~change
if~the~submitted IDs no~longer match the~same current slice. It~changes sibling
\codeword{sort_order} values only; it~does not~change gallery parents or~nest
galleries.

When an~administrator is~signed in~while viewing a~public gallery, direct child
galleries that have~a~filled \ui{From} date can~also be~preview-sorted
from~oldest to~newest or~newest to~oldest. Child galleries without a~usable date
keep their existing positions. The~preview is~temporary until \ui{Save}
is~chosen; saving writes the~result as~the~real sibling order seen by~all
visitors. At~least two dated direct child galleries are~required before the~date
order can~be~saved.

The~public header provides \ui{View as anonymous} for~a~signed-in administrator
and~\ui{Exit preview} while visitor rules are~active. It~preserves the~current
public destination and~locale. There is~no~separate preview or~idle reorder
instruction bar. Card handles retain drag ordering; progress, saved results
and~failures remain visible when relevant. Date sorting keeps its~compact
translucent bar, anchored question-mark help and~explicit \ui{Save this order}.

\subsection{Delete a~gallery thoughtfully}
\index{gallery!deletion}

Gallery deletion can~affect source files, descendants, metadata, thumbnails,
links, and~visitor bookmarks. Review the~exact selected gallery and~its
children, create a~backup, and~use the~normal Admin deletion workflow.
A~temporary visibility change can~provide a~safer preparation period when
a~gallery may~still be~needed.

The~gallery trash bin is~enabled by~default. Deleting a~gallery through a~normal
administrator gallery-delete action moves the~selected folder and~its complete
descendant tree out~of~the~live gallery root, records the~metadata required
for~recovery, and~lists the~entry under \ui{Maintenance > Trash}. Public pages
stop discovering that subtree immediately, but~an~administrator can~restore
it~to~its~original path while that path remains free. Restore never overwrites
a~new live gallery occupying the~same location.

From the~Trash panel, choose \ui{Restore}, \ui{Delete permanently}, or~\ui{Empty
Trash}. Permanent deletion and~Empty Trash cannot~be~undone. Empty Trash works
in~bounded batches so~large trash collections do~not~require one unbounded
request. Individual-photo deletion is~not~part of~this gallery-level recovery
feature and~remains permanent. A~backup remains necessary protection against
storage failure, administrative permanent purge, or~damage outside PHP Gallery.

\section{Smart Galleries}
\index{Smart Galleries}
\index{virtual galleries}

A~Smart Gallery is~a~saved query, not~a~folder. Open \ui{Admin > Galleries >
Smart Galleries}, add conditions, and~combine them with nested \ui{AND},
\ui{OR}, and~\ui{NOT} groups. Fields cover source galleries and~descendants,
tags, capture dates, EXIF/GPS, text, AI metadata, duplicate state, file
characteristics, and~private editorial ratings.

Use the~existing \ui{Tag} condition when a~rule should include photo tags
and~tags
assigned to~the~physical gallery or~an~applicable ancestor. Add a~\ui{Photo tag}
condition to~match only tags assigned directly to~each photo. Its~operators for
one tag, its~absence, any or~all selected tags, and~untagged photos ignore
gallery, music, and~PDF assignments. The~all-tags condition requires every
selected tag on~the~same photo. Existing saved \ui{Tag} rules keep
their~previous
results and~the~rule format does not~need a~version change. Where the~typed tag
schema is~available, photo-only conditions accept image-category tags; confirmed
legacy schemas without that column still use direct image assignments, while
an~unknown schema blocks the~condition safely.

When inline administration is~enabled for~a~signed-in administrator,
a~single-row
quick-add field appears on~eligible photo cards and~in~the~normal
lightbox/fullscreen
viewer. It~adds one direct photo tag. The~last successfully added tag
is~prefilled in
the~same field for~the~next photo, but~opening a~photo never applies
it~automatically.
Eligible photo cards also show a~passive preview of~the~first three direct tags.
If~more tags exist, a~fourth non-clickable \texttt{…} chip indicates that others
are~omitted; a~small horizontal gap separates the~chips.
Suggestions appear only while typing; use \ui{Enter} or~the~small add control
to~apply
the~selected or~new tag. The~adjacent close control or~clicking the~same tag
button again
dismisses the~row without applying the~current input. The~row does~not~show
a~tag list or~removal controls; use
the~existing photo-edit pencil to~inspect or~remove tags. Press \ui{T} while
viewing a~photo
to~focus the~add field. The~row closes after the~server confirms the~add
and~the~view synchronizes;
a~rejected, unconfirmed, or~saved-but-unrefreshed result keeps it~open with
the~entered value.
In~the~ordinary lightbox, \ui{Escape} closes
the~row. In~native fullscreen, browsers that support page handling
of~\ui{Escape} let the~first
press close the~row while the~viewer remains fullscreen; the~next press exits
fullscreen.
If~that support is~unavailable or~rejected, native fullscreen may~exit first
and~the~row closes
with it. Quick adding is~not~available in~slideshow/presentation mode or
document/PDF viewers. In~a~Smart Gallery, a~tag change applies to~the~authorized
physical
source photo and~the~saved rule results update dynamically.

Use \ui{Preview} to~see the~current match count and~real matching photo cards,
then save and~optionally publish. Images and~files remain in~their physical
galleries, so~metadata or~rating changes alter membership immediately.
The~editorial rating is~private and~independent from visitor voting.
Administrators may~also use \ui{Save search as~Smart Gallery} from public
search.

Choose \ui{Unlisted} for~URL-only access, \ui{Root gallery} to~show the~Smart
Gallery on~the~homepage, or~\ui{Subgallery placement} to~let physical galleries
attach it. In~each physical gallery editor, select whether an~attachment belongs
\ui{Above gallery content} or~\ui{Below gallery content} and~give it~an~order
value. Above attachments render first, then ordinary subgalleries and~photos,
then Below attachments. Equal order values are~resolved consistently by~Smart
Gallery ID. Existing attachments default to~Below with order~0.

The~physical editor's \ui{Identity} tab shows \ui{Smart Gallery attachments}
before \ui{Advanced gallery settings}. Only attached rows remain visible.
Use \ui{+ Smart Galleries}, type a~name, and~activate a~matching result;
at~most eight matches appear, with a~displayed/total count for~broader searches.
Each row has its~own placement, order and~\ui{Detach} action.
These edits remain a~draft until \ui{Save gallery}. \ui{Create Smart Gallery}
opens the~definition editor in~a~new tab. Keyboard arrows and~Enter
reach the~results; Escape closes the~anchored panel. Native selection fields
remain available without JavaScript. Slideshow waits for~the~next decoded
image and~uses additive fades in~supporting browsers to~avoid a~dark pulse
between equally bright images; older browsers retain the~ordinary fade.

The~same virtual gallery may~be~attached beneath several physical galleries,
with independent placement and~order in~each parent. The~Smart Gallery editor's
\ui{Used in~physical galleries} section shows every parent and~lets you~save
its~placement/order or~\ui{Detach} it~without changing other parents. When
the~editor is~opened in~the~Admin side panel, these actions stay in~that panel;
ordinary form submission remains available when JavaScript is~disabled. A~public
Smart Gallery placed beneath a~private or~otherwise restricted parent still
inherits that parent's public access boundary for~the~card.

Public results and~counts always apply the~same source-access policy as~ordinary
public gallery content. A~matching photograph contributes only when its physical
gallery is~publicly listed and~accessible to~the~current visitor
and~the~photograph itself is~public. Existing password, share-link, inherited
visibility, unpublished-gallery, and~age-restriction rules therefore continue
to~apply. Query-string routes work without rewriting and~clean
\texttt{/smart/<slug>} URLs are~optional.

\subsection{Relationship safety and~repair}
\index{Smart Galleries!cycle safety}

A~Smart Gallery must not~resolve back into a~physical gallery that attaches it.
