The~server checks the~complete relationship graph whenever rules, attachments,
or~gallery hierarchy are~changed; browser filtering is~not~treated as~security.
This includes indirect paths through physical descendants and~other attached
Smart Galleries. Filesystem-derived hierarchy synchronization and~public-path
parent repair validate their complete proposed parent map before any~new parent
link is~written. The~current rule editor can~reference physical galleries
but~does not~offer a~direct Smart-Gallery-reference condition.

A~complete Admin drag-and-drop tree is~validated once in~its final form.
The~subsequent folder moves use that prevalidated map and~defer hierarchy
synchronization until the~final tree exists, so~a~temporary intermediate state
cannot reject an~otherwise valid batch. Every committed physical hierarchy
mutation clears the~request-local Smart Gallery graph cache before later
graph-dependent work continues.

If~older or~manually modified database data already contains a~cycle, public
rendering stops that invalid relationship instead of~recursing. The~Admin
attachment list marks the~relationship for~repair, and~\ui{Detach} remains
available. Validation and~runtime traversal both use conservative depth/node
limits and~request-local deduplication, so~malformed data cannot repeatedly
expand the~same Smart Gallery. The~normal result query remains paginated
and~does not~copy all matching photographs into PHP merely to~perform cycle
detection.

Migration \texttt{202608170002\_smart\_gallery\_attachment\_ordering.php} adds
the~per-parent placement and~order fields. Until that migration is~available,
existing attachments keep their~historical Below behavior, while Admin placement
mutations are~refused rather than~partially saved.

\subsection{Presentation settings}
\index{Smart Galleries!presentation}

By~default a~Smart Gallery follows the~current Theme and~site presentation.
Enable \ui{Override Theme defaults for this Smart Gallery} when this virtual
collection needs its~own columns, rows, pagination, thumbnail size limits,
thumbnail renderer, gallery-card layout, metadata overlays, lightbox behavior,
slideshow, download, voting, or~breadcrumb style. The~breadcrumb selector
appears with its~presentation controls; \ui{Default / inherit from Theme}
follows the~Theme choice. Sorting and~direction remain part of~the~Smart Gallery
definition.

A~Smart Gallery does not~inherit presentation from the~physical gallery in~which
its~card appears. One Smart Gallery can~appear beneath several physical
galleries, so~there is~no~single parent presentation with clear precedence. When
no~override exists, or~when stored presentation data is~missing or~invalid,
the~current Theme/site defaults remain the~fallback. Physical gallery and~photo
thumbnail limits still win if~a~Smart Gallery limit conflicts with them.

\subsection{Lightbox across result pages}
\index{Smart Galleries!lightbox}

The~large viewer is~not~limited to~the~photographs rendered on~the~current HTML
page. Smart Gallery cards remember each photograph's position in~the~complete
authorized result order. As~the~visitor moves forward or~backward, the~browser
requests nearby metadata in~small authorized groups from the~server. The~server
repeats the~same Smart Gallery rules, source access checks, sorting, and~stable
tie-break used by~the~page.

This keeps large virtual collections practical: opening a~Smart Gallery does
not~preload all originals or~all result metadata. Existing keyboard, touch,
fullscreen, zoom, close/reopen, and~slideshow behavior is~shared with ordinary
galleries. If~slideshow is~disabled for~that Smart Gallery, its~slideshow
controls are~omitted while normal galleries keep their~usual defaults.

\subsection{Downloads and~safety limits}
\index{Smart Galleries!downloads}

When site downloads and~the~Smart Gallery download option are~both enabled,
the~server can~prepare a~ZIP from the~current authorized matching originals.
It~re-evaluates the~saved rules and~access policy instead of~exposing filesystem
paths to~the~browser. The~archive path refuses collections above 5,000 matching
images and~also enforces an~aggregate original-file byte ceiling. Existing
installations use a~2~GiB ceiling unless
\texttt{smart\_gallery\_zip\_max\_source\_bytes} is~set in~\texttt{config.php}.
Concurrent requests for~the~same archive signature are~serialized with a~file
lock; after the~lock is~obtained the~cache is~checked again, a~unique partial
ZIP is~written, and~the~completed archive is~published with an~atomic rename.
Failure logs keep only bounded reason/class diagnostics rather than raw
exception messages or~private server paths.

Rules are~readable versioned JSON, never user SQL: fields/operators
are~allowlisted, values are~prepared parameters, depth is~limited to~five,
and~no~more than 50 conditions are~accepted. Deleted references match nothing
and~malformed or~unknown rule versions are~rejected. The~normal migration
workflow adds Smart Gallery presentation storage; existing Smart Galleries
inherit Theme/site defaults until an~override is~saved.

\section{Cooperative galleries}
\index{cooperative galleries}
Cooperative galleries connect independently operated HTTPS installations while
each owner retains their albums and~original files. Apply pending migrations
through the~normal administration workflow, then enable \ui{Cooperative
galleries} in~\ui{Features} on~each server. The~feature is~off by~default;
turning it~off preserves identities, friendships, proposals and~preferences.

Open \ui{Settings > Advanced > Friendly galleries} and~pair every participating
server directly. Friendship alone shares no~photographs. In~\ui{Album
collaborations}, select a~public, listed, password-free, non-NSFW album
and~prepare its reference code. One participant combines the~references into
an~immutable proposal; every participant reviews the~exact members
and~permissions and~explicitly approves locally. \ui{Deliver and~verify}
advances a~bounded sequence in~the~existing panel. Email invitations use
the~configured account-mail transport; opening an~invitation never grants
consent.

After activation, open the~shared photographs from the~collaboration panel
or~the~local album's shared-trip link. Each source retains attribution
and~pagination. Existing JPEG/WebP thumbnails and~previews are~authorized
independently; originals, protected albums and~remote writes are~unsupported.
Ordinary links work without JavaScript. Missing derivatives need the~normal
thumbnail workflow. New photographs receive complete path-based identities; old
names remain intact without bulk rebuilding, but~ambiguous ownership is~refused.

Withdrawal, changed source protection and~local friendship revocation block
subsequent authorized reads. An~unreachable peer remains bounded by~the~maximum
120-second verification lease; downloaded pixels cannot be~recalled. Adding
a~participant creates a~separate proposal requiring fresh approval from
everyone. Ordinary use can~advance technical renewal on~demand; unattended
renewal is~optional. Missing or~unknown required schema refuses sharing
and~mutations. See \filepath{docs/COOPERATIVE_GALLERIES.md} for~operational
limits and~deployment qualification.

\section{Windows companion uploader}
\subsection{Durable capture queue and~recovery}

Connect once, start capture and~press \ui{Finish} once at~the~end.
A~local producer records screenshots throughout upload, renewal, outages
and~rate-limit waits. Windows notifications have a~one-second reconciliation
backup. Each source generation has a~durable ID and~immutable FIFO sequence
before its~hash is~known. Only the~oldest unresolved item may query inventory,
connect or~upload. Partial, missing, waiting, unauthorized and~unknown heads
block newer files. Import uses the~same consumer; ZIP extraction stays local
and~each HTTP request carries one original plus optional thumbnails.

New groups use recognized Steam capture time, otherwise birth time or~first
observation, with deterministic ties. Mtime never reorders accepted work.
Late old files cannot change past transfers. A~new session still excludes
historical images. Restored confirmed bytes are~deduplicated per destination;
changed historical bytes without an~original fingerprint and~old unordered
multi-path queues require review rather than an~invented chronology.

The~compact \ui{Refresh queue} icon reconciles the~same session and~safely
wakes processing. Repeated requests coalesce. An~active request retains
its~owner until it~actually ends. FIFO, target/scope, retry deadlines,
uncertainty and~Finish cutoff remain binding. Finish persists its~first cutoff,
waits for~eligible unfinished sources and~requires a~new final scan after
the~last acknowledgement. Photo queues can~wait beyond five minutes.
Verified same-target screenshot Finish intent resumes after restart;
legacy key revocation retains its~separate renewed-confirmation rule.
Linked flight reports remain local.

An~unknown head shows \ui{Needs attention}. \ui{Resume} in~Watcher,
\ui{Force Continue} in~progress and~\ui{Review pending uploads} after restart
open the~same review for~the~exact retained source and~target. Local preview
and~authorized remote observation are~shown separately; a~matching image,
gallery number or~hash is~not~an~upload receipt. \ui{Confirm already uploaded}
requires the~exact durable acknowledgement. \ui{Skip photo} records
an~explicit skip, keeps the~original and~unknown history, and~allows
the~next FIFO item. Skips are~counted separately from successful uploads.
Cancel leaves the~item pending. Each later unknown needs its~own decision.
After submitting a~decision, \ui{Hide (saving decision)} only hides progress;
the~already authorized durable publication finishes normally.
In~a~short window, evidence scrolls while the~decision and~close controls
remain visible below it.
After a~worker restart, \ui{Resume pending uploads} also releases safe pending
work in~the~same session, preserving its~retry deadline.
Explicit recovery after a~failed Stop save also restores reconciliation
and~the~required final scan under the~original cutoff.

An~earlier unknown can~also block a~new session's first photo before
it~is~sent. Relinking and~deleting local files do~not~clear that history.
Review must distinguish the~older unknown from the~current unsent source;
reviewing the~older operation cannot acknowledge the~new one.
\ui{Inspect folder (read-only)} shows separate filesystem findings without
changing the~queue, startup exclusions, cutoff or~live status.
Progress always offers \ui{Hide}. \ui{Stop and keep pending} stops admission
and~retains accepted work and~its~cutoff for~explicit recovery. It~does~not
revoke access or~delete files. A~request still running keeps its~owner;
the~UI reports that wait instead of~claiming successful completion.
Ownership remains held until the~retained cutoff has~been saved and~read back;
a~failed save keeps processing paused under the~same owner.

Inventory/upload no~longer share 60 requests per IP per 300~s.
Upload grant budgets count requests; account/IP/global storage budgets charge
every original atomically, including existing multi-original multipart requests.
New 60-second windows and~locks allow 120/60 per verified grant/account/gallery,
240/120 per account and~IP, and~600/120 globally, respectively.
Combined pre-authentication admission allows 240 per IP and~600 globally
per minute. Two 45-image clients fit including controls and~safe head checks;
this does not~measure hosting capacity. Retry-After reaches the~same head;
missing hints use automatic 5--300~s backoff without exhausting completion
intent. Speculative Photos preload/prefetch yields during upload blocks.
Local preparation failures can~retry; unknown dispatched uploads require
review without resend or~the~next image. Hash inventory and~request-local
IDs are~not operation receipts. Idle updates omit capture continuation;
active updates retain strict nonce/version checks. Diagnostics retain head,
counts, producer/consumer progress, next retry and~historical failure.
Feature BASIC checks source only; installed Windows, layout and~deployed
server behavior require separate acceptance. The~permanent contract
is~\texttt{docs/UPLOADER\_QUEUE.md}.

\subsection{Uploader layout}

While Watcher capture is~active, a~red dot pulses on~the~tray icon in~step
with the~in-app indicator. All Watcher markers share a~267~ms phase interval,
or~about 534~ms for~a~complete bright/dim cycle; the~update badge remains
independent.

The~normal launch opens on~\ui{Photos}; the~navigation order is~\ui{Photos},
\ui{Watcher}, \ui{Import}, \ui{Activity}, \ui{AI}, and~\ui{Settings}.
With an~active gallery link, Photos loads the~gallery tree on~the~left
and~opens an~initial gallery on~the~right. Expand or~collapse a~branch or
the~whole tree. Selecting a~gallery shows its~photos in~the~same window.
The~default-on \ui{Subgalleries} switch shows direct child-folder cards above
the~photos. Each cover uses the~first authorized direct thumbnail. When none
is~available, the~card shows a~neutral folder. Photos keeps gallery listings
in~memory and~uses the~current link grant.
When disconnected, \ui{Connect} opens the~existing address and~browser
approval flow. The~active link can then browse public photos directly.
Choose the~independent upload destination with \ui{Use for uploads}.
A~logon \texttt{--startup} launch remains in~the~system tray until restored.
On~macOS, source launches use the~normal Tk window.
Temporary links end with the~process.

Large trees initially load at~most 500 galleries, then continue automatically
from the~saved position, one page every five seconds. Loaded galleries remain
usable while the~loading status shows remaining work. Errors pause continuation
and~offer an~explicit retry; normal browsing needs no~\ui{Load more} click.
Loading status and~a~small smooth spinning ring sit above the~tree. They remain
visible during initial reads and~paced continuation, then stop on~completion,
error or~disconnection without estimating a~percentage.
Newer servers can~advertise an~optional complete JSON tree with a~revision.
They recheck current account permissions before returning the~tree or~an
unchanged reply. The~existing paged API remains available for~older servers,
unavailable data and~trees beyond the~complete-response limit.
The~client first shows the~last-known saved structure and~restores
the~account's last explicitly selected gallery. It~requests the~optional tree
only when advertised; invalid or~unsupported replies retain the~saved tree
and~use the~paged API, including with web~1.0.3. Every successful page
and~confirmed creation saves known metadata, preserving earlier rows even
if~loading is~interrupted. Accepted local writes finish on~normal exit.
Only a~complete successful refresh removes missing rows. The~circular arrow
beside expand, collapse and~add refreshes the~tree, including renamed galleries
and~changed parents, while preserving expansion, selection and~the~photo view.
If~the~selected gallery moves, its~new ancestor path opens to~keep it~visible.
Repeated refreshes ignore obsolete responses. Short UI batches update
changed rows. Incremental loading preserves numeric sibling order, including
later rows. A~row whose parent arrives later moves under that parent. Tooltips appear only
while the~pointer remains over their trigger; keyboard focus alone does~not
open them. Windows protects saved titles
with user-scoped DPAPI; unsafe
or~corrupt local storage is~a~cache miss. Media still needs live authorization;
new upload/create actions wait for~fresh gallery confirmation. This metadata
does~not grant offline photo access.
Maintainer diagnostics can~select \texttt{native\_only} in~the~hosted WinApp
workflow. The~central \texttt{winapp-native} suite records its~narrower native
viewer scope and~cannot qualify complete WinApp coverage. Default complete
discovery and~its~existing time limit remain unchanged.
Contextual panels keep their existing keyboard and~outside
click dismissal. Windows clips their rounded boundary without dark outer
corner blocks; if~native shaping is~unavailable, a~flat opaque themed panel
remains usable.

The~Watcher screen shows the~gallery target. A~verified API-mode title survives
refreshes of~the~same saved connection; credential or~upload-mode changes
invalidate it. Selecting a~Photos album does not~change the~upload title.
Its~top API-key control opens
a~compact key dialog with a~masked key and~\ui{Save \& Test}; this~control
remains available in~the~header on~every screen. The~separate ordinary header
\ui{Link gallery} flow remains the~upload/search path. Unsaved edits do~not~replace
the~saved key. The~app header also has Settings,
updates, and~help controls. The~Watcher screen keeps the~selected folder path,
watcher status, and~source-delete warning visible.
If~the~configuration cannot be~read or~verified, startup stops before work
begins. Preserve the~original file and~restore a~valid backup; a~missing file
still starts with first-run defaults. Windows reparse points and~power-loss
durability have not~yet been qualified.
Click \ui{Link gallery} in~the~main window. Paste the~gallery address from
your browser into the~dialog and~confirm with \ui{Link gallery}.
You can also enter a~host and~installation path without a~scheme.
On~confirmation, WinApp adds \texttt{https://} and~shows the~normalized
address before checking the~gallery.
An~HTTP address on \texttt{localhost} can link to~a~gallery on~the~same
computer. Remote installations use verified HTTPS.
The~dialog shows progress while waiting for~approval. If~the~connection
fails, it~displays the~error and~lets you try again.
Sign in~as an~administrator in~the~browser and~click \ui{Approve}.
Return to~WinApp when the~browser shows \ui{Approval received}. WinApp
shows the~linked site's address and~loads the~first gallery page
automatically. \ui{Cancel} declines the~request.

If~linking fails, copy the~WinApp diagnostics. The~separate browser-linking
entry retains the~last failure, request phase, HTTP status and~response type,
including recognized hosting verification pages. It~distinguishes a~blocked
request from a~gallery reporting linking unavailable. Credentials and~raw
response bodies are~excluded.
Use the~public website URL, including its~public subdirectory when needed.
Internal hosting directories do~not~belong in~the~link address. Servers with
internal path rewriting need PHP Gallery~1.0.1 for~public-mount verification.

Use the~ellipsis beside \ui{Unlink gallery} to~open the~links chooser.
Select an~entry under \ui{Linked now} to~switch using its current approval.
The~profile keeps up~to~20 \ui{Recent addresses} across restarts. Select one
to~start a~new approval. The~power icon unlinks the~selected site and~the
cross removes a~recent address. The~upload destination has its own selection.

Open \ui{Choose gallery…} beside the~address and type in~\ui{Search galleries}.
Suggestions favor names, then descriptions and~gallery tags. Names tolerate
a~small typo. Quoted phrases and~\texttt{AND}/\texttt{OR} work as on~the website.
Indentation and~the selected row's parent titles show the~hierarchy.
The~arrow loads more matches. With basic list access you can filter the~current
page by name. Approve a~new link on a~supporting server for catalog search.

With approved upload access, choose a~result and click \ui{Use for uploads}.
On~a~supported local profile,
including a~source run on~macOS, WinApp uses the~approved upload access
automatically. After verification,
\ui{Upload gallery} shows the~destination in~Watcher and~Import. The~ellipsis
reveals more gallery actions. \ui{Unlink gallery} ends the~current link.
After restart, reconnect to~the~same account and~select the~gallery again.
Use \ui{Manage link…} in~Settings for~advanced access. API-key uploads use
their own \ui{Gallery URL} and~gallery-scoped key.

Separately select \ui{Read gallery details} and~approve the~read permission
to~use \ui{Read selected details} for~a~selected gallery. It~shows its~ID,
title, revision and~stored visibility, access and~listing settings, not~inherited
effective access. A~read already checked by~the~server can~finish
after concurrent revocation. Older servers may~not~support this permission
or~endpoint; the~client reports unsupported or~unavailable without silently
reducing scope. The~saved \texttt{pgu\_} upload path remains available. Real
Windows, deployed HTTPS and~database behavior remain unqualified.
Delegated upload success requires a~new image row and~the~observed original
file at~its~final gallery path. If~confirmation fails, the~file may~already
exist and~the~client keeps the~attempt unresolved without automatic retry.
Revocation during an~in-flight upload cannot roll back an~already stored file.
The~new linked client requires the~exact \texttt{fresh\_insert\_v1} upload
evidence marker; an~older linked server leaves the~attempt unresolved. The
\texttt{pgu\_} upload path remains unchanged.
A~live reply may replace the~write-ahead uncertainty only for~the~same
positive attempt, unchanged
source identity and~hash, and~a~validated fresh image insertion. It~must report
one uploaded image and~a~nonnegative scanned count; an~unknown or~lost reply,
hash check or~inventory never clears the~marker. No retry, source deletion or
exactly-once guarantee follows from this correction.
Server file identity is~not byte-exact proof against outside writers. Client
source identity and~hash checks are~not atomic against local writers; Windows
reparse and~power-loss behavior remain unqualified.
Original ZIPs and~extracted staging directories remain retained regardless
of~age or~upload confirmation for~manual review; automatic age-based staging
cleanup is~disabled because visible job IDs do~not prove complete history.
For~exactly one uncertain linked upload, \ui{Check uncertain upload} asks
for~the~file size in~bytes, up~to~64~MiB, and~checks its~stored hash once
without opening the~local file. A~match reports
observed bytes, not~completion or~database registration; an~unknown result
does not~prove absence. Neither result clears the~record, deletes files or
retries the~upload. Older servers may~not~support this check.
To~review one current unknown upload, first inspect the~gallery and~original
source. The~optional hash check observes bytes only; it~does~not~confirm
registration or~prove absence. The~explicit review prompt defaults to~No.
Review preserves the~uncertainty marker, original and~ZIP. The~same source path
or~bytes stay blocked after restart, while other eligible sources can~continue.
Review does~not~resend or~delete the~unknown source.
Linked uploads require an~unredirected profile on~a~fixed local Windows
drive or~an owner-controlled local macOS volume. Close older uploaders
using the~same profile first.
The~separate \texttt{gallery:visibility} permission is~offered only when the
server advertises it, and~requires explicit browser approval. Before a~change,
the~client confirms the~current gallery state. A~stale revision or~schedule
conflict causes a~read-only refresh; the~change is~not~replayed. Older servers
omit this permission. The~operator command
\texttt{inspect\_gallery\_visibility.php} is~read-only by~default. Only an
explicit current \texttt{--reconcile=FINGERPRINT} can~repair eligible version-two
sidecar evidence and~retire its~marker after synchronized readback; it~does~not
write the~database row or~revision. Version-one markers are~inspection-only.
Candidate \texttt{8de8e50a} passed BASIC run \texttt{42107055888} and~WinApp
run \texttt{42107055797}. Installed GUI, MariaDB, and~deployed behavior
remain unqualified.
An~active gallery listing link permits browsing listed public, non-sensitive
photos. Supported servers offer explicit \texttt{presentation:read} consent
in~the normal link flow for presentation settings and~visible music.
For public-only links, every ancestor must be~public, listed, normal-access,
and~non-NSFW. Separate administrator media consent lets Photos display hidden,
restricted and~NSFW media. The~server rechecks the~live administrator grant.
Internal pages carry up~to 64~photo descriptors, or~eight on~older servers.
Binary responses have a~16~MiB cap and~playlists carry up~to 32~visible
MP3/WAV tracks. If~the
smallest thumbnail is~missing, the~server may use another existing rendition
or~the~bounded public display file. The~photo pane and~lightbox share the~main
WinApp window.
An~incomplete HTTP~304 cache reply triggers at~most one fresh authorized read;
the~rejected reply never grants access to~stale image bytes.
The~responsive grid scrolls continuously and~requests only visible thumbnails;
it~does not request detail photos while browsing. Explicit \ui{Preload gallery}
downloads both thumbnails and~lightbox renditions. Click a~photo to~select it
and~show its~metadata in~the~adjustable right pane. Double-click or~press Enter
to~open the~lightbox. Arrows navigate the~grid; Space toggles a~photo.
Control-click (Command-click on~macOS) toggles individual selections;
Shift-click or~Shift-arrow selects an~anchored range within the~64-photo limit.
Control-arrow (Command-arrow on~macOS) moves focus without clearing selection.
Only selected photos have a~small rounded outline fitted to~the~actual preview
and~its~shared rim, without an~empty caption area below the~image. Selected
formats keep a~compact 6-pixel gap between card rims in~both directions;
remaining viewport width stays after the~last column. Default retains its
stable natural-preview row reservation.
Filename hints use a~compact
contrasting tooltip. The~pane and~context information share authorized metadata
and~formatting, with a~short bounded session cache. Left/Right loops through
the~whole album. Close the
lightbox or~press Escape to~restore the~tree and~grid, including after an~image
error. By~default, thumbnails trim detected black bands and~fit the~whole
remaining scene. Seven visible shape buttons in~the~album toolbar apply
Default, 16:9, 21:9, 4:3, 1:1, 4:5 or~9:16 immediately, without a~Save step.
Each rectangle illustrates its~ratio; a~quiet accent marks the~active choice.
Explicit ratios center-crop grid previews; originals and~the~lightbox retain
their existing presentation. Choices stay local to~WinApp, separately for~each
gallery and~verified site/account. Website and~upload settings stay independent.
A~small \textbf{!} indicates a~local save failure; the~chosen view remains
applied and~the~tooltip explains how to~retry. Switching uses retained previews.
Photos and~thumbnails have a~separate
five-minute limit of~600 requests per IP and~3,600 globally. On~HTTP~429,
Photos displays the~limit and~pauses speculative requests until \ui{Retry}. Source launches use Pillow
from the~same Python interpreter.
The~optional server capability \texttt{media:thumbnail-batch-v1} is~controlled
in~\ui{Admin > Features}. It~defaults to~enabled for~fresh installations and~upgrades
without a~saved choice; an~administrator can~turn it~off, and~saved choices
survive updates. While no~choice is~saved, the~legacy
\codeword{PHP_GALLERY_THUMBNAIL_BATCH_V1} value is~used: unset or~1 means on;
0 or~another value means off. A~saved choice always takes precedence. It~uses
the~existing bearer media authority without a~new grant and~accepts a~fixed
read-only request of~up~to~32~photos, 8~MiB total and~4~MiB per~image. Responses are~
\texttt{private, no-store}. SHA-256 verifies bytes but grants no~cache
authority; Admin preload remains separate. The~server buffers at~most one
4~MiB image at~a~time. It~charges N requested photos against the~existing
600/IP and~3,600/global limits and~the~600/account limit per~five minutes.
When negotiated, WinApp starts with groups of~eight and~uses up~to~sixteen
after a~healthy group; slow, large or~exceptional groups return to~eight.
Unverified originals retain individual confirmation. Target FPM, proxy and~HTTPS
acceptance remains unverified; the~default does not establish host acceptance.
Existing single reads remain the~fallback for~mixed versions and~exceptional
images.
Retry reloads failed content. Use the~wheel, a~two-finger scroll or~+ and~\ensuremath{-}
to~zoom. Press 0 to~reset the~view and~drag to~pan. F or~F11 toggles
fullscreen. Pointer motion briefly reveals the~controls. When permitted coordinates exist, an~anchored
location pin offers \ui{Open in OpenStreetMap} in~the~system browser. Browsing
prepares the~previous and~next details in~bounded memory only while the~lightbox
is~open. Closing it~clears queued prefetch; an~in-flight bounded read may finish.
\ui{Fit by content} crops
detected black bands in~both views. \ui{Show original frame} restores the~full
frame. The~image keeps its aspect ratio in~the~available area. During a~resize,
the~lightbox updates a~preview and~refines it when the~window settles.
On~macOS~15 or later, compatible HDR source data can use AppKit on~an~EDR
display when PyObjC and~the~Tk window bridge are~available. Other configurations
use the~8-bit Pillow/Tk SDR renderer, including Windows.
Windows fullscreen shortcuts also work with NumLock, CapsLock and~ScrollLock
or~without a~numeric keypad. Control/Alt combinations and~text entry retain
their behavior. Large JPEG/MPO details use a~reduced decoder raster before
RGB allocation, within the~same 36-million-pixel budget and~display bound.
This also applies below the~pixel ceiling when the~source exceeds the~display
target; supported JPEG scaling reduces the~raster before conversion.
If~a~detail still exceeds the~budget, one authorized thumbnail can~provide
a~limited preview, including on~older webs. Authorization, cancellation,
throttling and~corrupt data do~not use this fallback. Copied diagnostics retain
safe decode reasons, dimensions and~limits, plus a~separate bounded F/F11 trace.
The~initial authorized photo page appears before optional presentation settings
are~loaded through the~same worker. A~green preload indicator means verified
encoded files; detail decoding and~required private server checks still occur.
These paths are~shared by~Windows and~macOS; installed latency needs a~live test.
Recent album metadata appears immediately from the~same live session while
a~fresh server read follows, without client visibility filtering. Unchanged
refreshes keep tiles and~update their verification ages. Large JPEG thumbnail
variants also reduce the~decoder raster before RGB allocation.
The~shared-header \ui{Thumbnails} indicator and~\ui{Photo storage} control use
the~compact Watcher-pill style: icons, short counts and~simple bars, without
long labels. The~storage popup opens on~demand below its~header control and
aligns with the~right edge of~the~visible main-window client area. Window
resizing and~changed values reposition the~whole panel inside that area.
It~shows
all~stored typed thumbnails and~their bytes; its~detail separates ordinary quota
and~\ui{Keep preloaded}, listing retained thumbnails, other media and~galleries
separately. RAM means this process's Windows working set or~macOS resident set;
unknown VRAM remains unknown. Selecting a~quota saves it~immediately, without
a~Save button or~confirmation. A~save failure keeps the~previous value and
shows the~error inline. Accounting scopes do~not~change.
The~photo grid combines repeated scroll/layout signals into one scheduled
viewport update. Visible thumbnails load first, with a~bounded neighboring-row
buffer for~quick reversals. Decoding stays in~a~worker and~Tk image updates
stay on~the~GUI thread. Copied diagnostics include event/update counts, image
creations/releases, cache hits and~the~longest viewport callback. These figures
are~local observations; installed scrolling still needs manual qualification.
One thumbnail worker publishes ready images in~bounded serial batches without
a~separate GUI polling wait between photos. Ready results and~decoded-cache
hits attach in~small GUI slices; foreground work and~viewport changes interrupt
remaining reads. A~brief local pause after a~transient transport failure resumes
pending thumbnails only after the~delay has~elapsed and~the~prior request
has~finished; an~HTTP~429 still waits for~the~existing explicit \ui{Retry} action.
The~session request gate
still owns authenticated transport.
Diagnostics separate disk access, gate wait, credential renewal, media
transport, decoding, queue wait and~Tk application. Compare cold and~warm
browsing separately; these measurements do~not establish FPS.
Current authorized disk hits use one independent disk/decode worker with
a~four-result queue; Admin reuse needs a~fresh, account-bound catalogue proof.
The~decoded LRU accounts four bytes per~pixel within 16~MiB and~256 entries.
Visible entries have eviction priority; full-cache overscan cannot displace
recent history. Tk images remain limited to~the~admitted row band.
Sequential media GETs may~reuse one HTTP/1.1 connection and~one verified
SSLContext, with both serialization gates retained. Only fully consumed,
validated responses can~be~reused. Server closure, incomplete bodies,
cancellation and~renewal discard the~socket. One stale borrowed GET may
reconnect before a~response, within its~deadline. The~initial idle bound
is~15 seconds; real hosting support and~latency remain to~be~measured.
Diagnostics count requests, connections and~TLS handshakes separately,
including discarded work, and~split context, DNS, TCP, TLS, headers and~body.
The~cache menu offers \textbf{Preload thumbnails only} for~the~selected
direct gallery. It~resumes current authorized cache data, yields to~browsing,
respects Retry-After and~never requests full photos or~subgalleries.
Its~completion does~not mean lightbox renditions are~preloaded; the~original
\textbf{Preload gallery} keeps that two-rendition meaning.
First viewing uses the~negotiated verified Admin binary protocol directly;
saving full private files still requires explicit local preload intent.
A~newer authenticated page with the~same photo and~strong validator can~confirm
an~older mounted descriptor, using the~new page's real proof time.
Full preload revalidates stale stored full responses instead of~skipping them
because their files exist. A~valid 304 refreshes age without a~body download;
final coverage still means verified files, not~simultaneous fresh deadlines.
The~current photo or~page leads background refresh. Superseded image reads
cancel, and~adjacent lightbox preparation leads remaining grid thumbnails.
Leaving a~view withdraws its~cancelled waiting tickets from the~serial gate.
Both preload modes yield to~the~current view before catalogue pages and~images.
Viewed thumbnails save automatically in~the~account-bound ordinary cache under
the~shared 4~GiB default limit; unrelated gallery pins cannot bypass that limit.
Cache events update its~measured count without waiting for~slower retained/RAM
accounting. Full preload counts each confirmed stored photo during transfer,
then reconciles final coverage. Selecting an~image already being prefetched
adopts its~prepared result instead of~restarting the~same request.
Diagnostics separate detail/prefetch disk, network, 304, bytes and~RAM/adoption.
An~isolated Windows pixel comparison checks the~settled view after scroll;
it~does~not prove removal of~transient strips during motion or~higher FPS.
One per-user disk cache stores opted-in photos and~tracks with verified
content digests. Ordinary thumbnails have a~separate configurable
4~GiB/262,144-entry
area. Full/detail photos and~audio retain the~1~GiB/4,096-entry area.
The~compact control beside Watcher opens a~shared per-user limit
of~1/2/4/8/16/32/64~GiB. The~main count and~bytes include ordinary and~typed
retained thumbnails. Used/limit in~the~detail covers ordinary files only;
pinned galleries, detail/audio and~decoded/Tk memory
remain separate. Loading/unavailable does~not mean zero or~offline permission.
SQLite accounting updates totals by~delta under the~existing lock; dirty
mutations and~changed directory generations require bounded cancelable
metadata recovery off Tk. The~index never grants access or~replaces SHA-256.
Preload shows activity before listing and~during checks, foreground waits
and~Retry-After. Processed, current-hit and~confirmed-write counts differ;
final checks reuse only exact unchanged verified file identities.
Failed writes and~no-store replies cannot claim complete thumbnails.
Serial FIFO admission preserves viewport/Watcher turns; speculative warm
queues cannot postpone explicit preload indefinitely. Hosting speed and~actual
rendition size remain live-test questions. The~right inspector saves its~own
preferred width in~the~existing layout file, including hide/restart/resize.
Optional RAM means current-process Windows working set or~macOS RSS, without children;
per-process VRAM remains unavailable. Decoded memory remains bounded at~16~MiB.
Each evicts only its~own least recently accessed entries; large photos cannot
displace ordinary thumbnails. Gallery retention and~private-preload settings
remain shared. Unpinning checks capacity for~all destination copies before
retained cleanup. Old thumbnails migrate by~their exact rendition identity
without resetting freshness. A~matching validator from the~current album catalogue
reuses a~local thumbnail immediately. Photos without this proof use the~usual
60-second freshness and~authorized ETag revalidation. The~current authorized
playlist can reuse a~track by SHA-256, size and~format. Media available
only to~administrators uses transient storage by~default.
Cache icons update automatically from loaded metadata: cloud means online,
half-circle partial, filled circle local and~dotted circle unchecked.
A~partial catalogue cannot prove full coverage. The~heading's cache icon
opens the~gallery menu.
\ui{Preload gallery} prepares direct photos, including thumbnails and~lightbox
images, in~the~background with progress and~Cancel. Subgalleries are~separate
targets. \ui{Keep preloaded} retains successfully prepared files outside
ordinary cleanup, entry limits and~byte limits, including after restart.
Disabling it~returns the~gallery to~ordinary cache policy, which may evict
excess files. Cancel keeps prepared files and~the~retention preference without
claiming full coverage. Existing verified bytes are~reused when retrying after
a~server limit. No-store media remains transient; retained bytes grant
no~offline access or~expired-link permission. One explicit preload accepts
up~to~100,000 direct photos as~a bounded work operation.
An~explicit Admin Photos preload can also retain private or~draft photos
on~supporting servers. The~photo, gallery and~complete ancestry must
be~verified as~non-NSFW. The~saved preference and~private files belong
to~the~verified account and~installation. A~current authorized catalogue proof
can~reuse matching thumbnails for~at~most 60~seconds without another binary
request. Ordinary full-image HTTP reads and~explicit preload still require
their own verified body within the~60-second freshness window and~normal
200/304 revalidation. The~full-image viewer can separately use an~explicitly
downloaded snapshot when saved full-preload intent, the~exact issuer, verified
account, gallery and~photo, a~hash-valid file and~a~live catalogue proof no
older than 60~seconds all match. The~downloaded body’s original HTTP age does
not limit this viewer-only path and~is never rewritten. An~expired page proof
refreshes the~small photo metadata page for~the~group, not each full-image
body. Missing or~corrupt files use the~normal server read. Prepared details
keep their 15-second bound; authentication, account changes, cancellation,
known rejection and~opt-out still block reuse. Unknown proof, renewal and~known
rejection otherwise require the~normal server path. Changes since the~last
page are~detected at~the~next authenticated read, not instantly.
Older servers and~ordinary restricted browsing remain transient.
A~server-directed retry delay shows a~cancelable countdown; exhausted
retries retain completed files. Zero stored photos report a~safe reason
instead of~partial success.
The~presentation button opens fullscreen and~loops through the~album at~the
server's enabled interval or~a five-second default. The~authorized soundtrack
starts with the~presentation. The~music controls play, pause and~change tracks.
The~player reads the~local file and~prepares the~next track.
Right-click a~gallery or~photo for information and~authorized visibility
changes. Click outside the~panel or press Escape to close it. Admin photo
information includes format, dimensions, stored EXIF and~GPS when allowed by
the~gallery. Thumbnail quality shows the~server's current generation settings.
The~gallery panel shows direct inventory and~local cache coverage.
A~tree icon appears
when coverage is~known. Use the~add icon in~the~tree toolbar for a~root
gallery and~the~add icon beside the~album title for a~subgallery. You can
also add a~subgallery from a~gallery menu or~blank photo space.
The~wheel and~trackpad scroll the~grid and~zoom the~lightbox.
\ui{Edit gallery} opens the~selected gallery's web Admin editor in~the~system
browser's right-side panel. It uses the~browser's own Admin login and~sends
no~WinApp credentials. Older servers open their normal full-page editor.
The~tree restores expanded and~collapsed branches for each linked site and
account. Use the~circular arrow beside the~album title to~refresh its photos.
WinApp checks the~visible Admin album's index about every 30~seconds and
reuses unchanged thumbnails. Older servers support manual refresh.
Scrolling uses bounded RAM and~the~verified persistent disk cache. Thumbnail
reuse is~bound to~the~issuer, session, gallery, photo, public eligibility,
strong validator and~private account. Current proof timestamps and~row epochs
authorize reuse; proof renewal may retain unchanged content, while expired or
denied access cannot reuse it. Before per-photo reads, WinApp coalesces expired
visible-thumbnail proof renewal through the~existing bounded catalogue refresh.
This supports public and~eligible non-NSFW Admin media; older servers and
no-store responses keep their existing authorized reads. One retry per~existing
revision interval bounds repeated refresh.
Select photos with~Control-click on~Windows, Command-click on~macOS or
the~photo menu. The~context menu can delete one photo or~the~selection.
WinApp uses the~current Admin Photos link. Confirm each batch of~up
to~64~photos in~WinApp. It uses the~same permanent deletion
operation as~web Admin. Refresh and~review the~album after an~interrupted
response before confirming another deletion.
Use \textbf{Use this gallery for Watcher} in~a gallery context or~the~Watcher
rail menu. WinApp starts capture with a~temporary gallery key and~shows a~red
REC indicator. Photos remains selected. A~small header display uses icons for
Watcher state, the~observed accepted-photo count and~the~last scan time.
A~dash means the~value is~not~yet available. Hover for~the~full gallery,
state and~queue description. This display sits just left of~\ui{Thumbnails}.
Its~Stop icon uses the~existing safe queue drain and~report finalization,
so~ordinary capture needs no~visit to~the~Watcher tab. Open that tab whenever
its~full controls are~needed. The~Watcher rail icon pulses red on~every tab. A~filled red
light marks the~capture album and~its heading. A~hollow light marks
a~collapsed branch that hides it.
SimBrief is available for a~detected flight simulator. Finish
drains screenshots, saves available flight reports for~a simulator session
and~revokes the~capture key. The~website stays linked.
The~Watcher panel reports preparation or~a reason it could not start.
A~new gallery or~a recovered creation confirmed by~the~current connection
can~use this action immediately, without reloading Photos. Cached rows still
need live confirmation. If~the~gallery or~upload access changes while
the~menu is~open, an~explanation stays in~the~Photos panel; reconnect
or~refresh Photos before reopening the~action.
During startup, a~busy indicator names the~preparation phase. Wait for~REC
before creating a~new screenshot. Stop and~Finish show preparation immediately
before the~upload drain and~report phases.
Recent Activity rows are saved in~bounded background batches. Normal exit
attempts a~flush within its deadline. Storage failure, timeout or~a crash can
lose buffered rows; failures are logged and~the~rotating log is written
immediately.
Destination selection checks accepted imports in~the~local upload history.
It uses the~same 256~MiB history limit as~state updates. Unverifiable history
or~unfinished work for~another destination still blocks the~change.
The~updated native viewer needs a~current installed
Windows click-through. Earlier protocol evidence predates this UI revision.
The~filesystem writer fence is~an~inactive prototype and~does not~protect
gallery operations until its~coordination has~been qualified.
The~experimental separate \texttt{galleries:create} permission requires
explicit consent and~verified scope storage. Its~source refuses occupied targets, canonical catalog drift and
catalogs over 10,000~rows including the~new gallery; slug search checks at~most
128~candidates. Neither is~a~time limit.
A~pending intent is~not automatically repeated after an~uncertain result.
A~pending linked-create reservation temporarily blocks Admin folder imports,
moving discovered photos, deleting discovered folders, copying selections,
moving galleries to~or~restoring them from Trash, metadata-organizer moves,
consistency repairs and~ordinary gallery creation, even for
another path. Do~not delete the
reservation: safe automatic cleanup is~not yet available. Existing API-key
upload remains available. This same-database guard is~not a~filesystem fence.
Before creating or~checking a~retained intent, the~client requires a~fresh
server identity matching the~selected or~saved Admin account.
The~maintenance CLI can~inspect private original evidence from standard input
and~report ledger correlation only; it~does~not prove that a~worker stopped
or~authorize another creation.
Before sending, the~client verifies a~complete staged intent and~publishes it
without replacing another key. An~interruption may leave a~stale lease that
blocks work; Windows filesystem and~power-loss behavior remain unqualified.
\ui{Resume original creation} shows the~original title and~parent for~explicit
confirmation, then sends the~same operation key and~inputs to~the~same HTTPS
issuer and~verified Admin account. Existing pending or~reconciliation claims remain
blocked and~can~require administrator reconciliation. This protection requires
the~original server ledger; restoring an~older database backup can~remove it.
The~unconfirmed status offers explicit resume or~\ui{Close unresolved creation}
after reviewing the~gallery and~storage. Closure shows the~original title and~parent for~explicit
confirmation. It~removes the~local intent only after the~server confirms a~durable
closure of~the~same key. An~unknown result leaves the~intent in~place. Closure
is~refused while the~server claim is~pending. Closure
does not~prove that no~gallery or~files exist or~that earlier work has stopped.
Verify the~original work has stopped and~inspect the~gallery and~storage before
starting a~separate creation. None starts automatically; a~verified
completed result instead remains as~a~receipt for~acknowledgement.
An~invalid or~unreadable local intent remains blocked and~requires administrator
assistance because its original key cannot be~verified.
Before sending a~new gallery, Photos checks earlier work in~the~same local
profile. A~pending result is~checked only with~the~original site and~Admin
account; another site offers reconnection. This check never repeats creation.
For~a~completed earlier gallery, confirm \ui{Continue} before creating
the~next one. Only the~exact local completion is~retired; the~earlier gallery
and~upload target stay unchanged. The~current connection and~selected parent
are~checked again. Submitting the~same title and~parent on~the~same site
and~account instead checks the~completed result without creating a~duplicate.
An~unknown result or~failed local confirmation keeps the~saved work
for~another check.
A~confirmed receipt remains until explicit acknowledgement. The~client writes
and~checks a~complete staged receipt before exclusive publication; uncertainty
retains the~intent and~any published receipt. A~local binding to~the~original
intent prevents a~stale selection or~acknowledgement from~applying to~another
operation with~the~same gallery details; the~operation key is~not~displayed.
These checks do~not~exclude unrelated filesystem changes or~prove power-loss
durability. On~a~supported local profile, \ui{Use created gallery}
rechecks the~receipt and~gallery before selecting it~for~Watcher and~Import.
This requires separate upload approval. The~server compares the~original ID, title and~parent
with~current data. A~rename yields unknown; a~move, deletion or~unverifiable
state can~be~unavailable. A~missing or~unsafely redirected directory or~sidecar
also remains unknown; validation checks current safe files, not~their original
inode. Neither automatically starts a~replacement.
Where supported, new access is~normal and~listed with no~scheduled publication.
Database
rollback cannot undo filesystem writes. Local flushes do~not~prove recovery
after power loss; actual database, HTTPS and~Windows creation remain unqualified.
With~separate explicit consent, a~short-lived grant can~list all galleries,
including private and~unpublished ones. An~identity-only grant cannot list
them. Selecting a~gallery alone grants no~upload permission. A~separate upload
scope explicitly covers all ordinary Admin galleries, including private
and~unpublished ones. The~transient linked Watcher and~Import are~implemented
but~not~qualified; the~gallery-scoped key remains available independently.

The~installer and~updater provision the~linking key in~the~database.
The~saved website address supplies the~exact origin and~mount.
Existing explicit issuer and~key settings keep precedence. Public discovery
reads readiness. The~browser saves a~short-lived request handle in~the~session
before ordinary Admin sign-in. CSRF-protected binding leads to~explicit consent.
\ui{Approve} delivers a~one-use code to~the~verified callback for~PKCE exchange.
The~main action requests account and~gallery access, with image uploads where
supported. On~compatible servers, approve rolling access explicitly. It~lasts
seven days after the~last successful authenticated use or~refresh. WinApp
renews the~15-minute access token using macOS Keychain or~Windows Credential
Manager. Verified HTTPS and~direct \texttt{http://localhost} are~supported. Older
servers use a~temporary link lasting up~to 15~minutes.
The~owner can revoke grants in~Connected
applications. Unexchanged codes expire within 120~seconds. See
\filepath{docs/APP_LINKING_PHASE_0.md}.

The~scope-capacity migration widens four binary scope columns without changing
existing grants. A~partially completed migration retains verified short-scope
support; new wide capacity is~ready only when all four columns are~verified.
It~does not~grant new permissions. DDL is~replayable, not~atomic, and~older
runtime code may~refuse the~widened schema; no~automatic narrowing is~provided.
Each metadata-lock wait is~limited to~at~most five seconds; a~stricter existing
setting remains unchanged. A~reduced setting is~restored, but~total DDL time
is~not~limited. The~callback lock
ends before the~runner writes its~migration checkpoint.

The~separate Windows HTTPS remembrance option uses the~legacy offline scope
and~expires after 30~days or~seven days of~inactivity, whichever comes first.
WinApp can resume an~approved saved link at~startup.
\ui{Reconnect saved link} also starts this check. The~client rotates once
and~checks the~server identity. If~the~same verified server explicitly rejects
an~expired or~revoked rolling credential, WinApp discards that saved credential.
Choose \ui{Link gallery} to~approve a~new link in~the~browser. Startup leaves
approval to~you. An~uncertain
response or~storage failure blocks reuse of~the~old credential. Review server
links and~use \ui{Forget saved link} before linking again if~needed. Local forget
does not~revoke the~server grant, and~the~current session may~remain active.
The~saved \texttt{pgu\_} key remains independent. Real Windows and~power-loss
recovery have not~been qualified. Rolling links keep one saved binding per exact
issuer. The~legacy Windows
HTTPS option keeps one shared binding. Resolve pending work and~forget
the~local link before replacing a~saved account for~the~same issuer.
The~server grant can be~revoked separately. The~linked upload
path uses separate history and~an~exclusive lock by~issuer, account
and~gallery. Before sending, it~records a~pending result. After an~uncertain
transfer, inspect the~gallery and~source manually; do~not~automatically retry
or~delete the~source. An~experimental bounded server probe can~report
\texttt{present} only when matching bytes were observed during its~read;
it~does not~prove image registration. \texttt{unknown} is~not~proof of~absence
or~permission to~retry, and~the~client does not~yet use the~probe. Its~time
budget is~checked between filesystem calls and~cannot stop a~blocked call.
External filesystem changes are~not~atomically excluded. When the~current
WinApp process exits, the~OS releases its~native lock for~the~next Retry.
For older locks, close all uploaders, review the~gallery and~sources, preserve
the~recovery JSON and~archive only the~verified inactive lock. Local
filesystem and~power-loss guarantees remain unverified. Linked flight data stays
local and~does not~implicitly send AI work or~PDFs with a~saved legacy key.
Configure proxy, web-server and~PHP request limits because multipart parsing
can~consume resources before the~application checks the~request.

An~idle Watcher starts after an~explicit \ui{Start Watcher} click; while active the~same
contextual control is~\ui{Stop Watcher}. While accepted work finishes, it
opens \ui{Show progress}. After an~interruption, \ui{Review pending uploads}
opens the~original queue. Reconnect and~select the~same upload gallery before
choosing \ui{Retry}. For a~linked session, \ui{Keep files locally} closes
the~queue after workers stop and~preserves the~photos, reports and~upload
history. Once the~session is~closed, \ui{Start Watcher} is~available again.
\ui{Check folder only} performs
a~folder
dry run without uploads or~starting continuous watching. The~Watcher gear opens
a~settings sheet for~folder choice, recursion, scan interval, file stability,
simulator metadata, and~the~saved deletion setting. The~sheet requires stopping
the~watcher before
edits. \ui{Choose folder…} saves the~folder from the~main Watcher view.
\ui{Start Watcher} asks for~a~folder when one is~missing.
\ui{Save watcher settings} stores the~Watcher preferences for~the~next start.
\ui{Cancel},
Escape, or~closing the~sheet restores the~opening folder, ignore count, last
scan, and~preferences.
\ui{Sync UTC time} remains an~opt-in beside the~main actions.
Long pages and~dialogs scroll with the~mouse wheel or~scrollbar; text and~table
controls keep their own scrolling.

Manual \ui{Import} still requires preflight review and~an~explicit start.
\ui{Activity} contains the~filtered event history, retry controls, redacted
diagnostics, and~log. A~compact latest-event footer opens \ui{Activity} when
selected. Icon controls expose tooltips and~keyboard focus; the~question-mark
help
opens usage guidance. These presentation changes preserve the
existing persistence, tray, update, and~API-key safeguards.
Pillow adds an~ambient cool gradient and~opaque glass-inspired card, footer,
and~pill-button artwork. This~composited treatment simulates frosted glass
without true alpha transparency or~operating-system backdrop blur; text stays
sharp, and~input and~table surfaces stay solid and~high-contrast. It~uses
no~whole-window
opacity and~adds no~runtime dependencies. Without Pillow, readable flat ttk
surfaces remain, and~native controls and~source-mode operation, including
\texttt{--once}, stay supported.
Windows prefers the~installed Segoe UI Variable Text font, with Segoe UI
and~the~native Tk font as~fallbacks. Shared text styles preserve Windows
font smoothing; fixed-width diagnostics stay monospaced. Common control,
cache, pin and~recording icons use supersampled artwork; without Pillow,
readable native glyphs remain. The~app downloads no~fonts and~changes
no~system preferences. Before creating windows, an~unaware Windows process
requests system DPI awareness and~preserves an~existing manifest context.
This does not~add per-monitor layout reflow. Restart on~the~destination
display when moving between displays with different scaling.
The~main window remembers its~normal size, position, maximized state and
Photos tree width automatically. Minimizing, fullscreen and~tray hiding
preserve the~last normal rectangle. Restoration keeps the~window and
divider within current display bounds. These view preferences do~not
change upload configuration or~credentials.
Switching albums cancels the~old view. A~new request waits within its
original deadline for~the~existing single transport slot while earlier
network cleanup finishes; canceling the~new view also cancels that wait.
Before its~scan, \texttt{--once} verifies the~saved upload mode with Windows
Credential Manager. A~verified legacy or~unmigrated legacy profile remains
eligible; linked, first-setup, unknown or~unavailable evidence returns a~nonzero
status without scanning. Each inventory and~upload attempt and~redirect
rechecks the~mode; an~already running request may finish. This in-process
check does not~fence other processes or~protect graphical workers.
API-key requests refuse redirects to~another host, an~unapproved port, or~from
HTTPS to~HTTP. Use the~final gallery URL if a~server requires such a~redirect.
Actual Windows behavior remains unqualified.
On Windows, watched-source deletion is enabled by~default for~new settings;
an~explicit saved off choice is retained. The~Watcher settings switch controls
both linked and~API-key captures. After a~successful upload response and~local
acknowledgement, a~native handle verifies the~same file identity and~bytes
before deleting that opened source. Changed, redirected, hardlinked or~busy
files remain with a~warning. Inventory matches and~uncertain uploads do~not
authorize deletion. Other platforms, manual imports and~source ZIPs retain
their sources. The~Watcher deletion preference applies only to~watched
captures; it~does not~delete files selected for~manual Import. Flight
API-key revocation is temporarily unavailable. GUI mode protection and crash
recovery are still being implemented and are not qualified.

\subsection{Windows startup and~one running instance}

The~installed uploader starts after Windows sign-in by~default, minimized
directly into the~system tray. In~\ui{Settings > App \& integrations},
\ui{Start with Windows (minimized to tray)} saves immediately; switching it~off
removes this user's startup entry, and~updates retain that choice. Setup applies
the~preference even if~the~final application-launch checkbox is~cleared.
Source/Python runs leave Windows startup unchanged. An~idle Watcher and~manual
imports need explicit Start. An~active Watcher can~continue after normal Quit
and~restart: Exit settles accepted writers, verifies the~original session/queue
and~confirms closure of~the~old temporary Watcher key before saving a~separate
credential-free restart checkpoint. Within its~existing 24-hour lifetime,
startup restores the~exact saved gallery/account and~rechecks access before
admitting the~same queue and~recorder with fresh contextual authority.
Stop, Finish and~unlink cancel this intent; stopped capture stays stopped.
Startup waits for~mode readiness. Temporary checkpoint reads or~gallery
verification failures keep the~same intent for~bounded retry; invalid state,
changed identity/scope and~revoked access still block capture. Stop or~unlink
also cancels pending retries and~late results. Diagnostics identify the~failed
phase and~a~fixed safe reason without keys, checkpoint contents or~local paths.
A~securely saved gallery grant or~unchanged saved API key is~required.
Expired/revoked or~unsaved transient links need normal reconnection;
uncertain uploads and~forced termination retain explicit recovery.

Opening the~app again restores the~existing window and~keeps current work.
One Windows process owns the~shared app profile, including executable copies;
another Windows session must open its existing process in~that session.
A~logon launch keeps import-recovery prompts deferred until the~window opens.
If~the~tray cannot become available within five seconds, the~window appears
so~the~app remains accessible. Startup registration belongs to~the~user running
the~application; an~installer explicitly started elevated may~use that account.

\subsection{Update notice and~indicators}

The~app checks for~uploader releases at~startup and~at~most once an~hour;
\ui{Check for updates} requests a~manual check. A~validated offer appears as~a
version banner and~marked update control in~the~main header on~every screen.
\ui{Later} hides the~notice but~leaves both indicators available to~reopen it.
When tray support is~available, its~icon shows a~red update badge, its~tooltip
identifies the~offered version, and~the~first menu action names that version
and~opens the~same notice. Without tray support, the~in-app indicators remain
available. The~app does~not~set a~Windows taskbar overlay or~show
operating-system toast notifications.

A~failed network recheck preserves the~validated cached offer across restart;
the~offer is~validated again before it~is~restored. A~successful check with
no~current offer clears the~banner, header mark, and~tray badge.

Update operations automatically record their phase, stage, result and~duration
in~\path{watcher.log}. Network failures retain the~HTTP status and~typed
transport cause; transfers include known byte counts. Checkpoint, helper
handoff and~cleanup have separate diagnostic stages. Cleanup failure preserves
the~original update error. Credentials, signed download queries, response
bodies, raw exception messages and~local paths are~excluded. The~notice keeps
its~concise status; technical detail is~available in~the~ordinary log without
a~debug switch.

\ui{Copy diagnostics} includes the~retained updater failure after later
successful checks. It~also includes current-session Photos failures,
distinguishing catalogue reads, thumbnail transfer and~image decoding.
Album changes retain those facts; changing the~approved live session resets
the~Photos snapshot.
Preload failures also reach the~ordinary log, visible Log and~copied
diagnostics, distinguishing listing, cache checks, transfer and~verification.
Terminal records include processed, stored and~skipped counts. A~running
preload keeps its~failure facts across album changes within the~same approved
session. Rejected media responses show bounded cache-header decisions without
copying raw headers or~relaxing permissions and~local-storage rules.
Equivalent repeated or~split cache directives are~accepted with the~same
authorization markers and~private revalidation policy. Longer additional ages
never extend the~local 60-second cap; incompatible directives do~not~authorize
storage, and~no-store browsing remains transient.
Worker events keep their FIFO order. The~GUI yields between complete handlers
after at~most 64 messages or~an~eight-millisecond work budget, then continues
after 16~ms; an~empty queue keeps its~200~ms poll. One slow handler can~exceed
this cooperative budget. Activity history and~persistence remain immediate
while table redraws are~combined. Copied diagnostics expose numeric batch
counts and~durations for~manual measurement, without claiming screen latency.

\subsection{Windows simulator location}

\ui{Sync UTC time}, beside the~Watch folder actions, is~off by~default and~saves
its~own preference. While watching, a~separate worker checks MSFS 2020 or~2024
every 10 seconds. It~compares full simulator UTC date/time with Windows UTC;
only an~absolute difference above 60 seconds requests year, ordinal day, hour
and~minute correction, followed by~fresh readback. The~worker waits
for~a~loaded,
unpaused flight at~1x rate and~five seconds of~settled state. Menus, loading,
pause and~other rates suspend writes. Window/tray stop and~complete exit cancel
UTC work; hiding to~tray preserves it. The~selection survives stopping.
Three failed corrections with 30-second backoff suspend that connection until
reconnection. Uploads and~photo-location acquisition remain independent.

Both generations use the~same time events. The~one-based ordinal day setter
and~any effect on~Live Time/Live Weather still require live verification
in~two separately loaded flights per~available simulator/build; native mocks
do~not~establish those results. See \filepath{winapp/README.md}.
Hosted installers explicitly use x64 CPython 3.14.x. Before packaging,
the~builder verifies its~interpreter and~the~actual frozen EXE's patch version,
implementation and~pointer width through a~pre-GUI report; a~wrong or~missing
report fails preparation. The~independent application source targets 0.10.0.

The~bundled native SimConnect DLL comes unmodified from the~official
MSFS 2024 SDK Core 1.7.3. Its~84,480 bytes and~SHA256 are~documented
in~\filepath{winapp/README.md}. Static x64 and~export checks preserve
the~previous 117 named exports; live camera, flight telemetry and~UTC acceptance
in~MSFS 2020/2024 remain separate. SDK version identifies provenance because
the~DLL has~no~usable embedded file version. Microsoft SDK terms apply.

WinApp 0.3.2 automatically uses the~MSFS 2024 camera world position and~falls
back to~the~aircraft position on~bounded camera failure. MSFS 2020 uses
the~aircraft position directly through the~same SimConnect runtime. Coordinates
are~optional: simulator unavailability does~not~prevent upload. Position-source
diagnostics distinguish camera and~aircraft results. The~installer requests
application shutdown before replacing files; complete pending work before
updating. Live simulator and~upgrade acceptance must be~checked on~the~target
PC.

The~frozen updater loads Windows APIs explicitly from System32, avoiding
a~collision between the~bundled VERSION text file and~the~Windows version DLL.
When upgrading from 0.3.1, manually launch the~verified 0.3.2 installer.
The~running older app copies its own helper before installation, so~that old
helper may~still report its DLL error after replacing the~app. Future updates
started from the~corrected 0.3.2 app use the~repaired helper; verify
the~installed handoff on~Windows.

Aircraft replies are~matched by~request and~definition IDs; the~request USER
selector does~not~require a~zero reply object ID. Bounded diagnostics record
receive metadata, dispatch sequence and~specific rejected-packet reasons.
Failure reasons survive path redaction and~activity messages display one
SimConnect marker. The~object-ID filter was~a~confirmed code bug,
but~the~reported FS2020 timeout did~not~record the~returned object ID. Retest
on~the~affected PC; simulated tests do~not~prove a~live fix.

The~installed uploader checks its own stable GitHub installer version at~most
once per~hour; \ui{Check for~updates} also starts a~manual check. The~last
automatic attempt is~saved independently for~the~current user and~survives
application restarts. CMS tags do~not~determine its version. The~highest numeric
installer version requires SHA256 from the~GitHub asset or~a~matching
same-release \filepath{winapp-update.json}. Missing or~conflicting integrity
data refuses the~update; identical installers in~several CMS releases
are~accepted. Gallery credentials are~never sent to~GitHub.

Choose \ui{Update} to~download with progress and~optional cancellation and
verify the~installer. An~active Watcher finishes the~current acknowledged
photo operation, saves its~queue and~flushes the~original recording before
the~helper starts. Unresolved work postpones installation after 120~seconds;
the~game-presence observer does~not~block it. Finish a~manual import, AI task
or~report finalization first. Helper failure keeps the~application open.
After update, the~same session, folder, target and~original file boundary
continue. Screenshots created during installation remain eligible. Telemetry
keeps its~original identity and~records a~gap. Explicit Stop or~Quit cancels
continuation. Linked capture requires the~same account, gallery and~scope.
Older versions without saved running intent can~offer \ui{Resume Watcher}
for~verified unfinished work; they do~not~resume it~without that explicit
choice. Abandon and~a~replacement key are~not~required for~continuation.
Persisted settings and~credentials remain available; unsaved Settings fields
are~not~saved by~an~update.
The~helper rechecks the~installer, waits for~the~installed executable to~exit,
requests Windows UAC approval, installs and~verifies the~new version, then
restarts the~uploader as~the~original user. Installation progress remains visible.
There is~no~automatic rollback. Python/source runs offer the~verified installer
for~manual launch. A~required Windows reboot remains explicit; cancelling UAC
reopens the~existing installation. Older clients blocked waiting for~workers
need a~manual installer launch after fully closing the~old application.


\subsection{Flight recording and reports}

The~uploader uses dark glass panels, an~icon navigation rail, and~sliding
switches. Hover over icons for~their descriptions. The~header groups
PHP Gallery with its~autopilot subtitle. Settings gears sit directly beside
their gallery, watch-folder, or~flight control. The~flight gear opens a~local
sheet for automatic reports, report languages, interface language, progress,
and~the~report folder. Starting the~watcher automatically arms recording;
there is~no~separate flight-start action. The~Record flight switch pauses
and~resumes acquisition during watching, preserving the~session and~marking
the~gap in~the~track. Starting with recording off opens no~SimConnect
connection. Photo uploads continue independently. Closing settings starts
no~operation. Glass artwork uses opaque RGB pixels for reliable Windows Tk;
source mode without Pillow retains usable native controls for repair.

Watcher shows the~folder path and~\ui{Choose folder…}. Saved paths,
gallery URL, and~feature choices are restored after restart. The~interface
language uses four directly visible choices. Scrollbars appear only when
content exceeds the~viewport; helper windows and~tooltips use the~same
dark palette.

The~Windows uploader records MSFS 2020 and~2024 aircraft telemetry through
a~shared read-only provider. Recording and~report creation are enabled by
default; recording can~be opted out independently, and~the~saved language
choices initially include English, Czech, German, and~Swedish. The~bundled
ReportLab renderer and~DejaVu fonts create a~separate chart PDF for each
selected language from one analysis. Reports remain local for retry.
Unsupported signals stay unavailable rather than invented; live simulator,
aircraft, and~build acceptance remains separate for each generation.

The~recorder requests \texttt{ABSOLUTE TIME} in seconds and load factor in
\texttt{GForce}. The~\texttt{E:} prefix belongs to RPN expressions.
Photo-camera success does not establish flight recording. Copied diagnostics
show the~app version, independent recorder state, saved sample count, and last
warning. Rejected SDK operations identify the~variable, units, send ID, and
parameter index. Known SDK/HRESULT codes include a~name, explanation, and
impact;
unknown codes stay explicit. Changed failure reasons stay in session warnings.
Connection, first valid frame, and first saved sample have separate log
messages.
Missing historical samples cannot be recreated.

The~Record flight row also shows a~single compact IAS/ALT/V/S line in knots,
feet, and feet per minute. It reads the~existing recorder cache without another
SimConnect connection. Paused, stopped, unavailable, or
more-than-three-second-old
values become dashes. The~adjacent \ui{PDF} shortcut opens saved flights.

\ui{Saved flights and PDFs} lists start time, aircraft, checkpoint sample count,
local PDF count, state, and retained errors. Refresh reads checkpoints only;
Open app log uses the~existing log. \ui{Finish flight and create PDFs} creates
all four editions and~finishes accepted work and~required delivery. It~keeps
the~upload connection and~works with automatic creation off.

Selected languages render simultaneously in separate processes, one per
language (currently up~to four). Flight analysis and map backgrounds are
prepared
once; the~main application uploads only after the~complete set succeeds.
Failure keeps completed PDFs for recovery. Cancellation stops queued work,
waits for running renders and retains their finished files.

With one version, expanding a~flight shows its files directly. Multiple versions
appear as expandable groups, always numerically descending (v5, v4, v3),
regardless of column sorting. Refresh retains expanded flights/versions and
selected files. This display does not move or rename files on disk.

For a~stopped flight, \ui{Create local PDF} or \ui{Re-create local PDF} creates
a~numbered version under \filepath{reports/}. Earlier PDFs, raw samples,
and upload receipts remain intact. No key is~required, no upload/revoke occurs,
and another running flight continues. Partial reports remain incomplete; active,
unclosed, and empty recordings are~refused. Local progress/errors are~retained
in \filepath{manual-report.json}; local creation does not resolve pending
remote delivery. Cancellation retains finished editions. Shutdown/update drains
this worker. Windows layout and live acceptance remain a~separate manual check.

The~Begin/Finish actions in~Watcher and~Import use \ui{Begin flight} and~
\ui{Finish flight} when SimConnect is~available. If~it~fails, known game
processes owned by~the~same Windows account provide an~optional fallback,
without admin rights. A~Microsoft Flight Simulator process also selects flight
wording. Otherwise the~labels are~\ui{Begin game}/\ui{Finish game}, with
localized
easter eggs for~Cyberpunk 2077, the~Mass Effect trilogy, Skyrim and~Dragon Age.
Unknown or~ambiguous games keep general wording. The~background observer checks
every three seconds, even before watching starts or~with recording/UTC sync
disabled. It~opens/closes SimConnect without telemetry or~simulator writes;
process names stay local.
SimConnect probes, photo GPS, recorder connections and~UTC sync require
this account's running MSFS 2020 or~2024 process; otherwise they wait or~skip
silently. Prepar3D and~X-Plane only select flight wording through process names.
Outside a~flight context, current recording controls and~progress use neutral
session wording. The~Watcher UTC controls are~hidden while their saved
preference is~retained; flight detection restores them. Historical flight
reports keep their actual identity, and~custom game action phrases remain.
In~idle API-key mode, Begin stays visible with or~without a~saved key
or~running game and~opens the~connection editor directly. A~Photos browsing
link does not~hide it. Explicit linked upload mode offers gallery reconnection.
Active capture, pending work and~finalization retain Finish, Review or~Progress
before setup; an~active manual import retains its~own controls.
SimBrief's Load/Unload control, settings icon and~status appear only
for~a~detected
flight; hiding retains the~loaded plan. Outside a~flight context, Watcher,
Import and~progress use \ui{Abandon game} with matching confirmation and~status
text. The~existing local cleanup leaves the~game running.
The~bundled offline process catalog is~broad but~incomplete. Add exact process
names and~optional four-language phrases in~
\filepath{\%APPDATA\%/PHPGalleryUploader/game-contexts.json}, then restart;
no~rebuild is~needed. The~schema, source and~refresh instructions are~in~
\filepath{docs/WINAPP_GAME_CONTEXTS.md}.

Watcher and~Import show \ui{Gallery:} followed by~the~current display title from
the~authenticated inventory API, wrapping long names within the~available row.
The~physical folder name is~always derived from the~sanitized source title.
Saving a~title change renames the~folder and~updates descendant paths;
a~destination collision refuses the~rename without overwriting data.
Older APIs without title metadata display the~final folder segment.
Hover over the~name to~see the~full physical folder
hierarchy on~separate indented lines, from root to~gallery. Connection changes
refresh both presentations; flight records and~import history retain
the~complete identity.

The~Finish button sits below the~upload gallery during capture. Finish and
Stop keep the~connection available for another session. Manage server keys
separately in~PHP Gallery Admin. An~active linked session uses the~browser
approval for uploads. After restart the~verified saved address prefills
reconnection.

In legacy API-key mode, \ui{Abandon flight} in~Watcher, Import and~progress
offers explicit local recovery after a~gallery is~deleted or~its~key revoked.
After confirmation, workers stop and~in-flight requests settle; pending delivery
is~abandoned and~the~saved key is~removed from this PC without contacting
the~server. Recordings, photograph sources, PDFs and~receipts remain local.
It~also works with no~pending sessions. The~main action becomes
the~Begin action. A~still-valid server key needs separate revocation in~Admin.
Submitted revocation must settle first; shutdown or~configuration-save failure
keeps the~key and~recovery available for~retry.

To~upload retained PDFs, save and test a~valid gallery key, including a~new key
for~the~same gallery. In~the~report browser select a~stopped flight and~PDF
files with Ctrl/Shift, then choose \ui{Upload saved PDFs}. Confirm the~gallery
and~files;
server inventory verifies the~exact target before transfer. Existing PDFs are
sent without rendering, stopping another flight, or~revoking the~key. Separate
\filepath{upload-receipts.json} retains acknowledged checksums; retries
reconcile
server status. Old plans, bindings, and~raw data remain intact. Cancellation
retains finished editions. Protocol errors include redacted HTTP/action/type
context and~a~bounded response excerpt.
The~client checks status before upload and~requires matching checksum and~size.
If~confirmation is~uncertain or~the~local PDF changes, it~retains the~file
and~does not~mark that edition complete. An~earlier saved plan receipt records
a~past acknowledgement; it~does not~prove the~PDF still exists on~the~server.
The~selection dialog identifies a~file, not~a~hash captured at~confirmation;
the~later file checks do~not~atomically exclude other writers.

Without a~saved key the~main Begin action opens API
settings. Expand a~flight and, when present, its version to~see PDFs;
double-click opens the~default
viewer. \ui{Open folder with PDFs} opens only the~PDF folder. Filenames contain
recorded UTC, aircraft, language, and~version. A~restartable private catalog
normalizes older layouts without changing bytes or~upload identity; JSONs are
in~\filepath{reports/.metadata/}. Keep that folder with backups.

New reports use A4 landscape (297 by~210~mm), with a~navy header,
teal accents, pale background, and~white panels. Embedded regular/bold DejaVu
uses larger type: 28~pt titles, 12~pt body, 9.5~pt notes, and~9~pt plot axes.
The~overview places value cards beside the~route; full-flight charts appear
