in~pairs. Each landing has a~summary and~a~separate detail page with four plots.
Earlier PDF editions remain available; this layout changes no~measurements,
recording, transfer, or~flight completion.

Reports use value cards and~vector phase/configuration icons. Technical versions
remain metadata; simulator build/date use small header text. Rounded Y~axes and
subtle grids show feet AMSL, feet AGL on~approach, IAS knots, and~fpm with
200/1,000 minor/major spacing. Long-flight time grids use ten minutes/hours.
Route axes label coordinates, north, and~first/last captured points. Scalar
profiles bridge gaps up~to~5~s in~curve color and~5--300~s in~amber dashes;
pauses, reloads, and~longer losses remain breaks. This display interpolation
never enters landing measurements. No~runway, score, or~missing rate
is~invented.

Expanded PDF rows show page count and~local creation date/time, with version
and~language first. A~star marks legacy file modification time when creation
metadata is~unknown. Phase milestones normally fit one row. Landing profiles
retain original samples before overview thinning; fitted touchdown rate and
the~simulator V/S are~separate, with actual cadence and~partial rollout
disclosed.
The~compass compares true aircraft heading with wind-from HDG/KT, such~as
020/05, and~heading-relative components; it~does not~infer runway alignment.
Descending below 100~ft AGL latches every acquired frame through rollout until
10~s at~15~kt or~less on~the~ground. Earlier dense capture below 300~m remains;
a~climb above 500~m exits for~a~go-around. Target acquisition remains 20~Hz.
PDF requests use the~established uploader identification; gateway HTML 401/403
is~reported separately from Gallery authentication. Completion requires
server receipt IDs/checksums, not~activity alone. Fuel defaults to~kg,
uses lb~for~Boeing/PMDG and~gallons for~common light GA. Measured simulator mass
takes priority; old volume-only mass uses a~visible 0.80 kg/L estimate. Raw data
stays unchanged. Landing rollout cannot produce a~false rejected-takeoff event.

Both actions use one durable coordinator. Standalone Stop stops the~watcher,
its~owned UTC synchronizer, and~flight recorder, then drains accepted
photographs and~completes required report delivery; unrelated AI, manual-import,
and~preflight workers remain running. Stop leaves the~key configured.
Explicit \ui{Finish flight/game} in~saved API-key mode also authorizes final
revocation after required work. The~captured revoke ticket remains active
through the~server response and~is~exchanged atomically for~the~configuration
save reservation. Only the~same operation, endpoint/key and~config generation
can clear that key; stale events and~failed saves cannot claim success or~clear
a~new credential. A~running ordinary Stop must finish before a~new Finish action.
Confirmed Revoke runs the~same Stop, also stops other authenticated producers,
finishes accepted work, and~requests scoped server revocation only after
required
receipts. Progress shows phase and~elapsed time; ETA appears only when
measurable.
Failures retain tasks and~local files for recovery, with explicit local-only
resolution available. Restart restores work but~never restores Revoke consent;
a~new confirmation is~required. No~empty report is~created.

The~gallery-scoped token advertises the~optional
\texttt{flight\_reports\_v1} capability. The~JSON
\texttt{flight\_report\_status} action accepts \texttt{report\_id},
\texttt{language}, and~\texttt{sha256}; the~multipart
\texttt{flight\_report} action accepts those fields plus
\texttt{session\_id} and~the~PDF field \texttt{report}. The~server confines
writes to the~token gallery and~reuses general PDF validation, storage, and
protected viewing without adding schema. Stable identity, checksum, and~status
reconciliation make retries idempotent. Reports are separate from OFP,
inherit gallery visibility, and~contain no~raw telemetry.

\section{Adding, describing, and~organizing photographs}
\label{sec:photo-workflow}
\index{photo workflow}
\index{image metadata}
\index{photo organization}

\subsection{Choose an~upload method}
\index{upload methods}

Browser upload suits everyday additions. Select a~gallery, choose files, review
the~batch, and~follow progress while the~server validates media and~prepares
records and~thumbnails. Filesystem discovery suits large existing folder trees
or~transfers made through FTP and~hosting tools. Automation and~WebDAV suit
repeated or~mobile workflows after their scoped credentials are~configured.

In~the~right-hand Admin \ui{Upload photos} panel, paste successive screenshots
with Ctrl+V on~Windows/Linux or~Cmd+V on~macOS, choose additional files, or~drop
images into the~upload area. Each selected image appears in~a~local thumbnail
queue before submission, together with its filename and~size. Move images with
the~arrow controls, remove an~unwanted screenshot, or~clear the~selection.
The~existing \ui{Upload} button sends the~originals through the~normal upload
process once; preview tiles are not replacement upload files. This queue is~not
a~server-side draft: leaving the~panel asks before discarding unsent files,
a~failed upload retains the~selection for retry, and~successful server
acknowledgement clears it. Unsupported preview formats show a~format tile
without bypassing the~server's upload checks. The~standalone Admin upload page
and~no-JavaScript form continue to~use their previous workflow.

For~a~large event, upload in~meaningful groups. Smaller groups make errors
easier to~identify and~allow titles, ordering, and~visibility to~be~reviewed
incrementally.


% iCloud assisted import contract
\subsection{Import photos from iCloud}
Open \ui{Import from iCloud}, paste a~public Photos Link
at~\url{https://share.icloud.com/photos/} or~a~public Shared Album
at~\url{https://photos.icloud.com/shared/album/}, select the~destination
and~click \ui{Download and import from iCloud} beside the~link. This action
recognizes the~link and~starts the~existing upload form in~one step; the~footer
shows the~same action for~an~automatic source. Keep browser preparation enabled.
Gallery checks public availability, requests Apple's compatible export,
downloads it~to~temporary browser disk storage and~imports supported photos
automatically. You~do~not~need to~download or~select a~ZIP yourself. A~new
gallery must be~Private or~Unpublished; review an~existing gallery's visibility
because it~also applies to~partial imports.

The~source types use separate acquisition paths. Photos Links can~expire
or~be~revoked; Shared Album availability is~controlled by~the~owner, including
optional temporary expiry. Gallery requests compatible JPEG/PNG/GIF
representations; received JPEG/PNG/WebP/GIF images are~checked by~their actual
signature. HEIC/HEIF/DNG/RAW, MP4/MOV, Live Photo motion and~sidecars
are~counted as~unsupported rather than renamed or~silently converted. Compatible
JPEG is~not~a~preserved RAW original; capture/GPS metadata and~Apple edits
are~not~guaranteed. Apple's download protocol can~change and~a~sign-in-only
source cannot be~imported anonymously.

Automatic transfer requires a~supported browser on~HTTPS with OPFS temporary
disk storage. Known byte totals produce download progress; unknown totals show
received bytes only. The~response is~written incrementally, never held as~one
whole-archive memory buffer. The~existing ZIP worker reads bounded metadata
and~one image at~a~time, preparing and~uploading small IndexedDB-backed
batches. Stored and~Deflate are~supported; ZIP64 and~multi-disk archives fail
explicitly. Bounds include 10,000 entries, 4~MiB directory metadata, 2~GiB
expanded data and~64~MiB/the~server limit per~image. Manually selected ZIPs use
the~same incremental reader: the~64~MiB bound applies to~each image, together
with the~server limit. The~archive itself is~not~uploaded or~compared with
the~PHP request ceiling. Mixed selections preserve drawer order or~standalone
natural image order. Retry retains the~source cursor and~exact pending batch;
manual new-gallery visibility follows the~explicit form and~server. Decoders
and~thumbnails also use memory; these bounds are~not~a~total heap measurement.

\ui{Cancel import} stops acquisition and~the~active work; accepted photos
remain. Keep the~same form open and~choose \ui{Retry the~same import} after
an~uncertain result: the~disk source, cursor and~unacknowledged batch/session
identity are~retained, so~retry does not~insert duplicates. Source and~target
controls remain locked until reconciliation. Safe failure before server work
and~successful completion remove temporary sources. Closing the~tab loses
in-page retry; the~next acquisition removes abandoned unlocked source files
older than one day. No~background import or~cross-session restart is~promised.

If~the~automatic path is~unavailable, open \ui{Manual export and details}
and~choose \ui{Open iCloud for manual export}. On~Apple, choose \ui{Most
Compatible} when offered, download the~ZIP or~supported images and~select them
in~the~existing picker. Clear the~source for~ordinary local uploads. The~link
is~cleared from its input after recognition and~retained only in~this form's
memory; bearer links, Apple cookies and~credentials are~never sent to~Gallery
or~stored in~drafts. Sign in~to~Apple only on~its official page.

\subsection{Write useful photo titles}
\index{photo title}
\index{captions}
\index{alternative text}

A~title identifies the~photograph quickly. A~description or~caption can~explain
people, place, activity, technical context, or~a~story outside the~frame. Useful
examples include:

\begin{itemize}
  \item \emph{Charles Bridge before sunrise};
  \item \emph{Arrival of~the~first train at~the~restored station};
  \item \emph{Family group in~the~garden, June 1998};
  \item \emph{Final approach during the~Saturday display}.
\end{itemize}

Meaningful text improves search, accessibility, later identification,
and~the~experience of~readers who want more than a~visual grid. File names
can~remain operational details when public display settings favor curated
titles.

\subsection{Use tags as~a~second organization layer}
\index{tags!practical use}
\index{tagging strategy}

Create a~small vocabulary before adding many near-duplicate tags. Prefer one
stable term, such as~\emph{aircraft}, over several accidental variants. Tags
can~represent subjects, people, places, techniques, equipment, seasons,
or~editorial status.

For~a~travel archive, galleries can~represent trips and~tags can~represent
recurring themes. For~a~family archive, galleries can~represent years and~events
while tags identify people. For~a~professional portfolio, galleries
can~represent clients and~tags can~identify deliverable type, location,
and~specialty.

Tag entry fields can~suggest existing tags while you type. Reusing a~suggestion
avoids accidental spelling variants and~keeps tag landing pages coherent.
The~suggestion is~an~editing aid only; the~ordinary save action still decides
which tags are~stored for~the~gallery or~photograph.

\subsection{Arrange the~viewing order}
\index{photo ordering}
\index{storytelling}

Ordering turns a~collection into a~sequence. Start with an~inviting image,
establish place or~context, vary wide and~close views, keep closely related
details together, and~finish with a~natural conclusion. Chronological order
works well for~events, while visual rhythm can~suit portfolios and~exhibitions.

The~public reorder controls help administrators adjust visible cards. Photo
reordering on~a~public page is~also scoped to~the~current visible pagination
slice and~saves through the~authenticated image-order endpoint. When Picture
Manager is~active, dragging selected photo cards over a~visible child-gallery
card changes the~gesture from~reordering to~a~validated move into~that child
gallery. Pagination can~divide a~large collection, so~review transitions around
page boundaries as~well as~the~first page.

\subsection{Review EXIF and~location information}
\index{EXIF!user review}
\index{location privacy}

EXIF can~enrich a~photograph with capture date, camera, lens, exposure, and~GPS
coordinates. This information supports maps, date suggestions, duplicate
evidence, and~technical context. Review GPS visibility before publishing
photographs made at~homes, schools, private workplaces, or~sensitive natural
locations.

\section{Publishing, sharing, and~audience scenarios}
\label{sec:audiences}
\index{publishing workflow}
\index{audiences}
\index{use cases}

\subsection{Public portfolio}
\index{portfolio}

A~public portfolio benefits from a~small number of~strong top-level galleries,
consistent covers, concise descriptions, and~careful ordering. Use tags
for~specialties that cross projects. Keep source archives in~private
or~unpublished branches and~copy selected work into public presentation
galleries where the~workflow calls for~separate curation.

Test the~portfolio on~a~phone, a~high-density display, and~a~slower connection.
Progressive sharpening is~the~default and~safe fallback for~photo cards
in~the~open gallery; responsive selection remains a~supported alternative.

\subsection{Private family archive}
\index{family archive}

Organize by~year, family branch, or~event. Use password-protected parent
galleries to~establish an~understandable private area, then place related
descendants within it. Add names and~dates while memories are~available. Scanned
photographs benefit especially from human descriptions because embedded camera
metadata may~be~absent.

Share the~protected address and~password through an~appropriate private channel.
Explain that recipients can~navigate child galleries without receiving
an~administrator account. Periodically review whether old links and~passwords
still serve the~intended audience.

\subsection{Client delivery}
\index{client gallery}
\index{proofing workflow}

Create one protected gallery per~client or~assignment. Upload proofs, organize
them into useful groups, and~use descriptions to~explain selection or~download
expectations. Voting can~support lightweight preference gathering when enabled
for~that gallery. A~final delivery gallery can~contain approved files
in~the~intended order.

Share links offer another controlled entry method where configured. Test
the~exact client journey in~a~private browser window before sending the~address.

\subsection{Event and~community gallery}
\index{event gallery}

An~event gallery can~begin unpublished while photographs are~arriving. Use child
galleries for~days, stages, teams, locations, or~activities. Publish the~parent
when the~first useful selection is~ready, then add later groups without changing
the~familiar public address.

Public voting can~highlight audience favorites. Tags connect recurring
participants or~subjects. Downloads provide a~convenient collection transfer
when the~event policy allows it.

\subsection{Travel and~location archive}
\index{travel archive}
\index{maps!travel use}

Country, region, city, and~trip galleries provide natural navigation. Date
ranges establish chronology, while GPS maps create a~geographical reading path.
Review coordinates for~accommodation and~private locations. A~descriptive
introduction can~explain the~route, purpose, and~historical context of~a~trip.

\subsection{Historical and~institutional archive}
\index{historical archive}
\index{institutional archive}

Historical collections benefit from careful source notes, uncertain-date
wording, names, locations, and~provenance. Use descriptions to~distinguish
confirmed facts from family or~institutional recollection. Tags can~connect
people, buildings, organizations, and~periods across many galleries.

Create backups before large metadata projects and~export information through
available tools when planning external preservation. The~gallery can~provide
an~accessible presentation layer while original preservation practices continue
alongside it.

\section{Understanding your gallery data}
\label{sec:user-data}
\index{data ownership}
\index{database!for gallery owners}
\index{filesystem!for gallery owners}

Gallery owners interact with two cooperating stores: ordinary files
and~a~database. Understanding their roles makes backup, migration,
and~troubleshooting easier.

\subsection{What lives in~gallery folders}

The~gallery folders contain the~photographs and~folder hierarchy, so~the~core
collection remains recognizable through FTP, a~hosting file manager, a~backup
tool, or~a~local copy. Moving or~renaming files outside the~application
can~require discovery or~synchronization so~the~database learns the~new state.

\subsection{What lives in~the~database}

The~database remembers titles, descriptions, dates, visibility, passwords
and~access configuration, public paths, ordering, tags, votes, image dimensions,
EXIF indexes, checksums, thumbnail records, administrator accounts, settings,
audit information, and~workflow state.\footnote{Password values use hashes
or~protected configuration appropriate to~their purpose. A~database export still
contains sensitive operational information and~deserves secure storage.}

The~database makes search and~administration fast. It~also allows a~file to~have
a~friendly title, rich description, tags, a~chosen order, and~access rules
without changing the~original image bytes.

The~\texttt{public\_content\_widgets} table persists authored titles, Markdown,
page scopes, seven in-flow zones, floating anchors and normalized coordinates,
publication state, ordering and optimistic revision numbers. An~upgrade creates
an~empty table and displays no~widgets until an~administrator explicitly
publishes one. Published records are rendered on eligible public home/gallery
pages; drafts and disabled records are never included in~public HTML. Back
up~these records together with the~rest of~the~database.

\subsection{How users benefit from the~database}
\index{database!benefits}

Everyday benefits include:

\begin{itemize}
  \item searching words across gallery and~photo descriptions;
  \item opening a~gallery through a~stable public path;
  \item displaying photographs in~a~curated order;
  \item remembering tags and~visitor votes;
  \item protecting a~gallery with inherited access rules;
  \item finding exact duplicate content through stored checksums;
  \item preparing suitable thumbnails without re-reading every source file
  on~every page;
  \item recording migrations and~maintenance decisions with an~audit trail.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Backup as~one complete set}

A~complete backup includes the~gallery tree, database export, local
configuration, and~installation-owned custom assets. Give these parts the~same
backup date so~they describe one coherent state. Store a~copy away from the~web
server and~test restoration in~a~separate location.

\section{Everyday ownership and~care}
\label{sec:daily-care}
\index{daily administration}
\index{monthly maintenance}
\index{ownership checklist}

\subsection{After every important upload}

Review the~destination gallery, photo count, ordering, titles, visibility,
and~thumbnail appearance. Open several photographs in~the~lightbox and~check
the~gallery anonymously. For~a~protected collection, repeat the~password
or~share-link journey from a~fresh private session.

\subsection{Monthly review}

A~monthly maintenance check should include:

\begin{enumerate}
  \item Review System Health and~pending migrations.
  \item Check recent Admin audit events.
  \item Inspect available application updates and~their release information.
  \item Confirm scheduled or~manual backups completed.
  \item Open representative public and~protected galleries.
  \item Review thumbnail maintenance when source files or~rendering settings
  changed substantially.
  \item Remove or~revoke credentials and~share methods that have completed their
  purpose.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Before a~major public launch}
\index{launch checklist}

Check titles, descriptions, cover images, spelling, mobile layout, privacy, GPS
exposure, downloads, maps, voting, search results, and~contact expectations.
Test through a~slow network profile if~the~audience may~use mobile connections.
Create a~backup immediately before the~launch state.

\subsection{When another person helps administer}

Give each administrator an~individual account where the~account model supports
the~intended workflow. Explain gallery scope, privacy expectations, naming
conventions, tag vocabulary, backup responsibilities, and~deletion procedures.
Individual accounts improve accountability in~audit records and~keep personal
duplicate-review ledgers separate.

\section{Public visitor experience}
\label{sec:public}
\index{public gallery}
\index{routing}

\subsection{What a~visitor sees}
\index{visitor experience}

A~visitor opens the~site at~the~home page or~follows a~direct link to~a~gallery
or~photograph. The~page presents the~site's name, navigation, gallery title,
description, breadcrumbs, tags, child galleries, and~photographs that
the~visitor is~allowed to~see. Protected content asks for~the~appropriate access
step before revealing private material.

Each gallery has~a~public address that can~be~bookmarked or~shared. A~direct
photo address opens the~gallery context and~leads the~visitor to~that
photograph. Meaningful titles and~stable public paths help links remain
understandable in~messages, bookmarks, and~search results.

\subsubsection{Local history and~new content}

Small icons follow each gallery title, including when the~title wraps. A~bright
dotted circle means unseen, a~part-filled circle means partially visited,
and~a~quiet checked
circle means seen. A~physical branch becomes seen only after its~own page
and~all
navigable descendants have been opened. Attached Smart Galleries count as~their
own items; opening one does~not mark its~physical sources visited. Covers,
search results and~background requests do~not count as~visits.
Click the~progress icon beside a~card title to~mark that gallery
and~its~available
descendants unvisited again. This includes attached Smart Galleries, whose
state is~shared across their placements, and~preserves New-content markers.
Opening a~gallery again records a~new genuine visit.

The~separate sparkle means newly available content since this browser's first
observation or~last acknowledgement. It~can accompany any visit state. Opening
a~parent does~not acknowledge new content inside unopened descendants. Cosmetic
changes and~deletion alone do~not create additions.

The~header bell opens a~small translucent panel immediately beside it, without
dimming the~page. Follow a~gallery link to~open its~content.
\emph{Mark all~new content as~read} appears beside unread notifications
and~acknowledges only their content, preserving gallery visit states.
The~separate footer action defaults to~\emph{Mark all~galleries as~seen}.
Only when every available gallery is~seen does~it~offer
\emph{Mark all~galleries as~unseen}. Both directions preserve content markers
and~work without a~confirmation dialog; neither dismisses notifications.
Escape or~a~click outside closes the~panel. Narrow-screen actions retain
comfortable touch targets. The~panel matches the~PDF/music applets with a~Theme
glass surface, small accent icons and~a~compact outlined read-all action;
the~independent visit toggle remains below the~list.
When there are~no~unread notifications, the~entire news section disappears,
leaving only the~compact visit action, Close and~the~local-history note.
The~panel grows with its~content up~to~the~actual viewport height, including
browser zoom. Only then does its~compact list scroll; heading and~actions stay
visible. There is~no~fixed item-count threshold.

History stays in~this browser's local storage, independently for~each
installation. It~does~not synchronize between devices or~send visitor history
to~the~server. If~storage is~blocked, the~current tab remains usable.
Unavailable
content evidence omits notifications and~their action without a~technical
warning or~a~false empty-state claim. The~visit toggle remains usable.
Authenticated administrators never see this feature, including in~anonymous
preview; after logout they receive the~ordinary visitor interface. Any~future
non-Admin Viewer account uses the~same separate visitor identity boundary.

\subsubsection{Typical visit}

One common visitor path is:

\begin{enumerate}
  \item Open the~home page and~choose an~interesting gallery cover.
  \item Read the~gallery introduction and~follow child galleries or~tags.
  \item Scroll through smaller preview images.
  \item Open one photograph in~the~large viewer.
  \item Move through nearby photographs with buttons, keys, a~strip,
  or~the~selected viewing mode.
  \item Open a~map, vote, search, or~download when the~gallery offers those
  actions.
  \item Use breadcrumbs to~return to~a~broader collection.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Gallery hierarchy and~pagination}
\index{pagination}
\index{gallery hierarchy}

\subsubsection{Direct content and~stable ordering}

A~gallery can~show smaller galleries first and~photographs afterward.
For~example, \emph{Italy 2026} can~contain \emph{Rome}, \emph{Florence},
and~\emph{Venice}, while also showing a~few overview photographs directly
on~the~Italy page.

Pagination divides a~long collection into manageable pages. This helps the~first
page appear promptly and~keeps scrolling comfortable. A~small exhibition
may~look best on~one page. A~wedding with 1,500 photographs will usually benefit
from pages or~child galleries. The~large photo viewer can~continue through
the~wider gallery sequence while loading additional information
as~needed.\footnote{Pagination changes how many cards appear at~once. It~leaves
the~underlying photographs and~their order intact.}

Long public listings include a~floating \ui{Top} control after the~visitor
has~scrolled into the~gallery listing. It~uses smooth browser scrolling,
disappears outside the~active listing, and~stays hidden while the~lightbox
or~fullscreen viewer is~open so~it~does not~compete with photo controls.

\subsection{Configurable public content widgets}
\index{public content widgets}

Open \ui{Appearance} and \ui{Widgets} in~the~Admin Zone to~create independent
content panels such as \emph{Partner galleries}, announcements or~useful links.
Each widget has a~title, a~Markdown text body, a~publication state, page scope,
appearance and~a~saved position. Create or~edit a~draft, choose \ui{Preview
without saving}, and then explicitly \ui{Save}. The~editor also supports
duplication as~an~unpublished draft, numeric ordering, disabling and~confirmed
deletion. A~concurrent edit from another Admin tab is rejected rather than
silently overwriting a~newer revision.

Use headings, paragraphs, emphasis, lists and~links with your own visible
captions in~one Markdown body. Both safe internal gallery URLs and~external
HTTP(S) links are supported. Executable URL schemes, arbitrary HTML, scripts
and~remote embeds are not permitted. Images or~logo uploads are not part
of~the~current widget editor. Widget authors must not place privileged
information in~public content.

Select the \emph{Homepage}, \emph{Gallery pages}, or~\emph{Homepage and
galleries} scope. In~page mode, the~available zones are top/bottom of~public
content, left/right content rails, immediately before/after the~homepage gallery
grid, and~the~global footer. A~homepage-only grid position on~a~gallery page
falls back to~the~bottom of~the~main content for an~all-pages widget. Side rails
remain part of~the~document layout and~stack after the~main content on~narrow
screens; the~navigation header and~hero are not widget targets.

In~\emph{Floating} mode choose one of~eight viewport anchors or~set custom X/Y
coordinates from 0 to~1000. The~published widget may become a~fixed, dismissible
panel on~a~sufficiently large desktop viewport. Up~to~two panels are enhanced
at~once; unsafe overlaps, small screens, zoom or~insufficient height retain
the~same accessible article in~normal document flow. The~visitor's \ui{Close}
hides the~panel only on~that page view and~does not update the~administrator's
publication settings. With~JavaScript disabled, authored content and~links
remain in~the~initial HTML exactly once.

On public pages, floating placement is recalculated when protected controls or
widget content change size, including an expanded Gallery toolbar and loaded
fonts. Unsafe placement returns the same article to normal flow.

The~protected Home/Gallery preview uses the~real Theme layout and~saved widget
order while keeping public scripts disabled. If Home has~no gallery cards,
an~after-grid draft remains ahead of~content-bottom and~floating zones; a~new
side rail follows the~same responsive stacking behavior as~the public layout.
In~the~separate Admin placement map, drag with mouse, touch or~pen, click,
or~use arrow keys to~set custom coordinates. Shift with~an~arrow makes a~larger
step; \ui{Reset floating position} restores a~valid bottom-right preset.
Desktop/tablet/mobile selectors and~the~content preview do~not save the~draft.
The~position map remains advisory. Use the~actual public page for final visual
verification after saving.

The~EN/CS/DE/SV Admin interface strings are translated, but authored titles
and~Markdown remain in~their selected source language without automatic content
translation. Theme/Custom CSS can target \texttt{.public-content-widget}
or~a~stable \texttt{data-public-widget-id} selector. Widget editing itself never
stores arbitrary CSS or~JavaScript.


\subsection{Search, tags, and~navigation}
\index{search}
\index{tags}

Search and~pagination use compact translucent controls in~the~selected Theme,
with a~32~px desktop search bar.
The~compact suggestion list preselects the~highest-ranked result as~results
arrive. Enter opens it; Up/Down or~pointer movement selects another result.
An~explicit choice follows that result when later phases reorder the~list.
Editing the~query resets selection to~its new top match and~immediately
disables old results. Long subtitles are~truncated; mobile rows retain
44~px touch targets. Escape clears the~query and~closes suggestions.
The~current page has~a~subtle accent tint and~line; keyboard focus remains
visible. On~narrow screens, the~search scope moves below the~field and~touch
targets grow. A~small switch selects the~search scope. Search scope and~page
navigation work as~before.

Search can~look across the~whole public site or~stay within the~current gallery
and~its children. A~visitor looking at~\emph{Family archive} can~search only
that branch for~a~person's name, while a~visitor on~the~home page can~search
across every public collection. The~current search service begins at~two visible
query characters, returns a~bounded mixture of~gallery and~photo results,
and~ranks stronger title/name/tag matches ahead of~weaker text matches.
The~default request returns up~to~12 merged results and~the~service clamps
a~request to~at~most~30 results.

Searchable evidence includes public gallery/photo titles and~descriptions, image
filenames, gallery and~photo tags and~their public descriptions, active
maintained-language content where its schema is~available, and~internal AI
searchable text when AI metadata is~ready. Access filtering happens as~part
of~the~query: private, inaccessible, or~non-public image content is~not~returned
merely because its text matches. A~branch search also constrains the~listing
context to~that gallery subtree rather than~searching the~whole catalog
and~hiding unrelated matches afterward.

Tags provide another route through the~archive. A~\emph{night photography} tag
can~connect Prague, London, and~Tokyo even when those photographs live
in~separate travel galleries. Breadcrumbs show the~current place
in~the~hierarchy, and~favorite shortcuts place important galleries directly
in~the~site header.

\section{Media and~gallery administration}
\label{sec:admin}
\index{administration}

\subsection{Gallery inventory and~quick controls}
\index{gallery!quick feature controls}
The~Admin Galleries workspace shows direct photographs, descendant photographs,
and~subgallery counts. Compact icons show each gallery's own feature settings;
hover or~focus to~identify the~state. Click Maps, File names, Voting, or~Picture
Game to~save the~selected gallery immediately. Maps cycle through inherited,
on, and~off; inherited descendants follow their parent. An~inherited OFF is~a
muted icon; an~explicit OFF is~crossed out and~turns orange when it~overrides
an~inherited ON. The~background icon
shows state only. Picture Game requires voting: enabling the~game enables voting,
and~disabling voting disables the~game. Stored votes and~game results remain
intact. Use bulk actions for~an~explicit selection of~galleries. If~a~save
cannot be~confirmed, check the~reloaded state before trying again.

Quick password controls affect only the~current gallery's~own password. Enter
a~new password to~enable or~replace it, or~remove the~own password. Share-token
protection, ancestor protection, visibility, and~unrelated preferences stay
in~force. An~outdated revision is~refused. Ordinary forms remain available
without JavaScript; panel actions complete in~place and~keep the~panel open.

On~the~public home, signed-in~administrators can~create a~root gallery.
The~first-gallery prompt requires a~confirmed empty catalog. Creation groups
content and~location/visibility, with secondary settings disclosed; source
language, tags, SimBrief drafts, and~remembered defaults remain available.

\subsubsection{Upload preferences and~mobile connections}
Open \ui{Settings > Uploads} for~format/rename preferences, browser workers
and~limits, ZIP batch tuning, thumbnail source chunks, and~mobile WebDAV
connections. Related limits are~normalized together; size controls display MB.
Enable the~legacy upload-menu preference only if~those original links
are~useful. Hidden links do~not~disable the~existing routes.

Mobile actions refresh their own workspace. Copy a~newly issued one-time
password immediately. If~the~following connection-list refresh fails,
the~connection remains saved and~the~password stays visible in~that response;
retry the~list separately. Disabled mobile uploads do~not~read the~connection
inventory.

\subsubsection{Appearance, language, and~custom stylesheets}
Appearance combines grouped controls with a~live preview and~a~bounded
pointer/keyboard resizer on~wide screens. Pane size is~a~local browser
preference. Site images groups branding/background tools; Layout separates
shortcuts, grids, card details, and~viewer defaults. Language has~Settings,
Design, Editor, and~Diagnostics. The~design preview uses that form's~offered
languages and~public default; changing a~sample does not~save a~language
preference.

All~five Appearance subsections use compact translucent groups and~question-mark
help beside their controls. The~shared live preview and~its browser-local
divider
remain available. Reload and~Back/Forward preserve the~active subsection,
including
existing deep links. GPS pin/background, display-all-tags, tag scrollbar
and~header
transparency switches save immediately and~individually with factory-default
tooltips. Other colors, text, selections and~numbers remain drafts for~\ui{Save
theme}.
An~unverified or~refused switch write locks that control and~blocks shared-form
replay until passive reload, without automatic retry. Ordinary form saving
remains available without JavaScript.

The~Language subsections use compact groups, switches and~contextual
question-mark
help. Admin language, public default and~the~viewer language list save
immediately
and~independently, without submitting other Theme drafts. After changing
the~Admin
language, use \ui{Reload interface} to~refresh labels. An~unverified save locks
its
group and~blocks shared-form replay until reload; it~is~not~retried
automatically.
All~language selectors show bundled flags. The~active subsection survives reload
and~browser Back/Forward navigation through the~page URL. Switcher design
remains
a~draft applied with \ui{Save theme}; JSON/import and~diagnostics retain
explicit
actions. Native form submission remains available without JavaScript.

Keep the~installed custom stylesheet by~leaving replacement empty. Explicitly
choose a~preset or~upload a~valid CSS file to~replace it; an~upload takes
precedence. Replacement/reset verifies storage first and~restores prior bytes
if~saving the~marker fails. If~recovery is~incomplete, retain the~available copy
and~follow the~bounded warning before retrying. Deployment preserves
the~installation-owned \filepath{public/assets/custom.css}.

\subsubsection{Card orientation and~operational status}
Vertical cards place the~photograph above the~description; horizontal cards
place it~beside the~description. The~normal orientation migration preserves
existing appearance across saved settings, physical and~Smart Galleries,
sidecars, imported metadata, and~Trash. Back up~the~database, galleries,
and~Trash together. If~conversion is~interrupted, resolve the~reported problem
and~retry; document markers prevent double conversion. Fresh installations keep
the~vertical default. Tags remain complete without JavaScript; browser expansion
and~scrolling also work on~gallery cards.

Public search defaults on~only without a~saved choice and~when its feature
is~enabled. Updates refresh versions, notes, and~API status together; automatic
metadata checks use the~saved interval (five minutes by~default), and~existing
background jobs continue independently of~new discovery. OurAirports refresh
uses weekly freshness and~hourly failure backoff. Trash shows remaining
retention and~safe purge availability. Presentation-schema problems also require
action in~System Health.

Optional SimBrief loading sits beside Begin/Finish flight, with an adjacent
identity gear. Enter one pilot ID or username; only a successful OFP fetch saves
the identity. Loading is explicit and never required for recording. The staged
plan is consumed by the next session; plans attached during recording belong
only to that session. No previous flight plan is automatically reused.
Saved flights also offer loading the~latest OFP directly into the~selected
flight,
or attaching an archived SimBrief JSON/XML export. A PDF-only OFP cannot supply
machine-readable dispatch data. Create a new local PDF version afterwards;
existing PDFs and delivery receipts remain intact.
Reports include recorded OpenStreetMap route maps independently of SimBrief.
An attached OFP adds a separate planned route, planned passengers and ramp fuel.
These values never replace measured telemetry. Coordinates come from the OFP
navlog and are retained in a bounded, AIRAC-scoped local SQLite evidence cache;
ambiguous names are not guessed, and no external navigation subscription is
required. Displayed OSM tiles are cached for at least seven days, with
attribution
and a bounded cache. Missing network tiles leave the route visible on a pale
background. Mercator maps clamp polar latitudes; original coordinates remain
in~the~saved raw recording. Online tile rendering sends viewport tile
coordinates
and~the~app User-Agent to~OSM, without Gallery keys or~SimBrief identity.

Both maps fit the~complete recorded and~planned route with a~small margin.
The~overview omits the~redundant note after the~phase timeline, avoiding
a~separate page containing only that note.
Detached jump-and-return position blocks are omitted from~both maps
with a~visible notice; raw samples remain unchanged. Distant starting
or~ending segments and~relocations without a~local return stay visible.

The~main screen offers Load SimBrief or~Unload SimBrief. Unload clears
the~staged/current OFP and~its route, including the~current session snapshot;
older sessions and~existing PDFs remain intact. Loading/finalization blocks
unloading. Stopped flights no~longer show their route on~the~main control.
The~SimBrief identity sheet uses the~dark app theme, validates input inline
and~accepts Enter/Escape. A~checked loaded state and~airport pair identify
the~staged/current flight's accepted plan; failed reloads preserve it. Flight
settings, history, progress and~confirmation dialogs open near their owner.
Click saved-flight headings to~toggle ascending/descending order. Starts include
seconds; times sort by~UTC instant, counts numerically and~names without case.
Created/page columns use the~newest PDF time/total known pages for~flight
groups.
Expanded flights and~multi-selection survive sorting and~refresh. Missing pypdf
leaves page counts unknown while saved PDFs remain openable; missing ReportLab
shows dependency-repair instructions for~new rendering. Install requirements
into the~same Python used to~launch the~source app. Update progress closes
itself
only after matching installed version and~successful restart; reboot
instructions
and~errors remain visible until dismissed.

Report map backgrounds are~composed from native tiles at~least 300 dpi
at~their final PDF size. Recorded tracks and~purple dashed OFP routes stay
vector; the~overview uses a~light background and~the~last map full contrast.
New PDF names may include the~OFP flight number and~departure/arrival ICAO.
Existing editions, names and~receipts remain preserved. Two planning pages
show passengers, cargo/payload, registration, runways/alternate, cruise
altitude,
cost index, distance/time, scheduled UTC departure/arrival, fuel components
and~planned weights. Missing fields remain unknown. Timed navlog altitude
and~fuel can~appear beside recorded curves: detected takeoff and~captured
simulator clocks align them even during simulator-rate changes. Known kg/lb
units are~converted explicitly; recording gaps break the~comparison. Missing
time anchors allow a~labelled comparison at~recorded route positions instead:
at~least two ordered OFP fixes must match captured legs within 10 NM. Gaps and~
position jumps still break lines; no~takeoff time is~invented. Missing matching
data suppresses comparison with a~visible explanation. OFP values remain planned
evidence; no~actual passengers, delay score or~unrecorded fuel is~inferred.
Earlier OFP snapshots remain readable; explicitly reload/import them to~obtain
new fields they did~not~retain. Map requests stay limited to~the~displayed
viewport with a~45-second network budget and~stop on~the~first provider failure.

An~attached OFP adds plan-versus-actual statistics in~all four editions while
retaining maps, profiles and~landing detail. The~final repeated-header
flightplan keeps every OFP point with planned/actual elapsed time, simulator
UTC, remaining fuel, pressure height, TAS, GS, wind and~passage distance.
Intermediate matches require ordered passages within 2 NM; airport rows use
detected takeoff/landing within 10 NM. Missing matches remain unknown.
Summary and~phase tables distinguish airborne/block time, distance, fuel,
weights and~estimated delays. More initial fuel is~separate from savings.

Vector deviation profiles show time, fuel onboard, load-adjusted net fuel use,
pressure height and~route separation at~projected OFP positions. These planning
estimates differ from the~ordered final flightplan matches. Time-weighted
histograms and~bias, MAE, RMSE and~absolute-error P95 use original saved
samples,
independently of~graph thinning. P95 uses fixed 1-second, 0.1-NM and~25-ft bins;
the~overflow bin gives a~lower bound. Amber map segments exceed the~descriptive
5 NM band; height uses ±300 ft. These are~comparison bands, not~operating limits
or~pilot scores. Phase averages include IAS/TAS/GS, V/S and~track-relative wind.

New optional pressure-height, parking-brake and~Zulu-calendar observations
remain separate from host UTC. Height differences require both airport
transition references. Estimated OUT/IN requires sustained parking/motion
evidence and~does not~identify a~stand. Full flight totals require matched
airports, one captured flight and~complete relevant data. Gaps, fuel increases,
incompatible dates and~legacy missing fields suppress affected claims. Old
recordings remain readable; explicit OFP reload/import or~a~new recording
supplies new evidence. Earlier PDFs and~receipts remain preserved.

Compact analysis panels pair plan, actual and~difference in~one cell and~group
short boundary phases with climb, descent or~arrival. Original statistics stay
preserved. Green indicates lower time/net consumption or~more remaining fuel;
red indicates the~opposite. Speed whiskers show observed min/max with weighted
means; phase range bars compare planned and~actual totals. These are~descriptive
ranges, not~confidence intervals. Verified duplicate airport rows may share
the~same observed endpoint without suppressing valid summaries. Missing values
use a~dash. Legacy AMSL height is~marked with an~asterisk and~never supplies
pressure-height errors; absent simulator UTC leaves an~explicit planned-only
UTC column. The~missing-channel context is~explained once.

The~main SimBrief control marks its loaded plan; saved-flight details show
their own OFP attachment. Finish uses available loaded OFP for~the~current
or~sole pending flight. Manual generation uses it~for~the~selected saved flight
and~creates four new editions without confirmation. With no~loaded OFP, existing
evidence stays unchanged; neither action fetches a~plan or~asks for~an~identity.
Partial recordings retain incomplete facts. Success updates status without
an~OK message box and~closes progress after durable completion/key cleanup.
Errors stay visible; earlier PDFs remain intact.

The~update notice resizes to~fit its wrapped status text and~complete button row
when background checks change the~message. Text and~action-row backgrounds match
the~window; the~primary \ui{Update} action remains distinct.

The~independent uploader source targets 0.8.0. A~separately requested installer
build will place the~installer and~matching generated
\filepath{winapp-update.json} under \filepath{winapp/dist/0.8.0/}. The~installer
and~metadata belong to~the~same hosted release. This build check
does~not~replace
acceptance testing of~the~installed Windows app or~the~simulator. Existing
version folders, including 0.4.0, remain preserved.

Develop integration compares the~complete installer inputs with current
\texttt{main}. Changed WinApp runtime, assets, dependencies or~build inputs
require a~version strictly greater than main's; tests, README and~instructions
are~excluded. Several integrations may~keep the~same upcoming version while
main retains its older version. After publication advances main, another runtime
change needs another bump. Admission runs before FULL and~again before protected
publication; release continues to~trust qualified develop.

\subsection{Admin workspace}

After signing in, an~administrator sees private controls for~the~dashboard,
galleries, photographs, theme, tags, maintenance, updates, logs, and~account
settings. Many edits open in~the~right-side panel, leaving the~gallery visible
while one item is~changed and~saved.\footnote{A~full Admin page remains
available for~workflows that need more space or~when browser enhancement
is~unavailable.}

On~public pages, anonymous preview/Exit preview, Admin, Test run, Updates,
and~Logout use compact transparent outlined icon buttons: 32~pixels square
with 16-pixel glyphs. Localized names appear as~native hover tooltips and~remain
available to~screen readers, including disabled Test run and~pending Updates.
Anonymous Admin login uses the~same treatment, including in~anonymous previews.
Show gallery map uses a~pin icon with its localized tooltip and~accessible name.
Header and~gallery-map targets grow to~44~pixels on~narrow or~touch screens.
Language choices remain governed by~Theme settings.

The~public gallery itself also exposes contextual Admin controls while a~normal
administrator session is~active. Gallery and~photo pencil controls open the~same
full editors in~the~side panel. Compact gallery removal uses the~recoverable
trash workflow when Trash is~enabled; disabling Trash returns future gallery
removals to~the~legacy permanent subtree-deletion service. Compact photo removal
is~deliberately labeled \ui{Remove photo ... from CMS}: that quick public-page
action removes the~catalog image row and~does not~run the~full source-file
deletion service. Use the~normal photo/bulk deletion workflow when the~original
file and~generated derivatives should also be~deleted. Anonymous-preview mode
hides these contextual Admin controls.

The~Admin gallery table can~filter rows by~visibility: all, unpublished, public,
or~private. Filtering is~a~browser-side view aid, not~an~access-policy change.
A~row hidden by~the~filter is~also unchecked, and~the~master selection
is~cleared, so~a~hidden stale checkbox cannot accidentally enter the~next bulk
action. The~summary reports the~displayed/eligible gallery count.

The~gallery list searches names locally while typing, ignoring case and~accents.
Longer words allow one insertion, deletion, replacement or~adjacent swap;
approximate results are~labelled. Search includes collapsed descendants and
shows their ancestors as~non-selectable context. The~status filter still applies
to~matches. Clearing search restores the~saved collapse state. Only matching
visible galleries are~selected by~\ui{Select displayed}; hidden selections
are~cleared. Reordering is~unavailable while search or~a~status filter is~active.
The~bulk action chooser groups available operations with icons; choosing an~action
does not~execute it~until \ui{Apply}. Removal of~a~selected gallery also affects
its~subgalleries, as~the~confirmation explains.

The~gallery tree supports per-branch expand/collapse plus \ui{Collapse all}
and~\ui{Expand all}. It~remembers which gallery branches the~administrator
collapsed. The~browser sends the~collapsed gallery IDs through an~authenticated
CSRF-protected action and~the~application stores that UI state, so~returning
to~the~Admin tree keeps the~same branches closed rather than~expanding
the~entire hierarchy again. Descendants hidden by~a~collapsed branch are~also
unchecked before bulk work. Reordering refreshes the~tree controls when
a~gallery gains or~loses children.

\subsection{Reliable in-place side-panel changes}
\index{administration!side-panel mutations}
\index{side panel!mutation completion}

When JavaScript is~available, a~persistent action started in~the~Admin
right-side panel completes in~that panel. The~drawer stays mounted and~open,
the~address in~the~browser remains unchanged, and~the~page is~not~reloaded
merely to~show the~result. This applies to~gallery create/edit/move/delete,
image upload/edit/delete/move/reorder/visibility/NSFW/cover actions,
scan/import, EXIF date suggestions, thumbnail creation, Media Renamer, Metadata
Organizer, Duplicate Photo Detector review/deletion actions, Upload Automation
credentials, Gallery Migration target pulls, tag edits, and~Smart Gallery
create/edit/placement/detach/duplicate/delete actions. A~standalone Admin page
and~ordinary POST/redirect behavior remain available when JavaScript is~disabled
or~the~route is~opened directly.

Only the~newest panel-open request may~replace its contents; closing or~opening
another item cancels ownership by~an~older response. Keyboard focus remains
inside the~open drawer and~returns to~the~control that opened it. Unsaved
ordinary text can~be~held in~bounded browser memory for~the~current page
and~restored when the~same form returns. Passwords, tokens, API keys, file
inputs, operation identities, and~other private values are~excluded, and~nothing
is~persisted in~browser storage.

The~saved database/filesystem state remains authoritative. After persistence,
the~server describes the~changed entity, the~panel fragment that may~need
re-rendering, and~every affected public context. Stable gallery, image, tag,
and~Smart Gallery identifiers are~used instead of~assuming that the~old URL
still names the~saved object. This matters after a~gallery folder/slug rename,
gallery reparenting, or~tag slug change: the~application can~fetch the~new
canonical render source while leaving the~visible browser address untouched.
Source and~destination galleries are~both described for~moves; old/new parents
and~the~moved gallery are~described for~reparenting; Smart Gallery placement
changes describe every affected root or~physical parent context.

A~successful fragment request is~not~by~itself proof that the~change is~visible.
The~refreshed server-rendered HTML must satisfy an~operation-specific observable
condition where one exists. Examples include gallery/card presence or~absence
and~full counts, exact effective visibility, saved gallery revision, image
absence/visibility/NSFW state, gallery-wide image revision for~off-page changes,
cover image identity, persisted image order, tag identity, and~Smart Gallery
placement/order. Pagination-aware checks accept a~directly visible matching card
before using aggregate counts, while an~off-page result still waits
for~the~authoritative total to~converge. Thumbnail cache repair
is~the~intentional exception: it~refreshes affected contexts but~has no~stable
database-backed public-HTML condition.

Independent render requests can~briefly return stale data on~shared hosting. PHP
Gallery therefore performs one centralized bounded retry sequence only after
the~mutation itself succeeded and~the~declared condition is~not~yet observable.
Each completion receives a~new operation generation; a~newer mutation aborts
or~supersedes older fetches, retry wakeups, and~panel responses for~the~same
context. Structurally valid but~stale HTML is~verified before replacement,
so~an~older response cannot overwrite a~newer visible state. Refreshes bypass
browser caches and~preserve applicable pagination, sorting, language, filter,
active-tab, drawer-scroll, and~page-scroll state.

If persistence succeeds but~the~refreshed view cannot be~verified within
the~bounded retry budget, the~panel reports a~synchronization problem and~keeps
the~saved server change. It~does not~misreport the~mutation as~failed, silently
reload the~page, navigate away, or~rewrite browser history. Authentication,
authorization, CSRF, schema readiness, path safety, and~validation failures
return an~expected structured error to~the~enhanced workflow
and~are~not~retried. Dynamically refreshed controls remain active because
the~panel uses lifecycle-safe delegated handling rather than bindings that
disappear after fragment replacement.

Browser-assisted create/upload has~additional safeguards. Selecting browser
processing is~an~explicit execution choice: a~missing browser capability
or~failed worker preparation stops before persistence instead of~silently
switching to~server thumbnail generation. PHP validates that every required
prepared thumbnail size/format is~present before it~stores originals. Once
browser persistence starts, direct-page fallback metadata is~never interpreted
as~permission to~replay the~classic create/upload request, preventing duplicate
galleries and~duplicate photographs. The~configured ZIP batch size is~a~soft
packing target: one atomic photograph package that exceeds that target
can~travel alone in~one batch, but~the~effective PHP upload ceiling remains
a~hard limit. Windows upload automation may~send the~complete JPEG+WebP
compatibility matrix; a~modern WebP-only server accepts required WebP variants
and~safely skips valid extra JPEG variants, while malformed, mismatched,
unknown-size, or~unsupported thumbnail payloads remain hard failures.

\subsection{Admin navigation}

The~desktop Admin menu starts expanded, with its existing function groups
and~icons. Use the~double-chevron button at~the~top to~collapse it~to~an~icon
rail
or~expand it~again. The~choice is~remembered in~this browser for~this
installation; it~is~not~stored in~the~database or~shared with other browsers.
Clearing browser storage restores the~expanded default. If~storage is~blocked,
the~choice lasts only for~the~current page. Both desktop states scroll with
the~page, without a~separate menu scrollbar. Scroll the~page to~reach the~last
account actions.

On~narrow screens, a~labelled hamburger opens the~same groups below its button.
The~panel scrolls independently and~overlays the~page, so~you can~reach content
without first scrolling past the~whole menu. Close it~with the~button, Escape,
a~click outside, or~by~choosing a~destination. Keyboard focus returns
to~the~button after Escape. Moving focus outside also closes it. Opening another
page
starts with the~mobile menu closed; returning to~desktop restores its separate
expanded or~collapsed choice. Without JavaScript, all~grouped links remain
visible.

\subsection{Central Settings hub}
\index{Settings!central hub}

The~Admin navigation includes a~central \textbf{Settings} page for~site-wide
configuration. General now groups the~website address, site name, Admin/public
languages and~navigation preferences. The~other sections cover Public
appearance, Content, Media and~browsing, Uploads and~automation, Privacy
and~diagnostics, and~Advanced. Existing Website address links still reach
General. Stable section links and~ordinary forms remain available without
JavaScript.

With JavaScript, sections load when opened and~save in~place. A~sticky save bar
shows changed settings and~offers saving or~reverting the~current draft.
Advanced summaries remain collapsed until needed. Address or~Admin-language
changes offer an~explicit link to~the~new address or~language. The~dashboard
first displays its shell, then loads Overview totals or~the~Galleries list
as~needed; failed reads offer a~retry. Overview counts indexed originals rather
than scanning files or~resolving gallery covers.

A~Spotlight-style field searches settings as~you type. It~matches names,
descriptions, categories, and~technical names, including controls that are~owned
by~specialist Admin pages. Selecting a~result opens the~relevant section
or~specialist page and~focuses the~matching control. Arrow keys, Enter, Escape,
and~the~clear button can~be~used without leaving the~keyboard.

Frequently used global settings can~be~edited directly from the~hub, including
the~site name, public language, URL rewriting, public search where available,
thumbnail rendering, the~global EXIF/GPS display default, and~development
diagnostics. Other settings remain on~their specialist pages when they need more
context, credentials, file uploads, destructive actions, or~larger editors.
Examples include Theme layout, gallery-scoped upload API keys, telemetry,
account security, Google/OpenAI configuration, custom CSS, branding, language
packs, and~database maintenance.

The~hub does not~flatten existing inheritance. Tag-page layout can~continue
inheriting the~global grid and~card design, while gallery-specific card,
lightbox, and~EXIF/GPS choices stay with the~gallery that owns them.
Context-specific gallery and~photo values are~not~turned into global settings.

Secrets are~never shown in~search results or~summaries. Passwords, tokens, API
credentials, and~similar values appear only as~safe states such as~Configured,
Not~configured, or~a~link to~their specialist page.

\subsubsection{Guided Setup Wizard}
\index{Settings!Setup Wizard}
\index{Setup Wizard}

Choose \ui{Setup Wizard} at~the~top of~\ui{Settings} to~start or~resume a~guided
configuration draft. The~wizard follows the~same eight sections as~the~hub.
Short subsections keep related choices together; \ui{Advanced} and~\ui{Expert
and~specialist settings} reveal less common options. With JavaScript disabled,
all subsection content remains readable and~normal form navigation still works.

Each supported setting has~an~explanation and~an~example. Keep its inclusion
checkbox selected to~stage a~value, or~clear it~to~preserve the~original
setting. \ui{Skip} preserves the~whole section. \ui{Next}, \ui{Back},
and~subsection navigation do~not~save application settings. Supported Theme
choices share the~existing live preview; a~preview is~temporary and~does
not~change the~public site.

The~wizard supports safe central Settings values, basic Theme colors and~layout,
grid/card and~hero-tag options, bounded upload and~telemetry preferences,
thumbnail warm-up, SEO guard, Gallery Trash, automatic updates, and~Scheduled
Maintenance preferences. Credentials, API-key actions, uploaded branding, raw
CSS, gallery-storage relocation, and~destructive maintenance remain
informational. Their specialist pages are~not~opened from the~draft; leave
the~wizard before using those workflows. Per-gallery overrides remain unchanged.

The~final review shows proposed changes first. Expand the~unchanged details
or~use \ui{Show all settings} to~inspect preserved and~informational items.
Select the~approval checkbox and~apply only after reviewing the~summary.
\ui{Cancel} or~\ui{Restart} discards the~draft. A~draft belongs to~the~signed-in
administrator and~the~current browser session; it~is~not~a~shared configuration
document.

Saving validates the~current settings again. If~another session changed
an~affected value, the~wizard refuses to~overwrite it. Required database storage
must be~verified; missing or~unknown storage blocks the~affected save.
Application and~telemetry rows use a~transaction, while website-address changes
have a~reversible configuration write for~failure recovery. Trash and~Scheduled
Maintenance preferences are~saved through their existing owners as~coherent
groups. Secrets are~never placed in~the~draft, summary, or~wizard log.

\subsubsection{Correcting the~website address}
\index{Settings!website address}
\index{configuration!base URL}

Open \ui{Settings > General} to~correct the~public installation URL after setup.
Enter an~absolute HTTP or~HTTPS address such as~\url{https://example.com}.
Include a~path only when the~gallery is~actually installed in~that subdirectory.
If~hosting detection added an~unwanted directory, remove it~from this field
and~save.

The~save changes only the~top-level \codeword{base_url} string
in~\filepath{config.php}; it~does not~create or~change a~database setting. Other
configuration values, comments, and~formatting are~preserved. Both the~file
and~its containing directory must be~writable. The~application rejects addresses
with credentials, query parameters, or~fragments, and~refuses to~rewrite
duplicate or~computed configuration values. Edit a~custom configuration manually
when it~cannot be~updated safely.

After a~successful save, the~browser opens the~same Settings section at~the~new
address. Subsequent requests use the~updated configuration. This setting
corrects generated addresses; it~does not~move gallery files or~configure DNS
and~web-server routing.

\subsection{Tag metadata administration}
\index{tags!administration}
\index{tags!metadata}

The editor uses compact translucent groups for the tag catalog, selected tag
and usage references. Groups fit beside one another on wide screens and stack
on narrow screens. The arrow beside the catalog heading jumps to the editor
below sticky navigation. Filter and sorting choices switch immediately through
the native GET form. Apply remains available without JavaScript. A question mark
beside each field explains its meaning in place;
Escape or an outside click closes the help. Review content edits and use
\ui{Save tag}. Music tags retain their internal identifiers and song references
without public tag-page actions.

\ui{Images} and \ui{Galleries} filter direct assignments over the same visual
tag vocabulary. A shared tag can appear in both; its edit still affects every
assignment. Counts and usage sorting follow the selected scope. \ui{All tags}
includes unassigned tags. \ui{Documents} is a disabled placeholder for future
PDF tagging; direct requests explain that document editing is not yet available.

\ui{Admin > Edit tags} maintains the shared tag vocabulary. Selecting a tag
exposes its canonical lowercase name, stable identifier and description. For
visual tags the identifier forms the public tag URL and the description can
appear on that page; music tags use internal metadata. Usage references link
to the related public gallery, photo editor or song editor. Counts in the
catalog follow the selected usage filter rather than always combining scopes.

Renaming a~tag or~changing its slug updates the~shared tag record, so~every
gallery/photo assignment continues to~reference that one reusable tag. Names
and~slugs are~normalized to~the~safe tag convention on~save and~a~duplicate slug
is~rejected. Deleting a~tag is~different: the~application transactionally
detaches it~from both galleries and~photographs and~then removes the~tag record.
Use \ui{View public tag} to~review the~landing page and~\ui{Configure tag
display} to~open the~Theme controls for~tag-page/card and~hero-tag presentation.

\subsection{Feature switches}
\index{feature switches}
\index{Features!Admin}

The~specialized destinations for~public live search, lightbox browsing,
Smart Galleries, gallery PDF documents and~gallery music use compact,
translucent controls. Central Settings shares this treatment across categories;
Smart Gallery editors retain it~inside the~side panel. Preference switches keep
their native keyboard operation, with larger tap targets on~narrow screens.
Settings question marks open help beside the~control; Escape or~an~outside
click closes it. Each specialist retains its existing Save workflow; catalog
row selections remain ordinary selection boxes.

Dashboard \ui{Maintenance > Content and~display} uses a~denser layout:
one toolbar and~compact groups flow into one to~three columns according
to~available width, retaining each group's own height. Groups match Features:
quiet outlines, translucent surfaces, tinted headings with a~left accent
and~thin
row separators. Question marks retain
the~explanations; counts and~warnings stay visible. Switches save immediately
without confirmation or~a~Save button; their tooltips state the~factory default.
Saving, success and~failure remain visible. Unverified completion requires
a~state reload instead of~replaying the~write. Without JavaScript, native help
disclosures and~the~existing Save/POST forms remain available.

This is~the~default pattern for~new or~revised Admin preference interfaces,
specified in~\path{docs/ADMIN_DESIGN.md}. Use switches with immediate saving,
compact headed groups and~one to~three independently sized columns.
The~reference
uses a~preferred column width of~300 CSS pixels, 38-pixel heading strips
and~26~by~14-pixel switch tracks; narrow/touch interaction targets are~at~least
44 pixels high. A~setting with its own configuration page has~a~clickable name
and~a~trailing settings arrow leading to~the~same destination. Name, switch
and~question mark remain independent navigation, preference and~help controls.
Apply the~pattern as~each owning interface is~edited; older forms may~still
await
migration.

\ui{Appearance > Features} presents optional application features in~compact
grouped tables. Whole groups flow into one, two or~three columns according
to~the~available workspace width, reading down each column before the~next.
Groups keep their own heights and~are~never split between columns; narrow
pages use one column. Switches sit directly before feature names and~save
immediately, without a~second confirmation or~Save action.
The~tinted group header switches
every editable feature in~that group; a~mixed switch indicates that only some
are~configured on. Linked feature names open their specialized settings where
available. The~plain \ui{?} opens contextual description, configured/effective
state, dependencies and~technical details; Escape or~an~outside click closes it.

Each row shows its factory value and~whether the~configured preference differs.
Factory means the~explicitly declared new-installation default, otherwise
the~registry default; it~can~differ from an~upgrade compatibility default.
\ui{Reset to~factory defaults} above the~table immediately saves all editable
switches to~those factory values. Feature data and~specialized preferences
are~preserved. A configured switch can~remain on~while a~disabled dependency
makes the~feature unavailable; enabling that dependency restores availability.

Saving, saved and~failure feedback stays visible. A group write can~partially
succeed if~a~domain owner refuses a~later setting; the~table then displays
the~authoritative persisted state. If~a~response is~lost, \ui{Reload state}
reads the~server before permitting another change and~never repeats
the~uncertain write. Without JavaScript, the~complete form retains an~explicit
Save action and~factory reset uses a~normal authenticated POST.

A~disabled feature is~hidden from~normal navigation and~its guarded routes
refuse the~feature workflow. Disabling a~switch does~not~delete the~feature's
code, photographs, database records, credentials, or~configuration. Re-enabling
it therefore restores the~normal entry points subject to~the~same schema,
access, and~configuration checks as~before. Examples of~switchable features
include public search, lightbox browsing modes, Picture Manager, Inline
Administration on~public pages, Smart Galleries, voting, Picture Game,
downloads, gallery and~flight maps, navigation data, SimBrief, OpenAI
assistance, local AI metadata, API uploads, mobile WebDAV, gallery migration,
Media Renamer, and~anonymous telemetry.

\subsection{Gallery management}

Gallery administration covers the~full life of~a~collection: creation,
discovery, naming, description, dates, tags, privacy, passwords, branding,
covers, layout, ordering, moving, and~eventual deletion. A~child gallery
can~inherit useful choices from its parent. For~example, every gallery inside
a~private family branch can~follow the~parent's protection, and~an~individual
child can~use its own presentation choice where the~editor offers an~override.
The~bulk gallery screen provides corresponding multi-gallery operations where
the~same change is~appropriate for~several collections at~once.

Gallery descriptions can~present safe external links written as~ordinary
Markdown links, \codeword{[link=URL]label[/link]},
\codeword{[url=URL]label[/url]}, or~the~short forms whose visible text is~also
the~target. Only HTTP and~HTTPS targets are~linked; other schemes remain inert
text. Recognized services use bundled local brand symbols. For~other public
hosts, saving the~gallery can~retrieve a~small validated favicon into the~local
gallery cache. Retrieval is~hostname-deduplicated, bounded by~size, time,
redirect, and~retry limits, rejects private or~reserved network destinations,
and~never occurs while a~visitor renders the~public description. If~the~cache
or~its database table is~unavailable, the~link remains usable without an~icon.

Link targets retain underscores and~query parameters. Emphasis in~labels
and~literal inline code remain intact, and~links stay in~the~surrounding
paragraph.

When clean public-path storage is~available, \ui{Regenerate clean public paths}
rebuilds the~stored gallery and~image paths from~the~current hierarchy
and~reports separate gallery/image counts. Reordering and~selected editor saves
also refresh paths when their changes require it. This maintenance action
is~useful after hierarchy repair, migration, or~older imports; it~does not~turn
URL rewriting on~by~itself, so~query-string routing remains the~fallback when
rewriting is~disabled or~unsupported by~the~host.

\subsection{Metadata organizer}
\index{metadata organizer}
\index{EXIF!date organizer}
\index{gallery!automatic organization}

The~gallery editor includes an~\ui{Organizer} workflow for~turning a~flat set
of~direct photographs into date-based child galleries. The~current
implementation groups by~EXIF capture date already stored in~the~image database.
Location-based secondary grouping is~reserved for~future use
and~is~not~an~active organizer strategy in~Version~\version{}.

Choose minimum and~maximum EXIF dates, then build a~preview. Previewing reads
database metadata in~small batches and~does not~scan, rename, or~move source
files. The~draft shows the~number of~direct photographs, matching candidates,
proposed child galleries, and~photographs ignored because they have~no~usable
EXIF date or~fall outside the~chosen range. A~minimum date is~particularly
useful for~excluding photographs from~a~camera whose clock was~unset.

For~each accepted calendar date, the~proposed child folder name is~the~sanitized
source title. Its~display title uses the~gallery date-display formatter
and~falls back to~a~human-readable day/month/year value if~that formatter cannot
supply a~label. Before proposing a~new child, the~organizer looks
for~an~existing direct child with the~same organizer title and~reuses it. This
makes a~repeated preview/apply cycle append to~the~same daily destination
instead of~creating another equivalent day gallery.

Applying the~draft requires a~separate confirmation. PHP Gallery creates
or~reuses the~proposed date child galleries and~moves matching photographs
through the~same physical move service used by~the~ordinary bulk image mover.
Originals and~known generated derivatives therefore move together with
the~database ownership update. Large applies are~split into bounded
browser-driven requests instead of~one long PHP request. Review the~draft
and~maintain a~backup before reorganizing a~large gallery.

\subsection{Media renamer}
\index{Media Renamer}
\index{filenames!renaming}
\index{media maintenance}

Choose the~scope, set the~filename pattern, then build the~preview before
confirming any physical rename. The~compact gallery list supports local search
without changing checked selections; it~is~shown for~the~checked-gallery scope.
For~a~single gallery, use the~dropdown. Question-mark controls reveal detailed
help. The~preview shows summary counts and~expandable gallery groups; open each
group to~review its~old-to-new mappings and~statuses before applying. Search
terms are~combined with AND regardless of~their order; enclose a~phrase
in~double quotes to~match its~word order exactly. Visibility filters refresh
the~gallery selector without generating a~rename plan. Build the~plan explicitly
with Preview rename plan. Empty galleries occupy compact summary rows; galleries
with file mappings expand within a~bounded result list. A~visible progress bar
accompanies requests: list refresh and~preview show indeterminate activity,
while batched checks and~renames report measured progress.

\ui{Galleries > Media renamer}, and~the~renamer panel available
for~an~individual gallery, can~normalize physical image filenames from~gallery
context and~the~current photo order. This is~a~filesystem operation,
not~a~cosmetic title change, so~the~tool always begins with a~dry-run plan.
The~preview lists each old and~proposed filename and~marks files that already
match, are~missing, would~collide, or~must be~skipped by~a~safety rule.

Only direct image files inside the~selected gallery folder are~eligible. Missing
sources, paths outside the~gallery, and~unsafe targets are~not~renamed.
If~the~preferred target name is~already occupied but~a~safe deterministic suffix
can~resolve the~collision, the~preview shows that adjustment. Applying a~plan
requires checking the~review confirmation beside the~preview. This is~the~single
confirmation for~gallery and~site-wide applies, enforced by~the~browser and
the~server; no~additional blocking dialog follows.

The~default pattern is~\codeword{\{gallery_path\}_\{seq4\}}. A~custom pattern
may~use \codeword{\{gallery_path\}}, \codeword{\{gallery_title\}},
\codeword{\{photo_title\}}, \codeword{\{old_name\}}, \codeword{\{old_stem\}},
\codeword{\{image_id\}}, and~sequence placeholders \codeword{\{seq\}} through
\codeword{\{seq5\}}. The~numbered sequence variants pad to~two, three, four,
or~five digits. Textual components are~normalized to~lowercase filesystem-safe
ASCII-style underscore stems, the~source extension is~preserved in~sanitized
form, and~an~empty custom pattern falls back to~the~default.

After a~successful physical rename, PHP Gallery keeps the~database row
and~public path references aligned, updates derived titles where the~renamer
owns them, moves or~invalidates generated derivatives as~appropriate,
and~removes stale cached ZIP archive records that refer to~the~old source
identity. Derivative cleanup warnings do~not~justify reverting an~otherwise
completed source rename because reproducible derivatives can~be~generated again.
For~site-wide selections, the~browser advances the~work in~bounded portions
and~shows the~result for~each gallery.

Gallery and~site-wide applies use requests of~at~most 15 images. The~bar and
completed/total counter advance only after server confirmation; an~incomplete
batch stops without reporting completion. Settings drafts remain in~place.
Progress uses the~Admin language. The~renamer's own controls remain accessible
on~narrow screens without the~settings Save footer covering them.
Exclusively owned JPG/WebP thumbnails move through temporary staging and~retain
their exact bytes. They become available under new names only after the~new
database identity owns them. Shared, unverified or~locked cache files retain
explicit invalidation/warning handling; originals never acquire another
image's cached pixels. Results count preserved thumbnails and~removed stale
derivatives separately in~one contextual status message. The~compact retry
action reloads existing thumbnails.

Temporary staging uses a~short internal filename independent of~the~original
name, so~valid long source names remain usable during a~rename. Final filenames
keep the~readable naming pattern. Sequence placeholders count the~complete
gallery order, including photographs outside the~current batch. Changing
the~gallery title preserves those positions; later uploads continue their
numbering after the~existing photographs.

\subsection{Image management}
\label{sec:media}
\index{images}
\index{uploads}

Photographs can~be~uploaded in~the~browser, copied into folders and~discovered,
received through configured automation, transferred from another compatible
installation, or~added through a~scoped mobile workflow. After a~photograph
arrives, the~gallery records its size and~available camera information
and~prepares the~smaller display copies used throughout the~site.

The~individual photo editor currently covers title, description,
maintained-language variants, visibility, sort order, tags, NSFW / 18+ state,
the~private editorial rating used by~Smart Gallery rules, optional per-photo
responsive-thumbnail bounds, and~read-only EXIF/GPS details. Where local AI
metadata exists, the~editor shows the~generated model/source, searchable
internal text, and~raw metadata JSON separately from~the~public caption. OpenAI
controls insert reviewable drafts into editable text fields and~do~not~replace
saved editorial text automatically.

Bulk image operations are intentionally narrower than the~individual editor
and~perform server-side ownership checks again. Current bulk actions include:

\begin{itemize}
  \item move selected photographs to~an~existing gallery;
  \item create a~new destination gallery and~move the~selection into it, with
  cleanup of~an~empty newly created destination if~the~move fails before files
  are~committed;
  \item delete selected photograph records together with their owned originals
  and~known generated derivatives through the~normal deletion service;
  \item set a~selected photograph as~the~gallery title/cover image;
  \item change photo visibility among the~supported draft/public/private states;
  \item turn per-photo NSFW protection on~or~off when its schema is~known;
  \item create thumbnails for~the~selected photographs.
\end{itemize}

Photo ordering can~be~changed by~dragging the~row handle, and~the~gallery
can~be~sorted by~filename or~EXIF capture date. Photos without a~capture date
