storage. Each central music asset counts once, including unassigned assets;
unmigrated local originals count separately. Smart playlists add no~copy.
Generated PDF pages already count as~photographs. Indexes, locks, conversion
state, unindexed files, Trash and~other caches are~outside these media totals.
Unreadable indexes or~unsafe/missing files produce partial-accounting warnings
without repair. Passive pages read cached results; attachment mutations mark
them stale when the~advisory revision write succeeds. After upgrading from
photo-only snapshots, apply the~accounting migration and run Update all once.
Attachment uploads, replacements and deletions maintain category totals from
committed metadata; repeat updates do~not reopen attachment files or~indexes.
External filesystem changes require explicit accounting invalidation before
Update all; see \codeword{docs/GALLERY\_PDF.md}.
With durable thumbnail metadata, ordinary photos are~accounted for~during
the~initial database pass without additional photo-batch requests. Only DNG
display masters retain bounded file checks. Installations without thumbnail
metadata retain the~full bounded filesystem fallback; a~failed required
thumbnail
aggregate preserves the~last successful snapshot rather than reporting zero.
For performance diagnostics, export the~latest Admin log event
\codeword{storage.refresh\_all.completed}. Its~run ID links progress
checkpoints;
the~summary includes wall/phase times, request counts, media totals, observed
file
calls and~read bytes, inspection source counts and~bounded per-table ANALYZE
times.
File-call counts are~application observations, not~physical disk I/O. Private
paths
and~workflow capabilities are~excluded; a~logging failure does~not~fail
the~update.
The final inspection records separate times for~schema and~migration reads,
source discovery and~reads, SQL-literal extraction, identifier indexing
and~matching,
cleanup counts, legacy findings, report assembly, thumbnail distribution
and~saving.
Read byte counts and~table/column comparison counts expose the~workload;
nested times overlap. Source references use a~fresh whole-word index per file
and~SQL literal, preserving production/test separation and~same-literal
evidence.
Unusual identifiers retain regex matching. No~persistent analysis cache is~used.
Required photo-query errors and~prematurely empty derivative batches fail
the~file stage and~preserve the~last successful snapshot. Later stages continue
with an~overall partial result; a~query failure never means completed scanning.

\index{storage statistics}
\index{Complete report}
\index{gallery report}

Detailed \ui{Storage statistics} are~calculated only when an~administrator
requests them, so~the~normal dashboard does~not~perform a~full
derivative-filesystem scan on~every visit. The~Files view separates indexed
source photographs from~generated JPEG/WebP thumbnails and~DNG browser-display
masters, shows source file-type and~size distributions, largest galleries
and~sources, unknown sizes, and~scan warnings. A~cached snapshot is~marked stale
when gallery data changes. Refreshing the~snapshot scans expected generated
files in~small browser-driven batches. The~same area links to~database storage
details and~database maintenance.

The~Database tab reads information-schema storage statistics separately
from~the~filesystem scan. It~distinguishes Gallery-owned tables from~the~whole
selected database where possible and~shows the~largest tables rather
than~forcing a~full table inspection into the~ordinary dashboard request.
The~\ui{Update all} action refreshes the~file scan, updates database-table
estimates through bounded \codeword{ANALYZE TABLE} requests, and~then runs
the~read-only database inspection. Progress is~kept in~a~server-owned workflow,
and~the~selected view refreshes in~place when~the~steps finish; repeating
the~action during the~same administrator's active workflow resumes its~saved
stage. Partial failures are~reported by~stage and~written to~the~Admin log.
This~action does~not~remove data, repair the~schema, optimize tables, or~execute
the~Maintenance Center plan.

\ui{Maintenance > Complete report} builds one self-contained HTML report
covering storage, database structure and~size, gallery structure, image and~EXIF
statistics, GPS overview, telemetry, operational logs, feature/settings state,
and~runtime diagnostics. Image and~generated-media work is~processed in~bounded
browser-driven batches so~the~report remains practical on~shared hosting.
Transient timeout, rate-limit, and~server failures are~retried with~short
increasing delays, while high-cardinality EXIF and~GPS summaries remain bounded
to~control session-storage and~memory use on~large libraries. The~selected
telemetry period affects only the~telemetry section; image statistics always
cover the~complete image library. GPS place summaries use local approximate
clustering and~offline reference data; probable simulator or~game screenshots
are~excluded from~real-world place frequency where the~available evidence
supports that classification.

The~completed report is~returned to~the~browser as~a~download and~is~not~saved
as~a~report file on~the~server. To~keep a~PDF copy, open the~generated HTML
and~use the~browser's Print or~Save as~PDF function; print styling is~included
in~the~report. Treat any exported report as~administrative data because
it~can~contain detailed operational and~collection statistics.

\subsection{Telemetry and~privacy controls}
\index{telemetry}
\index{privacy!telemetry}

The~compact workspace separates \ui{Overview}, \ui{Collection and~privacy},
\ui{History and~maintenance}, and~\ui{Reports}. Its~query URL preserves
the~selected tab across reload and~browser history. Each preference saves
independently after a~change; native forms retain explicit Save without
JavaScript. Question marks explain collection, privacy, limits and~defaults.
An~unverified save locks its~own form and~offers a~passive reload without
retrying the~write. Segmented HTML reports open in~a~new tab; Settings and~Logs
are~separate destinations. Rollup/purge remains an~explicit action, with
technical results in~a~disclosure.
Report links display the~standalone HTML document directly in~the~new tab,
which remains available for~reading.

In~Overview, click a~ranked photo name to~open its~public detail, or~a~gallery
name to~open that gallery. Existing access checks still apply. Six small daily
bar charts accompany the~totals. Each chart has~its~own scale; hovering shows
the~date and~exact value, and~\ui{Daily detail} provides the~same values
in~a~keyboard-accessible table. Missing or~expired days remain gaps. Retention
may leave totals without daily detail. Image bytes include photographs
and~thumbnails, excluding downloads. Unavailable public-path metadata leaves
ranking names readable without links.
The~small photo icon uses the~same preview as~the~gallery Images editor:
hover or~keyboard focus reveals it, clicking pins it, and~Escape closes it.
The~bounded thumbnail loads only when the~preview is~opened.

The~optional \ui{Telemetry} area records privacy-controlled operational
measurements and~presents them as~Admin reports. Available controls cover
the~master telemetry switch, public usage measurements, performance, cache
and~database events, honoring Do~Not~Track, excluding administrators,
the~maximum counted photo-view duration, and~retention for~raw, hourly,
and~daily data. Reports include measures such as~anonymous sessions, page views,
photograph opens and~capped view time, client errors, measured media bytes,
browser mix, and~cache/database activity where those categories are~enabled.

The~telemetry subsystem is~designed not~to~store raw IP addresses, raw browser
user-agent strings, raw referrer URLs, names, email addresses, account
identifiers, request bodies, or~exact visitor locations. Exports contain
aggregated anonymous telemetry and~session hashes rather than~raw visitor
identifiers. Maintenance rolls up~and~purges telemetry according
to~the~configured retention policy, and~the~Admin screen can~export a~standalone
HTML report for~offline review. Review the~privacy controls before enabling
collection on~a~public site.

SQL diagnostics have their own retention: seven days by~default, configurable
from one to~90 days. This does not~shorten hourly or~daily usage history.
Maintenance compacts historical daily summaries one day at~a~time while
preserving
counts, durations, photo identity and~bot segments. Browser, OS, viewport,
country
and~referrer details are~neutralized for~known daily metric families; browser
mixes
continue to~come from sessions. Unknown metrics retain their dimensions.
The~database migration preserves measurements and~rebuilds aggregate indexes
around short physical keys; plan temporary disk space and~a~DDL maintenance
window. Deleted data pages become reusable; allocated database size may~require
manual physical optimization. Actual savings depend on~the~stored data.

Scheduled telemetry is~eligible every five minutes, with a~ten-second normal
slice and~up~to~eight batches of~5000 expired rows per~table (40000 rows).
Tables alternate by~batch and~the~first table rotates between slices. The~shared
clock may~reduce actual progress. Maintenance Center keeps its four-second UI
slice. Successful public visits to~home, gallery and~smart-gallery pages
dispatch
a~private telemetry worker after rendering. Shared throttling admits work about
every five minutes when traffic exists, independently of~thumbnail windows.
The~visitor never waits for~retention SQL and~receives no~maintenance token.
External cron is~optional; without incoming requests, work waits for~a~later
visit.
Preview, media and~failed responses cannot trigger work. Telemetry OFF
and~central
maintenance ownership remain respected; retention ages are~unchanged.

\subsection{Runtime diagnostics, integrity, and~gallery benchmark}
\index{Runtime diagnostics}
\index{integrity checking}
\index{gallery benchmark}

\ui{Runtime diagnostics} provides a~copyable environment and~support view
for~PHP, database-readiness classes, hosting limits, GD, Imagick/ImageMagick,
and~relevant media-format support. It~also exposes the~DNG conversion policy
described earlier. The~readiness panels separate presentation/reporting
dependencies, authentication/security dependencies, and~mutation-sensitive
dependencies so~an~administrator can~distinguish an~optional missing capability
from~a~condition that must~block a~write.

The~same bounded model reports gallery edit-revision readiness and~unresolved
image moves. A~missing or~unknown required schema is~an~action state and~stops
the~affected mutation before target files or~rows change. Pending-move summaries
contain only capped operation/gallery identities and~safe state categories; use
the~documented command-line recovery workflow for~an~explicit operation rather
than~expecting the~diagnostics page to~move files.

The~separate \ui{Development diagnostics} switch is~administrator-only. When
enabled for~a~signed-in administrator it~adds viewer instrumentation
for~preload, cache, memory, network, and~frame-timing work
in~the~public/lightbox/fullscreen experience. It~does not~turn those diagnostics
on~for~ordinary anonymous visitors. Keep it~off during normal ownership unless
you~are~investigating rendering or~performance behavior; the~gallery benchmark
also depends on~this development-diagnostics context.

The~lightbox DEV dashboard opens as~a~compact overview. Use Freeze or~Resume
to~hold or~continue observations, Collapse to~keep the~overview, and~Expand
to~open separate pages for~media, preloading, graphs, viewport, and~lifecycle
details. Pages fit the~available viewport without scrollbars, preserve your
selected page during updates, and~remain within the~viewer in~native fullscreen.
The~media page distinguishes the~displayed photograph from~a~pending navigation
target and~shows loaded dimensions, displayed quality, and~requested quality.
Source labels omit credentials, query parameters, and~fragments.

The~three graphs have separate labeled scales: retained decoded-cache pixel
estimates, cache entries and~active detached loads, and~animation-frame
intervals with a~16.7~ms reference. Up~to~90 samples at~350~ms intervals cover
about 31 seconds. The~pixel estimate excludes the~live image, GPU allocations,
and~browser heap; frame intervals include scheduling and~background throttling
rather than measuring render cost alone. A~past successful decode does~not~prove
that the~viewer still retains that image. Unsupported browser measurements show
an~unavailable marker. Diagnostics observe existing work without requesting
extra media or~metadata; closing stops monitoring and~teardown removes
the~panel. When DEV is~off, the~dashboard module is~not~loaded.

The~Admin \ui{Integrity} workflow checks integrity-managed core files against
the~installed manifest and~helps identify incomplete or~unexpected deployment
changes. It~is~a~runtime verification tool; coordinated backups are~still
required because an~integrity manifest does~not~contain user photographs
or~replace recovery media.

When development diagnostics are~available, a~signed-in administrator can~start
a~\ui{Public gallery load benchmark} for~the~current gallery. The~browser loads
the~gallery several times in~a~hidden same-origin frame using an~anonymous
preview, combines PHP render counters with browser navigation timing, and~then
offers the~benchmark log as~JSON. Use the~same gallery, presentation mode,
and~network/cache conditions when comparing two changes; the~benchmark is~most
useful as~a~before/after measurement rather than~as~a~universal score.

\subsection{Scheduled site maintenance}
\index{scheduled maintenance}
\index{cron!site maintenance}

The~Media and~cache tab uses the~same compact translucent groups as~Features
and~Content and~display. Complete groups flow down one to~three columns, with
independent heights. Question marks open contextual help; scan status and~the
reason for~an~unavailable action remain visible. Schedule preferences save
immediately on~change, together with their time and~numeric values. Pending
writes lock only their form. An~unverified result retains the~intent and~offers
a~reload without replaying the~write. Without JavaScript, use the~normal Save
form. Thumbnail generation, cleanup, reset and~token rotation remain explicit
tasks with their existing safeguards.

In~Admin > Maintenance > Media and~cache, use~\emph{Clean updater cache now}
to~run one bounded step in~place. The~step removes only already staged updater
trash in~\filepath{cache/updates/prune-trash}; it~preserves photos, thumbnails,
downloads, rollback backups and~unknown cache data. Force bypasses the~timer,
but~respects active updates and~maintenance locks. If~debt remains, use~
\emph{Continue cleanup}. The~result reports actual files and~bytes removed.
For~unattended progress, run~\filepath{scripts/site_maintenance.php} with
\texttt{--cache-only} every minute from~hosting cron. This cleanup
is~independent
of~thumbnail scheduling and~does~not~establish an~overall cache quota.
Compatibility settings and~schedule diagnostics remain available in~the~native
disclosures; Maintenance Center retains ownership of~download-cache cleanup.

\paragraph{Measured cache retention.}
Automatic work rotates persistent database regions. Last service and~the~last
successfully completed check are~separate. Thirty-second opportunities serve
the~oldest due work for~at~most five seconds; partial areas resume their saved
cursor and~yield to~other due areas. Healthy completed areas are~due after one
hour; errors wait fifteen minutes. Root discovery finds new directories without
creating one database row per~file. Drained absent identities are~retained seven
days and~pruned in~small batches.

The main cache card shows recorded size, confirmed total removal
and~the~verified queue. The~question-mark help contains scope, coverage and~UTC
times; technical details remain expandable. A~small waiting bar counts down
to~the~scheduled check, never scan completion. The~local timer redraws four
times per~second; a~passive AJAX read refreshes visible evidence every fifteen
seconds without starting work. Historical measurements, current manual-cycle
counters and~lifetime receipts remain separate.

Search, cleanup and~refresh use labeled outline icons. Manual scan activity
stays visible above expandable details; its spinner uses CSS and~respects
reduced motion. Help has short labeled sections, and~operational dates use
structured UTC fields. Each thin owner bar shows measured eligible bytes
as~a~share of~recorded size, never scan completion or~the~live queue.
Maintenance subtab URLs survive reload and~browser history; the~anchor
is~aligned after deferred content loads.

Historical flat download ZIPs expire after seven days from payload modification;
changing permissions or~reading them does not renew expiry. Native abandoned
temporary ZIP outputs retain six-hour inactivity. Only recognized paths with
positive ZIP proof and~free producer/serving leases are~reclaimed. Historical
update ZIPs require a~verified Gallery source snapshot: the~installed
application stays in~place, and~two previous recovery copies are~retained,
counting the~latest job rollback and~selected backup first. Active or~unproven
recovery remains protected. Unfinished regions may reuse spare admission within
the~existing five-second burst; dense catalogs stream names with bounded memory.
After ninety seconds without progress, a~foreground-contended autonomous worker
gets only one forty-millisecond slice. Explicit OFF and~all ownership guards
remain effective.

The~regional sample shows the~area, state, last successful completed check
and~next check or~retry in~compact rows. Technical details retain the~relative
path, observation round, last service, both planned dates, failures and~saved
cursor. Dates explicitly use UTC; missing completion remains unrecorded.
The~cursor is~a~position, never a~percentage. The~sample does~not~cover every
region. AJAX updates preserve the~same localized presentation, open details,
panel and~URL; reading starts no~work.

The~compact overview labels last-known measurements and~their age, completed
coverage, current work, pending eligible debt and~the~last confirmed deletion
separately. A~new partial check keeps the~completed measurement visible;
a~smaller completed check can~replace it. Historical bytes are~not~current size
and~deletion is~not~subtracted from them. Reading status never scans storage. DB
checkpoints own cursors; private disk journals retain names and~detached
payloads. Unfinished legacy work is~drained safely before conversion. Invalid
checkpoints remain unverified. OFF preserves the~registry and~progress;
the~existing authenticated server continuation needs no open browser. A~killed
chain still needs a~later restart opportunity.

Registered disposable browser profiles and versioned release working copies have
a fixed 24-hour lifetime. Use does not renew expiry. New browser profiles record
creation; older workspaces use a filesystem-derived age anchor saved once.
Pins and unsafe or busy ownership can defer physical removal. Persistent release
preparation/qualification storage remains protected.

The same Media and cache page adds \emph{Scan cache} and an explicit
\emph{Clean eligible cache} approval for the exact preview. Inventory is
non-destructive; continuation stays on the page. The compact table shows
observed
and eligible bytes by owner, candidate examples, partial scans and actual
reclamation. Completed test audits and verified downloaded CI diagnostics expire
after 24 hours; generated managed ZIPs remain available for seven days. New
audit
ownership markers also permit expired interrupted reports once their producer
lease is free. Updater retention has no time limit: current installed files and
the latest successful rollback retain current/previous versions; active jobs,
selected beta recovery and rollback sources remain protected. Older verified
completed/cancelled work is reclaimable; failed jobs and unversioned legacy
backups require recovery review. Unknown content, originals, resume state and
\texttt{.keep} pins are preserved. GitHub response metadata expires after one
day,
and SimBrief drafts after their existing 30-minute expiry plus a one-minute
grace.
Hosting cron with \texttt{--cache-only} also advances this independent
lifecycle;
it never auto-approves a manual preview. Dense, slow or unreadable directories
are explicitly partial and preserved. Expiry establishes eligibility, not
instant
deletion. The existing managed-download capacity limit remains; there is no
global
cache quota. Inventory controls are excluded from measured payload totals.

Shared hosts such as~WEDOS may disable \codeword{disk_free_space()}. Automatic
cleanup continues with explicitly unknown free capacity, successful scheduler
and~candidate checkpoints, and~the~same bounded journals. Known capacity below
16~MiB still blocks admission; actual checkpoint write failures stop work.
Unknown capacity is~neither a~measured reserve nor~an~account quota. No~manual
Scan action is~required.

Automatic cache cleanup is~enabled on~installation and~upgrade through
the~canonical Admin Features preference; explicit OFF survives updates. One
ordinary GET visit can start an~authenticated server chain that continues
without an~open browser. New inventory is~due every 30~seconds and~independent
workers admit up~to~5~seconds of~checkpointed inventory and~immediate
reclamation of~verified eligible data. Unfinished cursors, originals, pins
and~active/recovery data remain protected. Waiting occupies a~PHP worker;
configured-origin HTTP access and~sufficient runtime are~required. A~terminated
process or~failed handoff can interrupt the~chain; a~later suitable visit
or~optional independent scheduler can recover it. Actual removals are~coalesced
in~the~Admin log at~most once per~five minutes, without individual filenames
or~empty checks.

A~measured eligible backlog of~at~most 128~MiB receives an~attempt
of~up~to~600~ms and~eight short slices where response finishing is~supported.
Larger backlogs receive 120~ms and~two slices. Without that support, admission
is~40~ms and~two slices; the~client may~wait for~this short shutdown work. These
are~admission deadlines, not~OS-call latency guarantees. PHP retains its worker
even after response finishing. A~shared 60-second throttle, nonblocking worker
locks, foreground leases and~arrival signals make cleanup yield to~other visits.
Each inner slice admits at~most 60~ms, inventory at~most 5000 observations
and~a~4096-name catalog, deletion 180 operations, with eight MiB incremental
memory admission and~eight MiB PHP memory headroom. Many tiny files therefore
continue across visits even below 128~MiB. Known committed eligible candidates
can~be~reclaimed before the~whole scan finishes; scan and~deletion alternate
fairly.

An~unapproved manual preview idle for~fifteen minutes receives a~new scheduled
identity; its old ID is~refused, never automatically approved. Approved deletion
cursors survive interruption. Attempts, actual progress, stale observations,
next due, current pending bytes and~successfully removed bytes remain separate
from~historical measurements; HTTP 200 alone proves no~progress. Optional CLI
or~protected HTTPS cron can~supply opportunities on~an~idle installation, with
at~most ten seconds and~32 slices per~invocation. No~hosting setup is~needed
for~visitor-driven operation. See~\filepath{docs/CACHE_MAINTENANCE.md}. Fixed
workspace lifetime remains 24~hours, ZIP retention seven days and~updater
recovery contextual without a~TTL.

Manual runs immediately show a~diagnostic ID and~a~trace linking to~filtered
Admin Logs. Events record locks, checkpoints, observed entries, removed bytes
and~safe failure codes. Busy results identify the~owner; force respects it.
Failed log writes are~visible in~diagnostics, with best-effort fallback
to~PHP's error log. Hard process termination can~leave an~incomplete trace.

Use~\emph{Copy log} inside the~disclosure to~copy its current JSON trace.
A~thin progressbar uses known updater phase counters; unknown totals remain
indeterminate. \emph{Stop update} uses safe pre-activation cancellation
and~never
forces an~activation-stage stop. Busy refusal remains visible. Read-only
progress
refreshes while the~panel is~visible and~does~not~advance the~update.
Failed phases show the~safe updater error/reference, also included in~the~copied
trace. A~phase counter at~100\% does~not~mean successful validation.
Copied checkpoints also include age, worker-lock observation, counter movement
and~available retry history. A~stored running status alone does~not~prove live
work; an~unlocked worker is~only an~instantaneous observation.

Routine maintenance can~run in~bounded slices instead of~one long request.
The~maintenance service chains safe web requests when traffic is~available
and~can~also be~called from~hosting cron with the~CLI command shown in~Admin.
It~can~check thumbnail state, refresh metadata, process cleanup tasks, roll
up~telemetry, and~archive eligible Admin-log days according to~their configured
policies. The~hidden web-cron token is~rotatable; rotating it~invalidates
previously published web-cron URLs. Maintenance is~non-destructive by~default
and~does~not~replace explicit confirmation for~database optimization or~other
consequential actions.

\subsection{Cron Overview and~visitor scheduling}
Open \ui{Maintenance > Cron Overview} for~a~dense, sortable table of~automatic
updates, log archival, site/cache maintenance, updater-trash cleanup,
telemetry retention and~one-shot attachment-count reconciliation. Each owner
supplies its effective cadence, next eligible threshold and~actual last-attempt
evidence. Unknown evidence remains unknown; admission is~not~completion.
Ordinary visitor and~crawler PHP page requests provide scheduling opportunities.
The~shared scheduler admits at~most one bounded background owner per~tick,
retaining owner locks, enablement, UTC windows and~the~Maintenance Center claim.
Scheduled gallery publication still happens before the~triggering page renders.

Sort the~column headings; the~visible page refreshes existing cells about once
per~second without executing jobs. Observation pauses while hidden and~backs off
on~errors. Play submits one protected task action in~place, overriding only
its time gate; native POST remains available without JavaScript. A~task can~be
busy, continued or~deferred rather than complete. Next eligible is~a~threshold,
not~an~exact execution promise: without visitors, work waits for~the~next
request.
Play is~directly before the~task name. The~pause icon beside the~heading freezes
only this live preview; resume reads immediately and~background tasks continue.
Future thresholds retain their absolute time with~the~remaining duration
in~parentheses.
Task names link to~their existing settings or~operational controls. No next
deadline is~labelled Disabled, Completed or~Nothing pending cleanup where
appropriate; Unknown is~reserved for~unavailable evidence.

The~collapsed \ui{Optional hosting cron} section creates or~regenerates
a~copyable
protected URL and~can~revoke it. The~URL respects installation subdirectories
and~query routing without rewriting. Its example uses \codeword{curl -fsS} every
one to~five minutes, optionally. It~calls the~same due-only scheduler and~never
disables visitor scheduling. Treat the~URL as~a~secret. No hosting cron, browser
job timer, resident process or~external queue is~required.
The~256-bit token and~shared locks are~supplemented by~one accepted hosting call
per~minute. Repeated calls cannot force work past its owner gates. Network/ICMP
flood protection remains the~hosting provider's responsibility.

\subsection{Admin log history and~archives}
\index{admin logs}
\index{log archive}

The~compact log table keeps event names, messages, severity, timestamps
and~instance counts visible. Open the~event name for~IDs, request/route
metadata, the~first and~latest occurrence, and~individual group members;
the~code icon beside a~message reveals its structured context. Copy and~download
icons have descriptive tooltips and~accessible names. Row actions export one
event or~the~entire group indicated by~that row; inside group details you
can~also copy, save or~select individual members. Every export includes the~full
diagnostic text and~context.

Search includes event names, messages, JSON context and~request/route metadata.
A~row marked \ui{Match in~details} matches inside its collapsed context. Live
filtering commits only the~newest request, keeping rows, counts and~the~URL tied
to~the~same result even during rapid typing. For~automatic cache failures,
\codeword{cache.automatic\_failed} records the~entry point, failure stage,
exception class, source filename/line and~validated SQLSTATE/driver codes when
available. It~omits raw messages, SQL, private paths and~credentials. Repeated
failures are~coalesced for~five minutes; changed diagnostics retain
a~thirty-second minimum. Logging failure preserves the~worker checkpoint
and~safe refusal. Complete pending migrations when schema diagnostics require
them; observe automatic progress through ordinary visits and~passive status
without starting a~manual scan.

Use the~checkboxes and~the~header checkbox to~select entries on~the~current
page. In~Admin bulk lists with item checkboxes, click one row and~Shift-click
another in~the~same list to~apply the~second checkbox's resulting state
to~enabled visible rows between them on~the~current results page. The~range
anchor resets when live filtering, pagination, or~another row replacement
changes the~results. Severity filters continue to~control which log rows
are~visible; \ui{Selected}, \ui{This page} and~\ui{All filtered} retain their
existing log export scopes. The~toolbar offers those scopes for~both copying
and~saving a~UTF-8 TXT file. Selecting a~group includes all its instances,
including those not~yet loaded; overlapping individual selections appear only
once. \ui{All filtered} exports matching raw events across all pages
in~the~chosen time order. Selection clears when filters or~pagination replace
the~rows. PHP reads large exports in~batches; copying still needs the~complete
chosen text in~browser memory, so~prefer saving for~very large histories.
If~clipboard access is~unavailable, a~dialog selects the~complete text
for~manual copying. TXT downloads also work without JavaScript; the~existing
full CSV/JSON ZIP export remains available.

Everyday administrative work creates a~useful history: uploads, gallery changes,
security decisions, maintenance results, warnings, and~other operational events
can~leave a~record in~the~Admin log. That history is~valuable when you want
to~understand what happened, when it~happened, and~which account or~request
was~involved. It~is~also normal for~the~table to~become large on~an~active
installation. Version~\version{} therefore treats log care as~its own explicit
maintenance task rather than allowing generic database cleanup to~remove audit
history unexpectedly.

The~Admin log screen can~show individual entries or~collapse repeated events
into a~summary. A~group may~represent repeated thumbnail warnings, upload
results, or~maintenance messages; opening it~shows the~individual records.
Filters and~workflow status controls help narrow operational review, while
bounded exports preserve selected history without loading the~entire table
in~one request. Large lists, raw-instance expansion, and~exports are~processed
in~limited portions, so~requesting a~complete history does not~require
the~browser or~PHP to~hold the~entire table in~memory at~once.

The~owner can~choose how long recent logs should remain in~the~live database.
Choosing \emph{Forever} disables automatic archival. Choosing a~finite live
period does not~immediately throw older records away. The~maintenance workflow
first selects complete calendar days that are~outside the~live retention window,
writes a~protected archive containing both machine-readable JSON and~a~readable
static HTML report, checks that the~archive contains the~expected number
of~records, and~only then removes those same rows from the~live table.
The~archive is~stored under \filepath{data/admin-log-archives/}, which
is~application data rather than a~public gallery area and~carries an~additional
web-server protection rule.

Archive maintenance is~resumable. It~writes temporary files before promoting
a~completed archive, records a~small state file, and~uses a~lock so~two
maintenance requests do~not~process the~same history simultaneously. If~hosting
limits, a~connection interruption, or~another problem stops the~work, the~Admin
screen can~show the~incomplete state and~the~next maintenance attempt
can~verify, continue, or~recover it. An~archive is~never considered a~reason
to~delete live records merely because a~file with a~similar name exists;
the~row-count and~archive metadata checks are~part of~the~safety boundary.

Administrators should still include the~database
and~the~\filepath{data/admin-log-archives/} directory in~coordinated backups.
The~live database contains recent operational history, while the~archive
directory contains older history selected by~the~explicit retention policy.
Keeping both gives the~owner a~continuous record and~makes a~restored
installation easier to~investigate after an~update, migration, or~unexpected
event.

\subsection{Application updates}

Automatic stable checks use the~saved 5/15/30/60-minute interval, defaulting
to~five minutes. The~first admitted gallery visit after the~interval, including
an~anonymous visitor or~allowed robot, can~start the~update. After a~successful
response, independent authenticated server requests continue three-second
worker slices until completion. Login, JavaScript, an~open Updates panel
and~hosting cron are~unnecessary. Blocked SEO requests do~not~start this work.
The~configured installation URL must be~reachable from~the~server with valid
TLS. Lost handoffs expire after 120 seconds; later visits recover them.
GitHub backoff and~worker failures may~delay progress. Five minutes controls
discovery eligibility, not~total installation time; a~future release still
needs an~eligible visit. See~\filepath{docs/AUTOUPDATE.md}.

The~compact release summary keeps installed and~available versions, channel,
status, and~the~main actions together; repository and~GitHub API details remain
in~a~collapsed diagnostics section. When a~job completes, the~browser refreshes
the~summary from~local metadata in~place, keeps the~panel open and~the~URL
unchanged, and~hides an~obsolete stable-update action. This refresh
does~not~perform another GitHub check. If~it~fails, use the~status-refresh
button to~retry the~local read without repeating the~installation.

On~Windows, an~updater checkpoint commit allows up~to~ten atomic replacement
attempts, with~50~ms pauses and~a~450~ms total pause limit, to~handle brief
file-sharing locks. If~the~commit still fails, the~previous checkpoint
is~preserved. A~persistent permission problem requires fixing storage access
before retrying the~job. Windows uses the~same bounded retry policy when
migrating orientation metadata in~\filepath{gallery.json}; a~concurrent edit
or~persistent refusal preserves the~existing document and~stops the~migration.

The~updater checks configured channels, validates packages, stages content,
verifies required runtime files and~sizes, preserves overwritten files
in~recovery storage, and~activates the~update. Rate-limit handling and~retry
state prevent repeated remote requests during provider wait periods. A~resumable
job card appears in~both \ui{Status} and~\ui{Advanced tools}, so~a~beta install,
restore, or~reinstall continues to~show its progress where it~was~started.
The~page may~be~closed and~reopened without discarding completed checkpoints.

The~Admin update area also shows cached release information and~versioned patch
notes for~the~available channel, so~an~owner can~review a~release before
starting its~job. Stable and~beta channels remain separate choices; the~Beta
label belongs to~the~release choice in~the~Admin interface and~does~not~change
the~updater's durable job or~manifest terminology.

Patch notes are~stored as~raw Markdown in~the~installed
\filepath{PATCH_NOTES.md} and~the~versioned cache. The~Admin viewer renders
the~selected version when it~is~displayed, including entries loaded from~older
caches; previously cached HTML is~discarded rather than~trusted. Reading
the~viewer uses locally bundled or~cached notes without making another GitHub
request. A~separate, bounded release-discovery operation may~refresh remote
notes; when that fails, installed notes remain available.

Markdown links such as~\codeword{[issue](https://example.org/issue/123)},
angle-bracket autolinks, and~plain HTTP(S) addresses become clickable. They open
in~a~new tab with~\codeword{noopener} and~\codeword{noreferrer} protection.
Other URL schemes remain escaped, inert text. The~renderer preserves headings,
lists, emphasis, fenced blocks, and~literal inline code; it~does not~turn URLs
inside code spans into links. Link punctuation, underscores, and~query
parameters are~retained without accepting arbitrary HTML.


Temporary download or~hosting failures may~be~retried. A~package whose files
do~not~match its own integrity manifest is~different: downloading the~same
published build again cannot change those bytes. PHP Gallery therefore asks
the~administrator to~cancel that prepared job and~choose a~newer release or~beta
code instead of~offering an~endless retry loop.

Stable discovery and~package work are~separate bounded operations. A~discovery
request may~create a~durable background job, while a~later safe request
or~the~CLI runner advances a~short slice through download, archive validation,
extraction, manifest verification, staging, rollback backup, activation,
migrations, and~cleanup. Range requests can~resume interrupted downloads, worker
locks prevent concurrent advancement, and~a~stale lock can~be~recovered after
its~owner expires. On~idle shared hosting, schedule
\codeword{php scripts/application_update.php} from~cron, for~example every five
minutes. Each invocation performs bounded discovery or~advances existing
background work; correctness does~not~depend on~\codeword{set_time_limit()}
or~\codeword{ignore_user_abort()}.

If~the~source server ignores Range, three incomplete full-response
restarts switch the~same job to~the~published \filepath{production.zip}.
Separate bounded metadata requests require the~release tag to~resolve
to~the~original
full commit and~require the~asset's canonical URL, byte count and~SHA-256.
The~archive digest is~checked before extraction even when optional per-file
publisher checks are~disabled. Flat production archives and~wrapped source
archives use the~same later installation stages. Missing or~unmatched assets
retain finite failure handling; request limits are~not~extended.
An~older installed updater cannot use this repair before receiving it.
Verify that first upgrade on~a~staging copy or~deploy the~complete compatible
release manually and~use \ui{Finish FTP update}.

Current releases also reconcile the~small set of~application-owned server-policy
files during update preparation and~activation. This protects installations
whose older updater skipped a~policy file: the~validated release copy
is~compared and~published atomically, rollback metadata is~extended before
replacement, and~a~migration-backed bootstrap path can~repair an~already
activated older tree. The~reconciliation is~limited to~known policy paths
and~does~not~alter gallery media or~user configuration.

Before activation, an~administrator can~cancel or~retry a~recoverable job. After
activation, the~Admin recovery action can~restore managed application files
from~the~saved rollback snapshot, but~file rollback does~not~reverse database
migrations. Stable restore, clean reinstall, and~beta installation remain
resumable jobs with their own package validation. The~standalone
\filepath{reset.php} emergency path is~a~separate recovery tool for~returning
to~the~stable branch when the~normal Admin route cannot be~used; it~still
requires the~same careful backup and~integrity review.

During the~short activation critical section, a~dependency-free early runtime
gate prevents new ordinary requests from~loading a~partially replaced
application. Such requests receive a~private, non-cacheable
\codeword{503 Service Unavailable} response until activation completion
is~durably recorded. The~authenticated update recovery/status path remains
available, so~an~interrupted activation can~continue. Corrupt or~incomplete gate
state fails closed instead of~presenting the~installation as~healthy.

The~same early runtime boundary converts uncaught and~catchable fatal PHP
failures into bounded \codeword{500} responses when PHP can~still control
the~response. Production hosting must keep \codeword{display\_errors} disabled;
otherwise the~PHP runtime itself may~print warning or~fatal details before
application-level handling can~format a~safe response.

\important{Run a~coordinated backup before an~application update. Keep
the~backup available until runtime checks, migrations, public galleries,
protected access, uploads, and~Admin operations have been verified.}

Before activation, updater archive validation also requires the~complete new
request runtime and~its reviewed module plan. A~partial archive is~refused while
prepared files remain outside the~active installation. Keep all shipped runtime
files together during manual deployment; replacing only the~bootstrap would
leave an~incomplete application.

Updater activation uses the~reviewed updater subset of~the~production inventory.
Incoming inventories list WinApp sources separately so~older installed updaters
can~validate the~release before installing its reader. The~CMS updater
does~not~install those sources. Older source archives without that inventory
retain a~bounded path based on~their owned manifest; a~malformed present
inventory is~refused. Obsolete cleanup requires prior ownership evidence
and~preserves unknown local files. Rollback uses the~server-written snapshot
index and~refuses unindexed files or~paths redirected by~symbolic links
or~Windows junctions. These file safeguards do~not~reverse database migrations.

Failed jobs from automatic, Admin/API and~Advanced commit installs share
three transient retries after 60, 120 and~240 seconds. Undefined functions
and~deterministic integrity failures do~not~repeat the~same defective package.
New jobs pin an~immutable trusted GitHub commit. The~publisher integrity sweep
is~retained but~disabled until explicitly enabled; the~short stage is~labelled
\ui{Preparing package}. Staging and~activation check actual copy bytes.
Non-core assets and~rollback snapshots additionally preserve
exact bytes through raw SHA-256 identities. At~least 600 seconds after the~first
failure, an~untouched
pre-activation job releases its active slot. A~safe post-activation failure
creates one separate rollback job restoring the~actual previous managed files
and~channel settings, including deleted files and~removal of~newly created
paths.
Both identities, archives, backups and~diagnostics remain preserved.
A~failed rollback cannot create another rollback.

The~sole public maintenance switch is~\codeword{maintenance.lock}
in~the~installation root. An~empty manually created file also enables
maintenance;
removing it~ends manual maintenance. Active jobs and~rollback records alone
do~not~close the~website. Inspect updater-owned markers before removal after
an~interrupted write. Legacy activation markers are~imported before admission,
with their exact bytes retained privately. The~marker stays outside deployment
and~rollback payloads. Runtime commit ends the~isolated automatic rollback
window. Application files continue to~be~updated in~place.

File restoration requires verified backup bytes, matching actual schema/ledger
and~proof that no~migration effects occurred. Shared runtime leases drain before
activation; supported targets keep the~gate through migrations and~required
finalization. They then commit the~usable runtime and~reopen before physical
cleanup, which may~pause or~retry while the~website remains available.
Ordinary use during the~observation window invalidates rollback safety even
with unchanged schema. Entered/unverified migrations, unknown DB state, missing
proof or~unsupported older packages stay failed with repair instructions.
Database migrations and~application data are~never reversed. Automatic discovery
OFF and~beta still prevent new automatic installation; capability OFF stops
installer work. After safe restoration, discovery can~skip known failed versions
and~install a~newer stable release. Gated failures need hosting CLI cron
or~an~authenticated Admin request; denied visitors cannot run the~scheduler.
No~idle worker waits through backoff.

Applied migration rows remain history when their files were intentionally
retired from the~installed manifest. Database evidence format~2 retains
the~complete ledger with timestamps and~actual schema, recording that absence
explicitly. Missing declared files and~changed bytes still refuse activation;
old saved witnesses retain their original strict comparisons.
An~old \codeword{activate} checkpoint alone does~not~prove partial activation:
pointer release requires unchanged originals, verified backups and~absent
created paths under start, worker and~exclusive runtime locks. Admin requests
release their own bootstrap read lease only after authentication and~CSRF.
Integrity progress shows the~verified-file cursor; entering each new stage
resets the~previous counters. The~compact progress surfaces remain synchronized
on~desktop and~narrow screens.
The~normal action remains Update followed by~automatic continuation.
Details call the~lifetime counter Processing runs: it~includes short worker
requests and~waits, and~Retry keeps the~same job and~count. Waiting for~other
requests is~explicit and~uses slower polling. Unmanaged local PHP files remain
preserved. Private worker diagnostics include stage/reference, exception owner
and~line, safety receipts and~the~validated migration identity when relevant;
unavailable Admin logging falls back to~the~PHP error log without blocking work.

An~older stuck updater does~not~receive this mechanism merely because a~new
release is~published. In~maintenance mode, stop workers, preserve coordinated
backups and~all failed-job evidence, establish actual database compatibility,
deploy the~complete verified compatible fixed release and~run forward
migrations.
Only after verified repair may~the~administrator archive the~matching old active
pointer. Never replace only the~bootstrap, edit a~job's target/package, discard
a~live activation gate or~restore application files alone. See
\filepath{docs/AUTOUPDATE.md} for~the~complete repair procedure and~limits.
That procedure includes the~reported \codeword{c2e2bc77} installation and
its pinned older job: after separate approval, deploy the~complete compatible
fixed release in~maintenance mode, preserve the~old job and~reconcile its
owner only after verified forward repair. Retry never changes a~job's target.
For~overwrite-only FTP deployment, upload the~complete release without deleting
old local files, jobs or~backups. Choose \ui{Reset updater / finish FTP update}
on~the~job card or~in~Advanced tools. It~stops previous jobs at~a~safe boundary,
preserves their records and~gate receipts, then starts fresh forward completion.
\ui{Install stable} is~always available and~downloads the~latest stable branch
head, overwriting every packaged file even at~the~same version. It~also replaces
stuck or~paused jobs without requiring FTP. Both actions preserve configuration,
media, unlisted local files and~backups. File-content differences are~warnings
with the~relative file and~expected/actual digests in~the~log when the~optional
verifier is~enabled. Its~sweeps are~currently disabled for~all installation
jobs.
Safe archive paths, required files and~runtime
ownership remain enforced. With the~optional verifier enabled, a~missing,
malformed or~stale manifest also becomes
a~warning and~cannot stop either recovery action. Reset closes visitor admission
before existing requests drain, so~ongoing traffic cannot prevent its~start.
Actual activation and~migrations wait for~those requests to~finish.
Ordinary automatic updates also close admission before waiting for~existing
readers, so~new visitors cannot indefinitely delay activation.
While the~gate is~closed, private workers retain a~paced successor when waiting
for~old readers; another visitor is~not~required. A~one-second handoff delay
runs after locks are~released. The~configured Admin session name survives
post-response session close, keeping authenticated recovery reachable.
The~start log names the~target version; a~separate completion event records
the~previous and~installed versions only after durable completion.
During activation, visitors see a~compact English maintenance page instead
of~a~plain server message. It~refreshes the~current page every three seconds,
even without JavaScript, and~offers \ui{Try again}.
The~self-contained light/dark layout needs no~database or~application assets.
HTTP 503 remains in~effect; POST and~machine replies never refresh
automatically.
Busy takeover is~shown explicitly and~continues
automatically. FTP retry rechecks installed files instead of~downloading.
Recovery runs forward migrations; database changes are~never reversed.
\ui{Pause} works at~the~next safe checkpoint in~every stage;
\ui{Continue update} resumes the~same job. A~partial activation stays gated.
Advanced commit installation also accepts older supported release commits;
database migrations are~never reversed and~a~compatible DB backup may~be~needed.

\subsection{Integrity manifest}
\index{core manifest}

\filepath{app/core-manifest.json} records the~application version and~hashes
for~integrity-managed core files. The~local generator under
\filepath{scripts/generate_manifest.php} calculates final hashes after runtime
changes. Documentation and~user-data inclusion follow the~generator's
established policy.

During deterministic release preparation, the~manifest generator accepts
the~optional \codeword{SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH} build timestamp for~its
\codeword{generated_at} field; without it, normal generation uses the~current
clock. The~hosted release workflow derives the~epoch from~the~release metadata
and~uses it~for~both the~manual PDFs and~the~final manifest, so~identical
prepared inputs retain reproducible timestamps. The~manifest still
has~to~be~regenerated after changing an~integrity-managed application file.

\section{Optional background: how PHP Gallery works}
\label{sec:architecture}
\index{architecture}
\index{controllers}
\index{services}
\index{views}

The~following pages describe the~request path used by~the~application. They
are~mainly relevant to~maintenance and~development; ordinary gallery
administration does not~require them.

The~generated module plan declares shared dependencies and~one canonical file
order, preserving the~complete route closure and~include order. Keep dependency
inputs and~the~generated plan together when changing routes. Request/session
adapters own transport access; language, navigation tokens, gallery grants, NSFW
acknowledgement, and~flash behavior retain their established owners.
The~lightbox separately owns navigation generations and~decoded resources, while
the~viewer keeps presentation and~authorized quality selection. This separation
provides regression boundaries; it~does~not~establish faster browser operation.
See \filepath{docs/BROWSER_LIFECYCLE.md}
and~\filepath{docs/COMPATIBILITY_LIFECYCLE.md}.

\subsection{Bootstrap, routing, and~dispatch}
\index{bootstrap}
\index{routing}
\index{controller dispatch}
\index{request!life cycle}

Ordinary requests enter through \filepath{public/index.php}; the~repository-root
\filepath{index.php} is~a~supported compatibility entry point for~other hosting
layouts. The~public entry loads \filepath{app/early\_runtime.php} before
\filepath{app/bootstrap.php}, then both layouts reach the~same normal request
pipeline.

The~small request-local kernel under \filepath{app/runtime/} coordinates
the~existing request lifecycle through Request, Router, RouteRegistry,
and~ModuleLoader before invoking the~procedural controller. The~canonical route
and~security table remains in~\filepath{app/bootstrap/dispatch.php}. A~reviewed
generated plan loads logical modules for~the~selected route; production performs
no~source scan or~dependency compilation and~has~no~load-all fallback. Existing
PHP~8.1 and~shared-hosting requirements remain sufficient, without Composer
or~a~Node build. Legacy CLI/test consumers may~use
\filepath{app/bootstrap\_full.php} for~the~complete procedural API.

Shared Admin help and~factory-value markup belong to~the~existing
\filepath{app/views/admin\_ui.php} owner; Maintenance sections remain deferred.
Theme reuses the~complete gallery-access policy closure already needed by~its
picker and~preview, while scoped saves use canonical mutation responses.
These owners add no~logical module or~runtime file. Hosted preparation and~FULL
verify the~unchanged duplicate-ownership limits.

The~loader validates the~complete module plan in~each request. It~checks
canonical layer/path order in~one pass, checks each selected physical path
once within a~load preflight, and~counts completed files without repeatedly
scanning module lists. Later loads check their paths afresh. No~validation
cache is~shared between requests, so~updates and~concurrent requests need
no~additional cache invalidation. Optional OPcache remains a~server setting;
verify its~state in~the~web PHP runtime and~keep timestamp validation
or~deployment resets effective. The~existing Test Run trace separates plan
loading, full validation, dependency planning and~includes. Developer
measurements use disposable public/Admin requests and~distinguish opcode-cold
and~warm samples; they do~not~establish production latency.
The~compiler explicitly reviews the~private tracing callback; only local loader
operations supply it, with no~additional module dependency.

Shared updater discovery reads safe errors and~opaque references from
the~lightweight job reader. Public galleries do~not~load the~updater worker
for~these values. Explicit Admin/background installer owners retain the~complete
worker; optional subsystem diagnostics observe already-loaded owners.

Thumbnail delivery reuses the~existing complete public-media module.
Compilation accepts this dependency only while its~full file closure already
belongs to~the~thumbnail owner. Route file limits and~the~duplicate-ownership
ratchet remain enforced.

Bootstrap reads local configuration and~establishes the~request context:
database access where required, language, session state, security prerequisites,
and~shared runtime services. Routing then translates the~incoming URL
or~query-style fallback into an~internal route plus parameters such as~a~gallery
path, page number, media size, or~share token. Dispatch calls the~controller
registered for~that route.

The~ordering is~deliberate. A~request must~be~classified before code decides how
to~serve it, and~access checks must run before protected output is~assembled.
A~direct-looking image or~thumbnail URL is~therefore still an~application
request; the~media controller authorizes it~before returning bytes.

\begin{lstlisting}
browser request
  -> index.php or public/index.php
  -> app/bootstrap.php
  -> cms_run() -> Gallery\Core\Kernel
  -> Request -> Router -> RouteRegistry
  -> ModuleLoader -> app/bootstrap/dispatch.php
  -> controller function
  -> service functions
  -> HTML, JSON, media, archive, or redirect response
\end{lstlisting}

\subsection{Controllers}
\index{controller}

Controllers own HTTP-level coordination. They receive a~resolved route
and~request context, validate the~operation, call the~services needed for~that
operation, and~choose the~response type. A~public gallery route normally returns
HTML; an~Admin side-panel action may~return JSON or~an~HTML fragment; a~media
route returns an~authorized file; a~download route can~stream an~archive.

Public gallery rendering begins
in~\filepath{app/controllers/public_gallery.php}, with related responsibilities
split into focused controller files. Uploads, maintenance, updates, downloads,
media access, and~Admin features have their own entry points, while shared
domain rules remain in~services.

Controllers are~not~an~authorization shortcut. Thumbnail, map, lightbox, search,
download, and~direct-media routes repeat the~relevant access decision instead
of~assuming that a~visitor previously opened the~gallery page successfully.

\subsection{Services}
\index{services}

Reusable application rules live in~\filepath{app/services/}. Services resolve
galleries and~images, evaluate access, normalize settings, manage uploads
and~mutations, prepare thumbnail/media manifests, perform searches, maintain
metadata, and~coordinate other domain work. A~controller should be~able to~ask
for~one of~these operations without reimplementing its rules.

The~separation matters because the~same photograph can~be~reached from several
routes. Access policy, thumbnail selection, or~deletion semantics should
not~depend on~whether the~request came from a~gallery card, search result,
lightbox, Admin tool, or~direct link. Source photographs remain in~the~gallery
tree; services coordinate the~database records and~generated derivatives that
describe or~support them.

\subsection{Views and~browser modules}
\index{views}
\index{browser modules}

Views render prepared data into HTML. They are~responsible for~structure
and~presentation, not~for~deciding whether a~visitor is~authorized or~how
a~thumbnail is~generated. Server output includes semantic links, forms, titles,
alternative text, and~initial media candidates before JavaScript enhancement
begins.

Browser modules add lightbox behavior, search suggestions, voting, upload
progress, drag-and-drop actions, thumbnail upgrades, diagnostics, and~the~Admin
side panel. The~code remains framework-free. Public pages therefore retain
a~useful server-rendered baseline, while authenticated workflows can~add richer
in-place interactions.

The~Admin side panel is~dynamic: its fragment can~be~replaced without navigating
away from the~page. Modules that own panel controls consequently use delegated
or~lifecycle-aware event binding so~a~freshly rendered copy continues to~work
and~an~old fragment no~longer owns listeners.

\subsection{Public selected-gallery render pipeline}
\index{render pipeline}

A~selected-gallery request first resolves the~gallery and~its effective access
policy, including inherited protection, password/share state, publication state,
and~age restrictions. Only permitted content proceeds to~rendering.

The~controller then loads the~direct child galleries and~the~current page
of~photographs, together with the~metadata needed for~titles, descriptions,
tags, dates, votes, ordering, and~navigation. Thumbnail metadata is~prepared
in~batches, and~request-local media manifests describe the~authorized candidates
available for~each visible image.

The~view receives that prepared model and~renders the~page title, branding,
navigation, breadcrumbs, cards, pagination, lightbox configuration, and~footer
assets. Browser modules enhance the~result after delivery. Access remains
a~server responsibility; JavaScript can~make an~interaction faster or~more
convenient, but~it~does not~turn an~unauthorized candidate into an~authorized
one.

This server-first split also preserves useful behavior when scripts are~blocked
or~late. Direct links, semantic content, and~the~initial image choices
are~already present in~the~HTML, while JavaScript handles the~features that
genuinely need an~interactive client.

\section{Optional background: what the~database remembers}
\label{sec:database}
\index{database schema}
\index{migrations}

The~database stores application state that does not~belong in~image filenames:
descriptions, privacy, ordering, tags, votes, dates, public addresses, accounts,
and~maintenance history. The~overview below is~enough for~backup planning
and~hosting discussions; column-level definitions remain in~the~database
reference \cite{database}.

\subsection{Principal domains}

\begin{longtable}{>{\bfseries}p{0.25\linewidth}p{0.67\linewidth}}
\toprule
Domain & Persistent responsibility\\
\midrule
\endhead
Users and~authentication & Administrator identity, password hashes, recovery
email, external identity links, reset tokens, and~scoped credentials.\\
Galleries & Folder path, hierarchy, public path, metadata, visibility, access,
branding, presentation overrides, and~operational ordering.\\
Images & Gallery ownership, relative path, filename, metadata, dimensions,
visibility, checksum, EXIF data, ordering, and~derived state.\\
Tags & Reusable tag identity plus gallery and~image relationships.\\
Votes & Gallery and~image scoring records with viewer association.\\
Thumbnails & Valid generated variant inventory, dimensions, format, status,
and~derivative version.\\
Settings & Scalar application, theme, feature, update, maintenance,
and~rendering values.\\
Audit and~telemetry & Administrator events, operational diagnostics,
privacy-controlled measurements, and~rollups.\\
Duplicate ledger & Per-administrator canonical pair decisions and~exact-gallery
suppression rules.\\
Jobs and~tokens & Bounded browser, detector, migration, WebDAV, reset, sharing,
and~maintenance state where persistence is~required.\\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}

\subsection{Relations and~cascades}

Foreign keys express ownership where schema evolution and~compatibility permit
it. Cascades remove dependent workflow records when their parent identity
disappears. Content deletion continues through application services
so~filesystem and~database effects remain coordinated. Unique constraints encode
identities such as~canonical duplicate pairs, exact administrator-gallery rules,
slugs, tokens, and~metadata variants.

\subsection{Application settings}

The~\codeword{app_settings} table stores key-value configuration.
\codeword{app_setting()} reads values, \codeword{set_app_setting()} persists
normalized values, and~focused services interpret domain-specific settings.
Public language configuration uses \setting{public_language} for~the~site
default, \setting{public_language_selector_enabled} for~the~default-on~viewer
override feature, and~\setting{public_language_selector_languages} for~its
ordered non-empty language list. The~public thumbnail renderer uses
\setting{public_thumbnail_rendering_mode} with \setting{responsive}
and~\setting{progressive} values.

% BEGIN gallery-key-api reference (#352)

% Author: Rudolf Klusal

\section{Gallery-scoped API-key reference}

\label{sec:gallery-key-api}

\index{API!pgu\_}

Complete reference for~the~original gallery-key protocol. Authentication does not~expose
automatic browsing. All examples contain synthetic identities; use only galleries and~keys
that you are~authorized to~operate.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{Scope and~support boundary}{Scope and support boundary}}

\label{api352:scope}

This reference describes the~existing gallery-scoped \codeword{pgu_} HTTP API. It
is~intended for~independently written clients, not~only the~Windows uploader. The~reviewed
baseline is~PHP Gallery \codeword{1.0.4}, commit
\codeword{150160d3771f97f5b96dab86e98ac6fe5365ccb4}. This identifies the~source checked
for~this edition; it~is~not~runtime version negotiation or~a~promise that every older
installation has~every optional action.

Only explicit operations authorized by~a~gallery key belong here. Automatic browsing,
server-wide gallery discovery/listing/search, filesystem discovery, Watcher traversal,
browser-approved linking and~the~resource API from issue \#345 are~not~part of~this public
contract. WebDAV is~a~separate protocol with separate credentials. An inventory request
compares caller-supplied file candidates; it~is~not~an~endpoint for~browsing all galleries.
Internal post-upload registration is~server implementation, not~an~exposed scan command.

The~key fixes the~upload target. It is~not~necessarily upload-only: when gallery migration
is~enabled, that same key can~export the~authorized gallery and~its descendants,
and~a~destination key can~import a~new child tree. Do not~hand it~to~an~untrusted party
as~a~write-only credential. Choosing \codeword{include_subgalleries=0} limits one export
request, not~the~authority of~the~key itself. No new permissions, endpoints or~server
behavior are~introduced by~this documentation.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{Operation index}{Operation index}}

\label{api352:operation-index}

The~first nine actions below share index.php?page=\codeword{upload_automation_upload}.
Migration uses the~eight individually named routes. Every call requires the~appropriate
gallery key; specialized actions add their own gates. The~document covers 17 operations.
Identifiers and~code remain identical in~all languages.

\begin{longtable}{@{}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash}p{0.28\linewidth}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash}p{0.67\linewidth}@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Action or~route} & \textbf{HTTP method and~request encoding} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Action or~route} & \textbf{HTTP method and~request encoding} \\
\midrule
\endhead
\bottomrule
\endfoot
\codeword{upload} & POST; multipart/form-data \\
\codeword{inventory} & POST; application/json \\
\codeword{revoke} & POST; application/json | form \\
\codeword{ai_next_job} & POST; application/json \\
\codeword{ai_heartbeat} & POST; application/json \\
\codeword{ai_complete} & POST; application/json \\
\codeword{ai_asset} & POST; form \\
\codeword{flight_report_status} & POST; application/json \\
\codeword{flight_report} & POST; multipart/form-data \\
\codeword{gallery_migration_manifest} & GET / POST; query / form \\
\codeword{gallery_migration_asset} & GET / POST; query / form \\
\codeword{gallery_migration_package} & POST; form \\
\codeword{gallery_migration_receive_manifest} & POST; form \\
\codeword{gallery_migration_receive_asset} & POST; multipart/form-data \\
\codeword{gallery_migration_receive_package} & POST; multipart/form-data \\
\codeword{gallery_migration_receive_status} & GET / POST; query / form \\
\codeword{gallery_migration_receive_complete} & POST; form \\
\end{longtable}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{Connection, keys and~request conventions}{Connection, keys and request conventions}}

\label{api352:connection}

In Admin, open the~intended gallery, then its API tab. Generate a~separately labelled key
for~each client and~copy both the~exact upload endpoint and~the~key. The~raw key
is~displayed once; only its SHA-256 hash is~stored. Ordinary issued keys use \codeword{pgu_}
followed by~64 hexadecimal characters. This API has~no~refresh-token exchange or~general
expiry field. A~key must remain active and~not~revoked; it~can~also cease to~work when its
gallery or~required storage is~unavailable. Rotate by~creating and~verifying a~replacement
for~the~same gallery before revoking the~old key. Do not~publish credentials in~source,
screenshots, URLs, logs or~shared shell history.

Use the~copied canonical front-controller URL, preserving any installation subdirectory.
The~portable upload URL is \codeword{<base>/index.php?page=upload_automation_upload}.
\codeword{/api/upload} is~a~rewrite-dependent compatibility alias, not~the~canonical
address. Migration uses the~same \codeword{<base>/index.php?page=...} form with the~exact
route below. API keys are~not~Admin sessions; key creation and~global key listing require
authenticated Admin UI/CSRF and~are~not~documented key-authenticated operations.

\begin{longtable}{@{}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash}p{0.28\linewidth}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash}p{0.67\linewidth}@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Field} & \textbf{Type, requirement and~meaning} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Field} & \textbf{Type, requirement and~meaning} \\
\midrule
\endhead
\bottomrule
\endfoot
\codeword{X-Gallery-API-Key} & Recommended header. First nonempty dedicated-header value
wins after trimming. \\
\codeword{Authorization: Bearer} & Fallback when the~dedicated header is~empty. The~bearer
is~still the~original gallery key, not~a~linked access token. \\
\codeword{api_key} & Legacy scalar form field, used only if~neither header supplied a~usable
credential. Not~read from the~query or~a~JSON object. \\
\codeword{Accept: application/json} & Request JSON refusals even for~multipart calls. Binary
success responses retain their actual media type. \\
\codeword{action} & For~upload/automation, nonempty form value takes precedence over JSON;
omitted/empty selects upload. Use only explicitly documented actions. \\
\end{longtable}

Send exactly one credential form. An invalid higher-priority key is~not~rescued by~a~valid
lower-priority one. Keep TLS verification enabled and~use HTTPS for~remote deployments;
the~legacy handler is~not~a~substitute for~HTTPS enforcement at~the~hosting boundary. Refuse
redirects while holding credentials, especially changes of~host or~scheme. Do not~reuse
browser cookies or~expose the~key in~public JavaScript. Cross-origin browser CORS/preflight
support is~not~promised by~this contract.

All code below uses synthetic URLs and~shell variables. Set \codeword{API_URL} to~the~copied
endpoint and~supply \codeword{PG_API_KEY} securely in~the~process environment; no~real key
belongs in~the~examples. A~curl transport exit code is~not~sufficient: inspect the~appended
HTTP status and~the~JSON \codeword{ok} field. Do not~use redirect following or~disable
certificate verification.

\begin{lstlisting}[language={}]
API_URL='https://gallery.example/photos/index.php?page=upload_automation_upload'
: "${PG_API_KEY:?Set PG_API_KEY securely before running requests}"
# Never add -L or --insecure to credential-bearing calls.
\end{lstlisting}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{Upload photographs}{Upload photographs}}

\label{api352:upload}

Operation: \codeword{POST index.php?page=upload_automation_upload},
\codeword{multipart/form-data}, \codeword{action=upload} (also the~default). Authentication
and \codeword{upload_api} must be~available. Do not~manually set a~multipart boundary when
using curl. No Admin cookie or~CSRF field is~required for~these key-authenticated calls.

\begin{longtable}{@{}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash}p{0.28\linewidth}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash}p{0.67\linewidth}@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Field} & \textbf{Type, requirement and~meaning} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Field} & \textbf{Type, requirement and~meaning} \\
\midrule
\endhead
\bottomrule
\endfoot
\codeword{image} / \codeword{images[]} & Binary; one file or~repeated files, required.
Prefer one spelling per~request; a~nonempty images[] input wins. The~server validates
filenames and~actual image bytes. \\
\codeword{gallery_id} & Optional integer assertion. A~positive value different from the~key
gallery returns 403. Omitting it~does not~broaden authority. \\
\codeword{create_thumbnails} & Optional scalar flag; default true. True spellings:
1/true/yes/on, case-insensitive. Use 0 to~suppress server derivative completion. Existing
valid client derivatives may~still be~installed. \\
\codeword{image_client_ids[]} & Optional per-original correlation strings; [A-Za-z0-9\_-],
1..80 characters. Aligned with original-file positions, not~persistent operation IDs
and~not~idempotency keys. \\
\codeword{client_thumbnails[]} & Optional binary derivative files. Each requires
the~corresponding three aligned thumbnail metadata arrays below. \\
\codeword{thumbnail_client_ids[]} & String; required for~each thumbnail. Must match a~valid
\codeword{image_client_ids} value in~this request. \\
\codeword{thumbnail_sizes[]} & Integer target long side in~pixels; one of~300, 600, 800,
960, 1280, 1600. Actual decoded long side must not~exceed the~declaration by~more than 4
pixels. \\
\codeword{thumbnail_formats[]} & String jpg or~webp (normalized to~lowercase); detected MIME
must match. Valid extra JPG variants may~be~counted as~skipped on~a~WebP-only installation,
rather than rejecting the~original. \\
\codeword{sim_location_source} & Optional string; only \codeword{simconnect_camera} enables
the~following shared camera coordinates. \\
\codeword{sim_camera_latitude} / \codeword{sim_camera_longitude} & Optional finite numbers,
both required for~accepted camera metadata. Latitude -90..90; longitude -180..180; rounded
to~7 decimals. Invalid metadata is~ignored, not~a~new GPS write endpoint. \\
\codeword{sim_camera_altitude} & Optional finite altitude in~feet (legacy SimConnect unit;
no~conversion by~this handler), rounded to~2 decimals; absent/invalid becomes null. One
camera tuple applies to~the~accepted images of~the~request. \\
\end{longtable}

Baseline image types are~JPG/JPEG, PNG, GIF and~WebP. HEIC/HEIF and~DNG depend on~the~actual
server converters; do~not~infer support from a~filename alone. No PDF, music, arbitrary ZIP
or~JSON/base64 image upload belongs to~this operation. PHP \codeword{upload_max_filesize},
\codeword{post_max_size}, \codeword{max_file_uploads} and~the~hosting proxy can~impose
tighter bounds. There is~no~advertised universal legacy batch size or~per-request rate
budget. Use modest batches; do~not~copy limits from the~separate linked API.

The~server natural-sorts uploaded filenames and~may~choose unique names or~apply configured
renaming. Do not~assume response positions preserve arbitrary input order. When using
position-based thumbnail correlation, submit originals already in~the~same natural filename
order; a~single original with its derivatives avoids ordering ambiguity. Re-uploading
an~original is~not~checksum-based deduplication and~can~create another file. Read
the~returned filenames and~image IDs, not~a~guessed URL. Modern derivative policy
is~WebP-first; disabling derivative creation does not~guarantee previews already exist.

\begin{lstlisting}[language={}]
curl --silent --show-error --proto '=https' --max-time 120 \
  --header "X-Gallery-API-Key: $PG_API_KEY" \
  --header 'Accept: application/json' \
  --form 'action=upload' --form 'image=@photo.jpg' \
  --form 'create_thumbnails=1' \
  --write-out '\nHTTP %{http_code}\n' "$API_URL"
\end{lstlisting}

\begin{lstlisting}[language={}]
{
  "ok": true, "gallery_id": 42, "gallery_title": "Example",
  "gallery_url": "https://gallery.example/photos/gallery/example",
  "edit_url": "/photos/index.php?page=admin_edit_gallery&id=42",
  "uploaded": 1, "scanned": 1, "image_ids": [501],
  "filenames": ["photo.jpg"], "renamed": 0,
  "rename_warnings": [], "rename_failures": [],
  "thumbnails": 6, "thumbnail_failed": 0, "thumbnail_errors": [],
  "post_upload_errors": [],
  "sim_camera_metadata": {"attached": 0, "skipped": 0, "error": ""},
  "client_thumbnails": {
    "installed": 0, "skipped": 0, "failed": 0, "errors": []
  }
}
\end{lstlisting}

The~example is~one possible response, not~guaranteed counts or~URL spelling.
\codeword{uploaded}, \codeword{scanned}, \codeword{renamed}, \codeword{thumbnails} and
\codeword{thumbnail_failed} are~integer counters; \codeword{image_ids} is~an~integer list;
\codeword{filenames}, \codeword{rename_warnings}, \codeword{rename_failures},
\codeword{thumbnail_errors} and \codeword{post_upload_errors} are~string lists. Nested
camera counts/error and~client-thumbnail counts/errors must also be~inspected. HTTP 200 with
\codeword{ok=true} can~coexist with failed derivative, GPS or~bookkeeping work. It
is~not~an~atomic all-or-nothing transaction. The~upload catch maps failures to~422; shared
authentication failures are~described below.

The~following optional example attaches one prepared WebP derivative to~one original.
The~thumbnail must already have the~declared dimensions and~actual WebP content; neither its
filename nor~its MIME declaration can~bypass server validation. Multiple originals
can~instead use repeated images[] fields, with aligned \codeword{image_client_ids}[]
in~the~server filename order.

\begin{lstlisting}[language={}]
curl --silent --show-error --proto '=https' --max-time 120 \
  --header "X-Gallery-API-Key: $PG_API_KEY" \
  --header 'Accept: application/json' \
  --form 'action=upload' --form 'images[]=@photo.jpg' \
  --form 'image_client_ids[]=photo-001' \
  --form 'client_thumbnails[]=@photo-300.webp;type=image/webp' \
  --form 'thumbnail_client_ids[]=photo-001' \
  --form 'thumbnail_sizes[]=300' --form 'thumbnail_formats[]=webp' \
  --form 'create_thumbnails=1' \
  --write-out '\nHTTP %{http_code}\n' "$API_URL"
\end{lstlisting}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{Inventory: compare supplied file candidates}{Inventory: compare supplied file candidates}}

\label{api352:inventory}

Operation: \codeword{POST index.php?page=upload_automation_upload},
\codeword{application/json}, \codeword{action=inventory}. It returns matches for~supplied
candidates in~the~one authorized gallery, not~a~complete photo catalogue or~a~server-wide
search. An empty files list can~check gallery identity and~returned capabilities without
uploading anything. JSON calls must authenticate in~a~header.

\begin{longtable}{@{}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash}p{0.28\linewidth}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash}p{0.67\linewidth}@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Field} & \textbf{Type, requirement and~meaning} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Field} & \textbf{Type, requirement and~meaning} \\
\midrule
\endhead
\bottomrule
\endfoot
\codeword{action} & Required string inventory. \\
\codeword{files} & Array of~candidate objects; omitted or~non-array normalizes to~empty.
Invalid rows are~discarded. Up to~5000 distinct SHA-256 values; duplicate hashes replace
the~earlier candidate. \\
\codeword{files[].sha256} & Required 64 hexadecimal characters; trimmed and~lowercased. Hash
the~exact original bytes sent to~the~server. \\
\codeword{files[].filename} & Optional string; path components are~removed. Empty becomes
sha256. This is~a~display/correlation name, not~a~destination path. \\
\codeword{files[].size} & Optional integer bytes; normalized to~at~least zero. Positive size
provides a~secondary match check and~is~required for~disk fallback. \\
\codeword{files[].client_id} & Optional trimmed string echoed for~mapping; default sha256.
It is~not~the \codeword{image_client_ids} upload identifier or~a~durable receipt. \\
\codeword{deep_check} & Optional JSON boolean, default false; only literal true activates
it. Disk fallback considers at~most the~first 8 normalized candidates. This bounds candidate
count, not~the~directory traversal time or~number of~directory entries inspected. \\
\end{longtable}

\begin{lstlisting}[language={}]
python - <<'PYJSON' > inventory.json
import hashlib
import json
from pathlib import Path
path = Path("photo.jpg")
digest = hashlib.sha256(path.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
print(json.dumps({"action": "inventory", "deep_check": False,
    "files": [{"client_id": "photo-1", "filename": path.name,
               "size": path.stat().st_size, "sha256": digest}]}))
PYJSON
curl --silent --show-error --proto '=https' --max-time 120 \
  --header "X-Gallery-API-Key: $PG_API_KEY" \
  --header 'Accept: application/json' \
  --header 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  --data-binary @inventory.json \
  --write-out '\nHTTP %{http_code}\n' "$API_URL"
\end{lstlisting}

\begin{lstlisting}[language={}]
{
  "ok": true, "action": "inventory", "gallery_id": 42,
  "gallery_title": "Example", "gallery_folder": "example",
  "checked": 1, "matched": 1,
  "existing": [{
