% END gallery-key-api reference (#352)

\section{Optional reference for~developers}
\label{sec:development}
\index{development}
\index{coding style}

This reference is~for~people changing PHP Gallery itself. Owners who only need
publication and~maintenance checks can~continue with Section~\ref{sec:testing}.

\subsection{Repository organization}

\begin{longtable}{>{\ttfamily}p{0.27\linewidth}p{0.65\linewidth}}
\toprule
\textrm{Location} & \textrm{Responsibility}\\
\midrule
\endhead
app/controllers/ & HTTP controllers and~response coordination.\\
app/services/ & Reusable domain and~infrastructure services.\\
app/views/ & Layout and~reusable server-rendered view helpers.\\
app/lang/ & Server and~browser translation catalogs.\\
database/migrations/ & Timestamped schema evolution.\\
public/assets/ & Framework-free JavaScript, CSS, and~public assets.\\
scripts/ & Migration, integrity, deployment, and~maintenance utilities.\\
tests/ & Standalone PHP and~focused JavaScript tests.\\
winapp/ & Windows-specific tools and~integrations.\\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}

For~new or~materially changed Admin interfaces, follow the~permanent visual
guidance in~\filepath{docs/ADMIN_DESIGN.md}. It~defines the~compact media-player
and~WinApp direction and~feature-scoped CSS review.

\subsection{Coding conventions}

New PHP files use strict types and~four-space indentation. Controllers
coordinate request behavior, while services own reusable logic. New JavaScript
and~CSS remain framework-free and~belong under public assets. Migrations use
timestamp-prefixed filenames. Existing explanatory comments, docstrings,
and~PHPDoc receive preservation and~correction as~behavior evolves.

Declaration docstrings are~required for~named functions, methods, and~class-like
declarations, including interfaces, traits, and~enums. Place PHPDoc/JSDoc
immediately above the~declaration and~Python docstrings first inside its~body.
Named functions and~methods need a~factual purpose summary, a~typed
and~described parameter entry for~each explicit parameter, and~a~typed return
entry, including void. Classes need a~factual purpose summary. Anonymous
functions, closures, arrow functions, callbacks, variables, constants, and~class
properties do~not~require declaration docstrings; named declarations nested
inside callbacks remain checked. File attribution, native signature typing,
operational-policy comments, and~parser coverage remain separate requirements.

Atomic modules should own one cohesive responsibility. Shared contracts deserve
normalized inputs, explicit outputs, and~focused tests. Dynamic Admin and~public
fragments require lifecycle-safe setup and~teardown.

\subsection{Authoring code before source checks}

Readability guidance covers code, TeX and~Markdown source. Prefer handwritten
lines near 80 display columns; consider natural breaks at~81--100 and~seek
readable splitting above 100. This is~advice, not~a~maximum or~CI gate. Put
lengthy arguments and~collection entries on~separate indented lines, group
conditions and~separate logical steps/declarations with idiomatic blank lines.
Wrap documentation prose at~existing spaces while preserving paragraphs,
TeX commands, non-breaking bindings and~Markdown structure. Preserve semantics,
types, comments and~docstrings; allow necessary longer literals and~URLs.

For~an~optional whole-tree report, open \ui{Code readability report}
in~GitHub Actions, choose \ui{Run workflow} and~select only the~branch.
Its~workflow, checker, rules and~source use that branch's exact event SHA;
the~summary records the~branch/SHA. Download the~artifact for~complete HTML,
Markdown, JSON and~per-file diagnostics covering PHP, JS, MJS, Python, CSS,
TeX and~Markdown. Findings never fail CI or~rewrite source; tooling failures
remain visible. The~workflow appears in~the~sidebar after reviewed
default-branch installation. Rules, exclusions, Unicode/tab measurement
and~focused examples are~in~\filepath{docs/CODE_READABILITY.md}; the~central
optional audit profile is~\codeword{readability}. Required qualification
remains unchanged.

The~canonical \filepath{AGENTS.md} requires each named declaration
and~its~complete documentation in~the~same edit. Start with a~factual purpose
before tags, document every explicit parameter (including defaults and~added
parameters), actual return variants and~concrete collection/object shapes.
PHP/Python native types remain independent. Anonymous callbacks need
no~declaration docstring; named declarations inside them still do. Runtime
policy sites independently need purpose, type, units, scope, consumers
and~rationale immediately before the~site. Checked examples
in~\filepath{docs/AGENT_AUTHORING.md} are~parsed by~the~existing scanners
in~a~registered regression, with negative fixtures preserving failures
for~incomplete contracts.

Python sources consistently use typed Google-style \texttt{Args:} and
\texttt{Returns:} sections. Each parameter entry names the~argument, its~type
and~its~actual meaning; procedures document \texttt{None}. Native annotations
remain independent. Legacy tag inputs stay supported by~the~scanner
for~compatibility, while authored Python docstrings use one consistent format.

GitHub Actions runs the~source preflight before hosted preparation. Coding
agents follow CI-first: identify the~current authorized working branch and~HEAD,
verify task-scoped branch/push access, finish source/tests/documentation, commit
and~push that exact branch. Never directly mutate \codeword{develop}
or~\codeword{main}. Missing authorization or~CI access is~BLOCKED. Local audits,
generators and~test loops are~exceptional diagnostics, not~routine handoff
gates. Hosted CI retains the~immutable merge-base with \codeword{origin/main};
releases retain the~previous stable tag. Red or~slow CI does~not~justify
local-only qualification.

A~requested implementation or~fix authorizes automatic creation/checkout
of~a~suitable codeword{feature/*} branch from verified codeword{origin/develop},
commits, exact branch push and~hosted BASIC. Routine branch permission needs
no~repeat question. Integration, squash, release, tags and~publication still
require separate explicit approval under ilepath{AGENTS.md}.

For~disposable MySQL diagnostics, \filepath{scripts/gallery_workflow_mysql.php}
offers \codeword{--audit-php-regression} through the~central PHP regression
suite. Isolate the~checkout separately to~isolate its~cache as~well.

Hosted qualification covers PHP/Node/WinApp tests, source contracts, required
Chromium, real MySQL/MariaDB workflows, PHP compatibility and~required
Linux/Windows package checks; macOS runs independently as~an~advisory check.
Every mandatory job and~the~exact-SHA gate must pass. The~release workflow
compiles all~four PDF manuals once after the~final source edits, not~during
routine development.

The~hosted portable WinApp job installs the~existing Pillow requirement into
the~Python interpreter selected for~the~audit, so~tray-badge pixel coverage
runs instead of skipping. Local runs without Pillow may~still skip this
optional visual fixture; the~headless hosted job does~not~install pystray.

The~title-picture regression extends the~already registered Admin side-panel
delegation and~browser lifecycle suites, plus the~existing gallery edit
concurrency HTTP/database suite. These fixtures remain under
\filepath{scripts/audit.php}; they add no~YAML test owner or~alternate runner,
and~the~protected evaluator registry is~unchanged.

Reports record source HEAD, resolved comparison SHA and~dirty state. A~modified
checkout is~feedback, not~clean-commit qualification. The~linked
\filepath{source-failures.md} lists all~failed changed-source findings, blockers
and~findings in~over-budget categories; complete value-free JSON retains
the~evidence and~historical debt stays identified. Candidate workflows upload
these together. Repair the~whole batch before rerunning; unchanged input
is~not~a~repair. Finish all~four manual sources and~push the~authorized working
branch. Hosted Candidate preparation regenerates artifacts after the~final edit
and~runs the~mandatory full CI matrix for~the~exact prepared SHA. Record SHA,
base, profile/result and~material incomplete coverage. Measure first-attempt
results and~repair cycles from actual reports; expected savings remain unknown
until observed.

\subsection{Adding a~feature}

A~typical feature change follows this sequence:

\begin{enumerate}
  \item Trace current implementation and~identify the~owning controller,
  services, views, browser modules, persistence, and~tests.
  \item Define access, CSRF, scope, fallback, migration, and~no-JavaScript
  contracts.
  \item Add focused services and~route integration.
  \item Add append-only migration work where persistent schema changes
  are~required.
  \item Add translated interface strings.
  \item Add focused automated tests and~documented manual verification.
  \item Update permanent Markdown references and~all~four TeX manual sources
  whenever current user, administrator, architecture, or~maintainer guidance
  changes.
  \item Hosted Candidate preparation runs
  \filepath{scripts/prepare_candidate.php} and~its read-only check: runtime
  modules, indexed production inventory and~complete-byte integrity hashes
  are~generated in~dependency order. It~commits only changed artifacts back
  to~the~same authorized branch, refuses concurrent writers, and~qualifies
  the~exact resulting SHA. Agents push focused source commits and~inspect CI;
  they do~not~duplicate generation locally.
  \item Report the~GitHub Actions URL, checked working branch, exact prepared
  candidate SHA, immutable comparison base, required job results and~manual
  or~missing coverage. A~stale, missing, skipped, cancelled, failed or~blocked
  mandatory job is~not~green qualification. Fix source and~push the~same branch
  for~fresh CI.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Preparing source candidates for~CI}

Atomic source commits are~allowed during development, but~qualification must use
a~candidate whose generated artifacts match the~exact source revision. Permanent
technical documentation is~part of~the~source edit. Changes to~administrator
behavior, architecture, runtime ownership, security, packaging, or~CI also
require reviewing all~four TeX manual editions, not~only user-facing pages.
Routine development leaves \filepath{PATCH_NOTES.md} and~the~tracked manual PDFs
unchanged.

The issue-362 source workflow also provides an unprivileged recovery artifact
for prepared outputs. Both PHP quick jobs run preparation and its read-only
check; after successful preparation, only the PHP~8.5 job uploads the complete bytes of
\filepath{app/runtime/modules.php}, \filepath{app/production-files.json}, and
\filepath{app/core-manifest.json} before feature tests. The artifact name starts
with \codeword{issue-362-prepared-} and includes the exact source SHA. It lets
the owner review and commit those prepared bytes. It is not FULL qualification
or publisher authority; verify the SHA and inspect all three files before use.

Preparation in a temporary CI checkout does not update the local checkout.
Persist all three canonical outputs on the feature branch and require final-SHA
hosted re-preparation to reproduce them before claiming a runnable checkout.
Startup checks every registered route, including optional routes with a disabled
capability. When localhost smoke is explicitly requested, reload the exact mount
and a content page after persistence; correlate any error reference with the PHP
emergency log and repeat the browser check after repair.

\begin{enumerate}
  \item Hosted Candidate preparation runs
  \filepath{scripts/prepare_candidate.php} and~its read-only check: runtime
  modules, indexed production inventory and~complete-byte integrity hashes
  are~generated in~dependency order. It~commits only changed artifacts back
  to~the~same authorized branch, refuses concurrent writers, and~qualifies
  the~exact resulting SHA. Agents push focused source commits and~inspect CI;
  they do~not~duplicate generation locally.
  \item Report the~GitHub Actions URL, checked working branch, exact prepared
  candidate SHA, immutable comparison base, required job results and~manual
  or~missing coverage. A~stale, missing, skipped, cancelled, failed or~blocked
  mandatory job is~not~green qualification. Fix source and~push the~same branch
  for~fresh CI.
\end{enumerate}

The~current qualification and~release assembly sequence is~described
in~the~maintainer verification section and~\filepath{RELEASE.md}.

\section{Checking your gallery before publication}
\label{sec:testing}
\index{testing}
\index{quality assurance}

Testing for~a~gallery owner means walking through the~site as~the~intended
audience. A~technically healthy page can~still contain a~private location,
an~unclear title, an~incorrect date, an~accidental download permission,
or~an~awkward mobile layout. A~short human review catches these presentation
and~privacy concerns.

\subsection{A~visitor-centered publication check}
\index{publication checklist}

Open a~private browser window and~follow the~same link you plan to~share. Check:

\begin{enumerate}
  \item The~title and~first paragraph explain the~collection.
  \item The~chosen cover remains clear at~a~small size.
  \item Child galleries appear in~a~sensible order.
  \item Photograph titles and~descriptions match the~visible subjects.
  \item Private photographs and~galleries stay hidden.
  \item Password or~share access works from a~fresh session.
  \item GPS maps reveal only intended locations.
  \item The~first page loads comfortably on~a~phone.
  \item The~large viewer, forward and~backward controls, and~close action feel
  natural.
  \item Search, tags, voting, and~downloads behave according to~the~gallery's
  purpose.
\end{enumerate}

Ask another person to~try the~gallery without instructions. Their first click
and~first question often reveal navigation or~wording that felt obvious only
to~the~owner.

\subsection{Automated checks for~software maintainers}

The~central audit covers clipboard MIME/name handling and~synthetic browser
paste events, ordinary multipart upload and~panel persistence. These fixtures
do~not~read the~operating-system clipboard. Record actual Windows
Chrome/Edge/Firefox, Linux Chromium/Firefox and~macOS Safari/Chrome/Firefox
results using the~manual matrix in~\filepath{TESTING.md}, for~both browser
and~server preparation on~direct-page and~side-panel forms. Unexecuted checks
remain pending.

The~registered miniature tests cover bounded raster headers, cancellation
and~resource disposal. The~Chromium fixtures also check 100-item FileList
parity, a~320-pixel drawer, reset/navigation cleanup, focus and~classic/prepared
retry without duplicate ingestion. Observed URL/decode counts
are~not~a~whole-process memory measurement; real OS clipboard delivery
and~Firefox/Safari checks remain manual.

Based on~hosted timeout evidence, the~Windows feature job uses
\codeword{php scripts/audit.php --suite=winapp-partitioned}. It~places all
classes from \codeword{test_native_gallery_viewer.py} into one child, the~small and~large
thumbnail-cache benchmark classes \codeword{ThumbnailCacheSmallBenchmarkTests}
and~\codeword{ThumbnailCacheLargeBenchmarkTests} into separate children,
the~\codeword{GalleryPreloadWorkerTests} class into a~fourth, and~the~exact
remaining inventory into a~fifth. All five serial processes retain the~existing
90-second child limit; the~coordinator has a~480-second outer bound for these
child caps and~inventory/report handling. The~central
audit verifies all five schema-1 reports and~the~complete disjoint test-ID
union before merging actual outcomes. Missing, timed-out, malformed, duplicate
or~unsuccessful evidence fails. The~ordinary full-suite owner keeps its
existing single-process behavior and~limit. Earlier full-process and~two-,
three- and~four-partition timeouts remain failed runs.
Selected-child logs flush the~full failure traceback immediately after each
FAIL/ERROR terminal record, preserving its~reason if~a~later timeout prevents
the~final JSON report.

The~\ui{Issue 381 Watcher recovery} workflow also offers a~manual browser
diagnostics mode. It~runs only the~central
\codeword{php scripts/audit.php --suite=browser-map} on~the~selected feature
SHA, retaining reports and~screenshots. Separate concurrency prevents that
diagnosis from cancelling normal source/WinApp checks. It~preserves central
limits and~assertions and~does~not replace the~complete integration FULL.
Interactive fixtures activate their own browser tab before trusted input;
emulated DOM focus alone does~not prove native fullscreen entry.

For~coding agents, GitHub Actions is~the~default and~authoritative verification
environment (CI-first). The~central audit commands below describe the~hosted
runner; agents do~not~execute them locally by~default. Focused local
reproduction is~reserved for~an~observed environment-specific CI failure,
genuinely inaccessible CI or~an~explicit request. Local evidence never replaces
required hosted coverage.

\begin{lstlisting}[language=bash]
# Hosted source feedback (exceptional local diagnosis only):
php scripts/audit.php --profile=release-preflight
php scripts/audit.php --profile=quick
# Hosted full source qualification:
php scripts/audit.php --profile=full
# Hosted authoritative release job:
php scripts/audit.php --profile=release
\end{lstlisting}

The~current qualification and~release assembly sequence is~described
in~the~maintainer verification section and~\filepath{RELEASE.md}.

The~registered breadcrumb Theme test compares the~Theme and~physical-gallery
style lists in~\filepath{docs/ADMIN_SETTINGS_INVENTORY.md} with
\codeword{breadcrumb_style_registry()}. It~also protects the~\codeword{chevron}
fallback and~the~gallery-only \codeword{inherit} sentinel. When adding a~style,
update both documented lists; \codeword{inherit} is~not~a~Theme visual style.

\subsection{Manual browser verification}

\subsubsection{Network and~interaction checks}

Browser-dependent software checks use representative data, anonymous
and~authenticated sessions, an~empty cache, mobile and~desktop viewports,
JavaScript-disabled operation where relevant, reduced-motion preferences,
and~network throttling.

For~public thumbnail rendering, inspect the~Elements and~Network panels.
Responsive mode should expose its complete active candidate set. Progressive
mode should expose a~small active candidate and~inert larger candidates before
activation, then perform at~most the~documented concurrent upgrades for~relevant
cards. Check layout stability, cache reuse, error fallback, lightbox
interaction, maps, voting, protected media, and~direct links.

\subsection{Release hygiene}

Follow \filepath{RELEASE.md} for~the~complete release sequence. Compare
the~exact previous stable tag, complete the~release notes and~permanent
documentation, and~align all~four maintained manual editions. Rebuild each PDF
with the~indexed multi-pass sequence in~\filepath{docs/LATEX_BUILD.md} and~check
compiler success, references, index entries, and~layout warnings. Visual PDF
review is~reserved for~an~explicit request or~diagnosis of~a~concrete layout
problem; routine successful builds need no~additional visual-approval gate.

The commands in this section describe hosted release ownership and the retained
local maintainer recovery path. Normal preparation and qualification run on
GitHub; agents do not execute these commands locally by default. Local recovery
requires an explicit request or genuinely unavailable CI and remains BLOCKED /
not CI-qualified until hosted evidence exists. The later review workflow
performs protected merge and tagging; GitHub Release publication stays manual.

The~current qualification and~release assembly sequence is~described
in~the~maintainer verification section and~\filepath{RELEASE.md}.

Create a~production folder or~ZIP only when explicitly requested; hosted release
assets use the~same PHP builder automatically. Pass the~candidate epoch with
\codeword{--timestamp} for~deterministic metadata. The~builder refuses stale
manifests and~existing outputs; \filepath{scripts/release_files.php} inspects
membership only. Required Linux/Windows packaging owners cover the~real builder
independently of~the~database matrix; macOS repeats the~same suite in~a~separate
advisory run. The~main-only locked TinyTeX cache warmer requires reviewed
installation before release branches can~reuse its exact key. A~CMS-only release
does~not~require a~new WinApp installer. Commit, tag, publish, upload,
and~deploy require explicit authorization. CI tests PHP 8.1 as~the~compatibility
floor and~PHP 8.5 as~the~newest representative. All~three database variants run
on~PHP 8.5; historical evidence retains its actual versions.

During ordinary development, update all~four \codeword{.tex} sources together
and~review their actual explanations; do~not~rebuild the~tracked PDFs after
every change. The~PDFs may~lag behind the~sources until final release
preparation. Automated version/date replacement is~not~semantic documentation
generation: maintainers must add new feature, security, compatibility,
and~operational content in~English, Czech, German, and~Swedish themselves.

The~current qualification and~release assembly sequence is~described
in~the~maintainer verification section and~\filepath{RELEASE.md}.

\section{Making the~gallery feel fast}
\label{sec:performance}
\index{performance}
\index{speed}

Perceived speed is~not~one number. Visitors notice when the~page first appears,
when the~first photographs become recognizable, whether scrolling stays
responsive, how quickly controls react, and~how long a~larger image takes after
they ask for~it. Connection quality, server distance, page size, thumbnail
policy, branding assets, and~the~selected renderer all contribute.

\subsection{Practical ways to~improve speed}

\begin{itemize}
  \item Keep the~number of~cards per~page reasonable for~the~target audience
  and~hosting plan.
  \item Generate the~configured thumbnail sizes in~advance for~large public
  collections.
  \item Avoid unnecessarily large hero, background, logo, and~other theme
  assets.
  \item Prefer the~responsive thumbnail renderer when predictability
  and~no-JavaScript behavior matter most; use the~progressive renderer when its
  staged sharpening suits the~collection and~browser mix.
  \item Place the~gallery on~hosting geographically close to~its main audience
  where practical.
  \item Test with an~empty cache. A~warm developer browser can~hide expensive
  first-visit transfers.
  \item Check PHP/database latency separately from image-transfer time before
  assuming that every slow gallery is~a~thumbnail problem.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Slow-connection test}

Open the~gallery in~a~private browser window, select a~representative mobile
viewport, enable network throttling in~developer tools, and~reload with an~empty
cache. Watch the~order in~which the~page, covers, visible cards, and~later image
upgrades arrive. Repeat with the~renderer or~page-size setting you intend
to~compare; otherwise cached files make the~comparison unreliable.

\subsection{Technical measurements for~maintainers}

Public render profiling and~browser diagnostics are~useful when a~subjective
report needs numbers. Record at~least:

\begin{itemize}
  \item time to~first byte and~server-side render duration;
  \item HTML transfer size;
  \item request count and~bytes transferred for~initial thumbnails;
  \item time until the~largest visible content has~settled;
  \item layout shifts while cards and~media load;
  \item main-thread work attributable to~browser modules;
  \item number and~size of~progressive candidate upgrades;
  \item database-query totals from the~development profiler where available;
  \item image decode/memory pressure when testing very large originals or~deep
  lightbox zoom.
\end{itemize}

The~development profiler and~captured benchmark records are~intended
for~before/after comparisons across renderer modes and~gallery layouts. Use
representative high-density screens and~constrained connections rather than
relying only on~a~fast desktop with a~warm cache.

\section{Troubleshooting}
\label{sec:troubleshooting}
\index{troubleshooting}

\subsection{Application does not~start}

Confirm the~PHP version, required extensions, configuration location, database
connectivity, writable paths, and~web-root routing. Review PHP and~web-server
logs. Run syntax checks on~recently changed PHP files and~inspect pending
migrations.

\subsection{Gallery or~images are~missing}

Confirm filesystem paths, gallery discovery state, database records, visibility,
parent access rules, password unlock state, NSFW policy, supported file type,
and~image relative path. Test as~both administrator and~anonymous visitor.

\subsection{Thumbnails are~missing or~soft}

Review thumbnail metadata and~maintenance reports. Confirm generated sizes,
format support, configured bounds, source geometry, derivative status,
and~public endpoint authorization. In~responsive mode, inspect
the~browser-selected candidate and~\codeword{sizes}. In~progressive mode,
inspect the~small candidate, queue state, selected replacement, and~decode
result.

\subsection{Admin actions leave the~side panel}

Confirm the~matching JavaScript module loaded, delegated handlers recognized
the~form, AJAX headers and~response contracts are~correct, the~returned fragment
contains expected lifecycle markers, and~generic Admin form handlers exclude
feature-owned forms. Direct POST behavior can~still provide the~documented
fallback route.

\subsection{Migration fails}

Keep a~verified database backup and~record the~migration filename, Gallery
version, database-server version, bounded on-screen message, request reference,
and~existing schema/ledger state. Retry through the~Gallery administration page
so~the~normal idempotent compatibility path can~finish an~interrupted migration.
Version~\version{} does~not~need triggers, stored routines, \codeword{SUPER},
\codeword{log\_bin\_trust\_function\_creators}, or~a~global database change.
If~the~bounded error remains, inspect Runtime Diagnostics and~give the~request
reference to~the~maintainer or~hosting provider; do~not~paste database
credentials or~raw production data into a~support report.

\subsection{PDF manual compilation fails}

Run the~commands in~\filepath{docs/LATEX_BUILD.md}. Confirm \codeword{pdflatex}
and~\codeword{makeindex} are~installed. A~first MiKTeX run may~request common
packages. Delete ignored intermediate build files after a~failed package
transition and~repeat the~documented sequence.

\section{Backup and~recovery checklist}
\label{sec:backup}
\index{recovery}

\begin{enumerate}
  \item Export the~complete application database.
  \item Copy \filepath{galleries/} with source files and~gallery-owned assets.
  \item Copy local configuration and~relevant installation secrets securely.
  \item Preserve custom theme assets and~custom CSS.
  \item Record the~application version and~pending migration state.
  \item Store backups outside the~active web tree.
  \item Test restoration periodically in~an~isolated environment.
  \item Verify public routes, protected access, Admin login, thumbnails,
  uploads, and~migrations after restoration.
\end{enumerate}

Updater-created backups preserve overwritten application files for~update
recovery. A~complete operational backup also includes the~database, gallery
tree, local configuration, and~installation-owned assets.

\subsection{Validate a~recovery set}
\index{recovery!assurance}

Keep the~database export, original photographs, local configuration, relevant
\filepath{data/} storage, Trash contents, custom assets, and~exact application
version together as~one coordinated recovery set. A~provider snapshot
or~a~bounded
maintenance window can~prevent writes from~splitting database and~file state.
Store an~off-host copy and~agree how much recent work may~be~lost and~how
quickly
service must return.

The~CLI entry point \filepath{scripts/recovery.php} can~inventory that set
and~validate restored file samples and~manifest hashes in~a~separate location.
Follow \filepath{docs/RECOVERY_ASSURANCE.md} for~the~evidence format
and~commands.
The~tool does~not~execute a~database dump, restore production, or~verify
a~backup
provider. Its synthetic corruption drill proves the~checker detects fixture
damage, not~that your actual backup can~restore the~site.

Before opening a~restored installation, isolate email, scheduled jobs,
integrations, and~write access from~production destinations. Verify the~restored
database, relationships, Admin login, private-media denial, and~Trash
restoration.
Record backup age, elapsed recovery time, and~the~operator's observations
without
copying secrets into reports. Use \filepath{docs/RECOVERY_OFF_HOST.md}
for~the~full
procedure. Updater file rollback still does~not~reverse database migrations.

\section{Maintainer verification and~release evidence}
\label{sec:release-evidence}

\subsection{Source qualification and~selected feature workflows}

Feature pushes and~the~final squash receive BASIC syntax and~declaration checks.
In~GitHub Actions, choose \ui{Integrate into develop}, select main
or~the~intended
feature in~\ui{Use workflow from}, and~select the~source branch. The~owner's
explicit dispatch runs that revision's actual workflow, audit and~tests.
Changes to~tests or~registries need no~preliminary installation on~main.

The~source is~one commit above observed develop. Hosted preparation generates
runtime modules, inventory and~manifest, folds changed generated data into
the~final candidate and~obtains fresh BASIC. A~separate authority checkout
inspects the~clean candidate. FULL retains all~27 required native owners:
shared source contracts, portable Node/WinApp, Linux/Windows packaging,
four shards for~each~of~three databases, four shards for~each PHP
8.1/8.5 source environment, required Chromium and~the~aggregate.
Missing, failed, cancelled or~skipped mandatory owners block integration.

After preparation, the~selected feature workflow dispatches macOS packaging
as~an~independent advisory run on~the~same exact candidate and~base. It~executes
the~central package suite and~retains its~actual result and~diagnostics.
Runner queues, timeout, cancellation, failure or~dispatch refusal never block
integration. Separate concurrency and~check names protect required FULL.
Historical owner contracts 1--3 keep their original mandatory macOS proof;
authenticated immutable workflow bytes determine the~applicable contract.

Successful execution is~sealed before the~protected non-force fast-forward.
\codeword{qualify_only} retains qualification without writing develop.
Supply its~run ID as~\codeword{qualified_run} to~publish the~identical
candidate, complete input tree and~base without another FULL. Changed inputs
require new execution. The~publisher still checks actual owner/run identities,
source leases and~branch protections.

After successful integration and~record upload, the~workflow deletes
the~selected
remote feature branch with an~exact candidate-SHA lease. Newer commits prevent
deletion. \codeword{qualify_only} and~\codeword{recover_existing} retain
the~branch.
Cleanup failure leaves the~completed integration and~retained evidence intact.

For~an~explicit CI-only installation ahead of~a~release, base the~feature
on~main and~dispatch \ui{Basic source checks} with \codeword{install_ci}.
Complete FULL and~the~authoritative release audit produce a~bot PR;
one real human approval installs it~through normal protected auto-merge.

The~publisher binds the~same verified FULL to~both Checks and~a~standard
Actions commit status, because dispatch-suite Checks can~be~absent from
GitHub's branch-rule summary. It~rechecks the~status identity, state and~run
before each protected fast-forward.

After qualification and~protected installation, a~sole HTTP~422 rejection
for~the~new required matrix check permits up~to~six publication attempts with
two-second waits. Each retry revalidates the~active run, BASIC/FULL evidence,
original check IDs and~branch leases. Other failures stop; FULL is~not~replayed.

\subsection{Release assembly from qualified develop}

Registered Markdown version markers accept soft line breaks within the~same
paragraph. Preparation and~consistency checks preserve the~existing wrapping
and~historical version prose while updating the~registered value.
WinApp README, agent instructions and~tests alone do~not~trigger an~installer
rebuild; runtime and~build-input changes still do.
The~draft attachment reader uses the~same exclusions when reusing verified
published installer bytes. Recovery after tagging preserves Q, M and~the~tag.

In~GitHub Actions, run \ui{Begin Release} from~the~branch containing
the~intended workflow revision, normally \codeword{develop}, and~enter
the~version including \codeword{v_}, for~example \codeword{v_0.136.2}.
The~workflow separately reads current protected develop, freezes its~SHA,
creates \codeword{release/v_0.136.2} and~explicitly dispatches assembly
on~that release branch. Its~new workflow and~scripts execute before they
reach main. No~BASIC/FULL is~repeated. Initialization authenticates
the~original maintainer and~initiating run, attempt and~version;
the~bot identity alone grants no~authority. Retry can~reuse an~unmoved ref
and~avoids duplicate active assembly. If~the~release ref has~advanced,
continue its~existing assembly directly. PR approval and~publication remain
separate maintainer actions.

Before PR review, assembly retains the~frozen main history and~prepared tree.
After approval and~tagging, unchanged develop fast-forwards to~the~actual tagged
main commit. Both branch refs then match; no~content-copy commit is~created.
Its~status represents verified assembly and~qualified source ancestry;
functional FULL is~not~repeated. Parallel develop work is~preserved.
The~permanent receipt records the~actual main result, tree and~base.
Develop's history policy must admit reviewed main merges while retaining
non-force updates, required Actions checks and~no~bypass. The~legacy
linear-history requirement explicitly blocks readiness and~PR creation;
only the~maintainer changes installed policy. A~later release can~join existing
divergent histories without rewriting them. Historical content-copy receipts
remain readable; immediate ref realignment requires separate authorization.
The~next release trusts protected develop without historical reconciliation
archives. Only the~post-assembly observer handles approval, including approval
submitted early; successful origin Git push needs no~immediate REST readback.

A~commit admitted to~protected develop is~qualified. Create the~separately
authorized \codeword{release/v_X.Y[.Z]} branch from that source.
Release does~not rerun BASIC/FULL or~download older qualification archives.
It~freezes source A, updates metadata and~patch notes, aligns all~four manual
editions, compiles their PDFs with the~locked TinyTeX toolchain and~reproducible
timestamp, and~refreshes inventory and~the~complete-file manifest last.
Changed WinApp inputs require the~installer; unchanged inputs retain its
independent version and~verified existing assets.

New patch notes link every evidenced issue or~PR directly addressed
by~a~described
change in~that release. Complete commit metadata includes changed paths
for~each commit, supporting comparison with the~source diff and~evidence
digests.
Previous notes supply style only; historical examples and~routine release
bookkeeping establish no~current issue work. Release origin messages and~PR
wrappers add~no~fixed references. Actual new~work on~older issues remains
eligible.
Copilot may~return \codeword{NOTES_REVIEW_REQUIRED} when extraction,
consolidation or~grounded synthesis cannot finish reliably, including
an~unresolved material issue relationship. Validation then uses the~same
external
JSON handoff described below. Format checks do~not~prove semantic relevance.

Editorial notes preserve completed maintainer prose or~use the~existing
bounded Copilot generation from complete Git evidence. Automatic work admits
at~most 100000 diff bytes, 20 source files and~180000 prompt bytes before
provider setup. Larger changes, missing credentials, explicit external mode
or~Copilot/quota/invalid-output failures create \codeword{NOTES_PENDING}.
Download \codeword{release-notes-handoff} from~the~Actions run and~give
\filepath{prompt.txt} to~your chosen AI. The~30-day artifact includes the~full
template, commit evidence, source diff and~hash manifest. Review its~single-line
JSON response, then select the~same release branch in~\ui{Release qualification
> Run workflow}, paste it~into \codeword{external_notes} and~run.
The~bundle README provides CLI continuation when the~button is~not~yet
registered on~main. JSON escapes retain Markdown newlines. Repository, version,
branch, source SHA and~prompt hash must match freshly rebuilt evidence;
changed evidence needs a~new bundle. Existing notes remain authoritative.
Pending handoff uses no~waiting runner and~creates no~prepared commit,
qualification check, manuals build or~release PR. Continuation adds
no~BASIC/FULL.
Invalid output is~never applied. Build failures, missing attachments
and~stale generated hashes stop
assembly; they do~not introduce another source qualification stage.
Lifecycle schema 4 records the~qualified develop source and~assembly outcome.

The~existing stable tag remains immutable after CI-only main maintenance.
Initialization follows actual first-parent main history instead of~requiring
a~new tag or~a~version-specific exception. Git leases still protect concurrent
writers. Original release identities and~published assets stay unchanged.

\subsection{Review, version and~complete attachments}

The~workflow opens the~exact bot-authored PR to~main. Approve Q once.
Automation performs the~protected standard merge, verifies its~parents/tree,
creates the~immutable version tag, attempts safe develop synchronization
and~fills an~unpublished release draft with the~original ZIP, metadata,
checksums, all~four PDFs, notes and~permanent assembly/completion evidence.
It~adds missing verified assets and~preserves identical existing ones.

Wait for~\codeword{DRAFT_READY}, then manually choose \ui{Publish release}.
\codeword{TAGGED / DRAFT_PENDING} is~recoverable for~the~same Q;
do~not~rebuild the~candidate or~move a~correct tag. Parallel develop work
blocks unsafe synchronization, not~a~correct completed tag or~draft.
After \codeword{DRAFT_READY} and~retention of~completion evidence, the~final job
automatically removes the~exact release branch with a~Q SHA lease.
It~rechecks uploaded asset hashes, reviewed merge/tag, main/develop ancestry
and~active PR/workflow dependencies. Incomplete synchronization keeps
the~branch;
failed proof blocks cleanup while preserving the~draft. Retry only the~failed
cleanup job; immutable Q and~retained evidence support an~already deleted
branch.
This starts with the~first release containing the~change in~qualified develop.
Manual publication and~browser/hosting smoke tests remain separate. Historical
branches retain the~separately approved retirement workflow.
See \filepath{RELEASE.md}, \filepath{docs/RELEASE_LIFECYCLE.md},
\filepath{docs/CI_COVERAGE_REUSE_CONTRACT.md}
and~\filepath{docs/LATEX_BUILD.md}.

\section{Glossary}
\label{sec:glossary}
\index{glossary}

\begin{description}
  \item[AJAX] Browser request used to~update a~page region while preserving
  the~surrounding interface.
  \item[Branch scope] A~gallery and~its descendants, when the~selected feature
  defines recursive scope.
  \item[Canonical pair] Two image identifiers stored in~a~stable low-to-high
  order.
  \item[CSRF token] Session-bound value used to~validate intentional
  state-changing form submissions.
  \item[Derivative] Generated media representation such as~a~JPEG or~WebP
  thumbnail.
  \item[EXIF] Camera and~capture metadata embedded in~supported image files.
  \item[Integrity manifest] Versioned list of~core paths and~expected hashes.
  \item[Lightbox] Fullscreen or~overlay photo viewer with navigation and~related
  controls.
  \item[Media manifest] Request-local rendering data prepared from thumbnail
  metadata for~visible images.
  \item[NSFW guard] Age and~gallery/image policy controlling access
  to~restricted media.
  \item[Progressive sharpening] Small-first thumbnail pipeline that activates
  a~decoded larger candidate later.
  \item[Responsive selection] Browser-native candidate selection from
  an~immediately active source set.
  \item[Selected gallery] Gallery represented by~the~current public route.
  \item[Side panel] Admin right-side contextual interface loaded and~refreshed
  through fragment requests.
  \item[Thumbnail bounds] Gallery-aware minimum and~maximum generated sizes
  eligible for~selection.
\end{description}
